If the four will not co-exist, either pick three and say which, or move the camera OUTSIDE to the customer's view over the counter — which loses nothing the concept needs (the hands still cross the brass line, the transaction is still the subject) and gains the entire reference set as grounding instead of standing in the authored region.

b. RUN F14's KILL ON THAT SAME FRAME IN THE SAME SESSION. T1_ENDSHORT=1 already exists as the watched-failing switch on gal_end_a. Render the interior view with it on and off. If the two frames differ at the edges, the interior view is reading unlit body cavity and the true cost is interior geometry, not one camera. Report it as a finding either way — it also closes the register's oldest inherited row as a by-product, which is worth more than the print.

c. RETRACT THE GREP CLAIM IN THE SAME REVISION (rule 13) AND RE-COST WITHOUT THE CO-FUNDING. line_pass.py shipped at rev 77. Then state the model contribution at its true, smaller strength: a measured trim, a measured frame, one reference render. That claim is defensible. The current one is not.

d. RE-COST PRINTING AS THREE NUMBERS, NOT ONE. (i) Proof: 1 block, 1 ink, 30 pulls — $9 and one evening. (ii) Give-away run: 300 x 3 colours, ~900 pulls, 3 sessions over 4-5 days with overnight drying between colours, ~$60 stock + 15 h labour. (iii) Commissioned blocks: 4-6 days professional cutting, US$1,500-3,500, QUOTED not guessed. Say plainly that the 250 gsm rag edition needs a press, and either price the press or drop the edition.

e. PRINT THE GIVE-AWAY IN ONE INK. Three hand-registered blocks on 60 gsm newsprint is the single largest driver of both labour and failure. The key block alone is already what the first step makes and already carries the whole image. Reserve two and three colours for the signed 50, where the paper can take registration. This removes attack 5 almost entirely and costs the concept nothing it has argued for.

f. ASK THE ACCESS QUESTION BEFORE CUTTING, AS ONE MULTIPLE CHOICE WITH THE APERTURE CROP ATTACHED. (A) 30 prints on Nolita tables on a named Friday; (B) one sitting to draw a plate and a pair of hands in the kitchen; (C) both; (D) neither — in which case the hands come from a life model anywhere and the margin says so. The concept's entire honesty position turns on the answer and it costs one question. Do not cut a block before it is answered.

g. GIVE THE UNGATEABLE PART THE ONLY GATE AVAILABLE. Before any block is cut, draw the hands and the tray at 1:1 on paper, photograph them, composite them into the rendered frame, and put THAT to the owner as the multiple choice. It is the only check that exists for the one element no probe can score, and it is reversible where the block is not. Rule 1, applied to the part of this artefact that has never been subject to it.

h. DE-RISK THE LETTERING. 62 characters of reversed hand-cut text in a 258 mm margin is the highest-slip, lowest-load-bearing element on the sheet. Either cut it to one line — "SIN MEDIR · NO EXISTE GEOMETRÍA HUMANA EN EL MODELO" is the whole argument in eight words — or move the text to a separate fourth strip block, so a slipped letter costs a 40 x 258 mm offcut instead of the image.

DO (a), (c), (d), (e) and (f) and this becomes a 3: the cheapest concept in the bench to prove, honestly costed, with its one unmeasurable element gated by the owner's eye instead of by assertion.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**ORIGINALITY — 1.5/4 — ORIGINALITY: FAILS, and it fails first inside its own bench before it ever reaches the world. This is the #1-ranked concept's picture in a cheaper coat. PROVECHO (`dream:beautiful-2`, 8.73, rank 1 of the rev-77 bench) is already "the one picture this project has never made — two pairs of hands crossing the counter with a plate," and it is built from THE SAME FIGURE SET: aperture 515.5 × 403.0 r55, counter 1254/1147 = 107.0 mm slab, brass 18.6 % = 19.9 mm corroborated 0.191 on a second frame, fringe r9 at 26 mm pitch, strip-light emission (1.000, 0.918, 0.790), plus an identical closing confession — "UNMEASURED AND DRAWN ANYWAY: the hands, the plate, the food, and the galley's DEPTH." DE MANO EN MANO reproduces that list almost item for item. Even the trim conceit is pre-claimed: PROVECHO's tray is deliberately the aperture at 1:1 and says so ("it is the shape of the hole it does not fit through"); this is that move halved. And the cultural register is occupied too — RUIDO, in the same bench, is one-ink relief-family broadsides on cheap coloured stock, given away, explicitly "the Posada/TGP broadside's economics, not its iconography… No skull on any sheet, ever." That is this concept's guardrail paragraph, already written, by the same programme. A concept whose image, figures, confession, format logic and ethical disclaimer are each already held by two siblings is not fresh; it is a substrate variant. Externally it is worse: VW bus + relief print is arguably the single most saturated subject in amateur block printing on earth, and hands meeting over a counter (one out of dark, one out of light) has been the default artisanal-food image since roughly 2010. What genuinely survives is small but real: irreversibility, the visible record of a human hand, and misregistration kept — none of which litho tin can give. That is a process thesis, not a picture, and the concept currently spends it on a picture somebody else in the room is already making better.**

* ATTACKS: 1. INTERNAL DUPLICATION, TOP SEVERITY. `CONCEPT_BENCH_rev77.md` line 1221, PROVECHO, screen 8.73, rank 1: cook's hands out of the hatch, customer's hands rising at the bottom of frame, plate, brass strip, bobble fringe ball after ball, strip light down the galley wall. That is this concept's image, at a higher score, in a substrate that outlives three refits. Both pitch it with the same sentence-shape ("the one image/picture this project has never made"). Two artefacts, one picture — and PROVECHO gets the better half of the trade, because a tin tray carries food and a newsprint sheet carries a copy of a tin tray's idea.

2. THE TRIM CONCEIT IS ALSO PRE-CLAIMED, AND AT A BETTER SCALE. PROVECHO's fourth stated risk is that the tray IS the aperture at nominal size and therefore cannot pass through it — "a small, deliberate lie about its own dimension," dimension printed on the rim. "258 × 202 is aperture No. 2 at 1:2" is that idea at half strength: a 1:1 object that physically fails to fit the hole is a joke with a body; a 1:2 sheet is a proportion note.

3. THE CULTURAL REGISTER AND ITS DISCLAIMER ARE OCCUPIED IN THE SAME DOCUMENT. RUIDO (line ~2022): "the Posada/TGP broadside's economics, not its iconography," one ink, coloured uncoated stock, given away or a few dollars, "no calavera… No skull on any sheet, ever." DE MANO EN MANO's risk (4) is that paragraph rewritten. Two concepts reaching for the same lineage and the same defence in one bench means the defence has become boilerplate — which is exactly what a living tradition's heirs would say about it.

4. EXTERNAL SATURATION OF THE SUBJECT. The VW T1 in relief print is a craft-fair perennial: block prints of combis fill Etsy, surf-town print shops and Mexican tourist markets, and "combi + tacos + linocut" is a template, not a discovery. Tacombi's own existing identity already leans on hand-drawn folk illustration of a combi (Vista Hermosa packaging included), so within its own market this reads as brand-adjacent stock rather than a new object.

5. EXTERNAL SATURATION OF THE IMAGE. Two hands meeting over a counter, one emerging from darkness into light, is the default composition of craft-food branding for fifteen years and is Caravaggist before that. Calling it "the transaction the restaurant actually performs" is true and also what every coffee, bakery and taqueria campaign has said while shooting the same frame.

6. THE ECONOMICS CLAIM HAS A NAMED OWNER. "One block, cheap stock, given away" is Amos Kennedy Jr.'s entire practice, and the hand-pulled-cheap-stock-for-a-business lineage runs through Hatch Show Print. In Mexican-American relief specifically, Artemio Rodríguez's La Mano Press has been printing exactly this — daily life, relief, cheap, in the Posada line — for decades. Presenting the economics as the honest borrow does not make the borrow original.

7. THE TITLE IS A STOCK IDIOM AND COLLIDES LOCALLY. "De mano en mano" / "mano a mano" is used by NGOs, fair-trade brands, songs and TV. Live collisions: Mano a Mano: Mexican Culture Without Borders — a nonprofit in NEW YORK CITY, the brand's own city; Mano en Mano in Maine; and La Mano Press in the exact craft register this concept enters. A giveaway print handed out in Nolita under that title will be read as an affiliation.

8. THE ONE FRESH TECHNICAL CLAIM IS BOTH OLD AND SLIGHTLY WRONG. "Relief removes the light, so the occlusion pass is a CUTTING MAP" is a metaphor wearing an identity's clothes. A lino block's cleared area is compositional mass — what the carver decides prints white — not what the renderer says is lit; threshold an AO pass and you get mezzotint mush, not the line-and-mass grammar that makes a linocut read. As process it is also standard studio practice (print the inverted image on the block, carve it) and, computationally, ~25-year-old NPR territory: Ostromoukhov's digital facial engraving (SIGGRAPH 1999), Mizuno's virtual woodblock printing, Winkenbach & Salesin's pen-and-ink illustration. Worse for this concept, EL TROQUEL in the same bench makes the mathematically TRUE version of the move — a Z pass is single-valued, a bas-relief is single-valued, no translation step — so this competes with a sibling on strictly weaker ground.

9. IT IS THE HOUSE AESTHETIC RE-ENTERING THROUGH THE CAPTION. The register escapes flat measured line — polychrome, hand-cut, irregular, people — and then the margin key drags it straight back: MANOS Y COMIDA — SIN MEDIR, plus an itemised measured/unmeasured schedule. That is THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE's grammar transposed to lino, and the confession-as-punchline is now the programme's fourth-in-a-row tic. PROVECHO explicitly DROPS it and names the cost ("it abandons the programme's only unique asset… no ceiling, no tolerance, no confession") — which makes this the timid version of the same idea, hedging in cut letters because it does not trust the picture.

10. THE EMOTION IS ASSERTED IN TEXT, NOT CARRIED BY THE IMAGE. The strongest line in the pitch — forty revisions built the counter, the plancha, the hooks and the tools and never put anybody behind them — is a sentence, and it is a sentence PROVECHO also delivers, without needing it printed on the object. If the picture only lands once you have read the margin, it is a diagram of a feeling.

11. FRESHNESS RESIDUE, MEASURED HONESTLY: the irreversibility (a wrong gouge kills the block), the kept misregistration, and the fact that a human hand's wobble is the one mark in this programme no pass can produce. That is genuinely unoccupied — by PROVECHO, by RUIDO, by the world's combi linocuts. It is currently a footnote under risk (2), not the subject.
* WOULD FIX IT: 1. CEDE THE HAND-OFF TO PROVECHO. Do not make one picture twice. PROVECHO holds the transaction better (it carries food, it survives service, it ranks first) and it holds it without the confession. Paying an illustrator and a carver for the same image in two substrates is what a programme does when it has run out of pictures, and the owner will see the repeat before he sees either object.

2. RE-POINT THE SUBJECT AT SOMETHING ONLY AN IRREVERSIBLE MEDIUM CAN SAY, AND THE CANDIDATE IS ALREADY IN THE RECORD: THE OFF SIDE. `SPEC.md` grades that elevation "E (never photographed)" — an entire side of the vehicle no frame in the reference set shows. Cut it by hand, at 1:2, on the same 258 × 202 trim. A surface nobody can check, committed in a medium where nothing can be taken back, is a thesis no tin tray, no poster and no line pass can make: the print IS the act of deciding what the record could not recover. Nothing in the 78, and nothing in the world's combi block prints, occupies that. Keep the margin key here — on this subject the confession is the picture, not a hedge on it.

3. IF THE HAND-OFF STAYS ANYWAY, DELETE THE MARGIN KEY ENTIRELY. Silent print, no cut letters, no schedule. Let the invention be answered by process instead of by text: the hands drawn from the actual cook, from life, in one sitting, in front of him, and the drawing kept whatever it comes out as. Then the honesty is in how it was made, which is the only honesty a relief block can hold, and it stops being the timid twin of concept #1.

4. RENAME IT. "De mano en mano" is a stock idiom colliding with Mano a Mano (NYC — the brand's own city), Mano en Mano (Maine), and La Mano Press in this exact craft register. Take a name off the vehicle or off the number: the aperture's own dimension, the brass fraction, the ink. Anything that could not be a nonprofit's letterhead.

5. FIX OR DEMOTE THE AO CLAIM. State it as what it is — a value study to compose over, not a cutting map — and cite the practice rather than announcing it: studio transfer-and-carve is routine, and computational engraving from 3D is Ostromoukhov 1999 onward. Better: drop the claim and let EL TROQUEL own the depth-pass identity, which is actually an identity. Building the AO/occlusion pass is still worth doing as shared infrastructure with the line keystone — that argument survives intact and should be the only pipeline claim made here.

6. COMMISSION BEFORE YOU DESIGN. In this lineage the difference between homage and extraction is a name on the block. Approach a Mexican or Mexican-American relief printmaker first, credit and pay them, and let the concept be co-authored rather than defended. "Economics not iconography" is now a disclaimer two concepts in this bench share; a signature is not.

7. RUN THE HEAD-TO-HEAD THIS WEEK AND LET ONE DIE. Same A4 hand-off image, one painted for PROVECHO, one cut and pulled for this, both handed to the owner with nothing written on them and no explanation. Thirty sheets on the tables Friday is a good test and it should be run — but run it against the sibling, not against nothing. If the painted one wins, this concept's honest future is the off-side block, not the counter.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**OWNER-AND-BRAND — 2.5/4 — CONDITIONAL — the object is one he could be proud of; the GIVE-AWAY as specified is one he would wince at. Its brand problems are not in the image, they are in the margin text, the lighting of the cook, the missing wordmark, and a first step that cuts an invented taco into a block before anyone has drawn his actual plate. All are fixable this week and none requires giving up the concept's risk.**

* ATTACKS: WHAT SURVIVES FIRST, SO THE ATTACK IS FAIR. It is the only thing on the rev-77 shortlist that contains the transaction the restaurant performs; it dodges F191/F193 and the VW trademark BY CONSTRUCTION (interior view — no roundel, no hubcap, no nose in frame, and that is a geometric fact, not a promise); the 258 x 202 trim being serving aperture No. 2 at 1:2 is the best-earned formal move in the round; and its cultural handling (commission a Mexican printmaker, credit and pay, no skull, homage to the ECONOMICS not the iconography) is the most careful in the bench. Now the attack.

1. THE MARGIN TEXT IS THE PROJECT TALKING TO ITSELF, IN SPANISH, ON A SHEET HANDED TO A PAYING CUSTOMER. "MANOS Y COMIDA — SIN MEDIR. NO EXISTE GEOMETRIA HUMANA EN EL MODELO." The diner does not know there is a model. To the audience actually receiving it that sentence is either noise or — read literally — the restaurant's own giveaway confessing that its picture contains no people and no food, printed directly under a picture of people and food. He said "we need to finally present something." This presents the making-of. The confession that makes the object honest to US is an internal artefact; it belongs on the colophon of the signed 50, not in the hands of table six. Single highest-probability wince.

2. IT COLLIDES WITH A RULING ONE REVISION OLD. F331, rev 77: the audience is split deliberately, and the option he chose was stated to him as "the free/handed-to-you things are for children, the paid/kept things are for adults; NEITHER PRETENDS TO BE THE OTHER," with the standing instruction that every future shortlist must say which line each item is in. This artefact is one image issued across both lines — unlimited free newsprint AND a signed rag edition of 50 — and its register (TGP homage, engraved measurement key, Spanish process epigraph, three measured inks) is adult in both editions. Handing an adult conceptual print to a family with the check is exactly the pretending the ruling forbids, and the concept never names its line.

3. THE TACO IS THE EMBLEM AGAIN, AND THE FIRST STEP WALKS STRAIGHT INTO IT. He has reported the roundel wrong NINE times because it was not exactly the object. He is a restaurateur; the one thing on earth he is the world authority on is what his plate looks like. This concept puts an INVENTED tray of food at the optical centre — and then FIRST STEP says cut the key block this week over an inverted render and put thirty prints on tables Friday. There is no food in any render; there is not one taco in 229 objects. So first contact is a hand-carved, IRREVERSIBLE block containing a made-up taco. Risk (5) states the problem ("drawn from THIS restaurant's actual plate, in front of the cook, or it is a stock taco") and the schedule violates it. That is the emblem's failure sequence with the medium's irreversibility bolted on: he will look at the food first, exactly as he looks at the roundel first.

4. THE COOK IS IN THE DARK AND THE CUSTOMER IS IN THE LIGHT. "the cook's out of the dark, the customer's out of the light." As interior lighting that is honest. As brand imagery for a Mexican-founded taqueria whose staff ARE the brand, it is a faceless worker emerging from blackness to serve an illuminated customer — drawn in the one graphic lineage, Posada / Taller de Gráfica Popular, whose entire subject is the visibility and dignity of the worker. The homage argues against the image. Nobody intends that reading; it will still be the first thing a critic writes, and it is one gouge away from fixed.

5. NOTHING ON THE SHEET SAYS WHOSE RESTAURANT IT IS. Three inks measured off the vehicle and not one brand identifier: no wordmark, no exterior, no address, no name. As merchandise it markets nothing; as a brand asset it is dilution by association without attribution — a beautiful anonymous print of two hands that could be any taqueria on earth. And the obvious fix has a trap: the one mark the model holds is the "Senor Tacombi" script, and SPEC 10.10's own scope table grades that element "REJECTED BY DONALD". Do NOT cut the model's reconstruction; that is printing a rejected artefact with his own name on it.

6. BRAND RED, HAND-INKED, ON 60 GSM NEWSPRINT, 300 AT A TIME. Body red (196,49,36) is the closest thing this project has to a brand colour. Oil ink hand-pulled on warm newsprint will drop its value, shift it warm, mottle coverage, and — by design — misregister a millimetre or two. Every one of those is a defensible art decision and a bad brand-consistency decision when 300 leave the building in one service and no two match.

7. THE SUPPLY IS NOT A SUPPLY. "Unlimited" and "hand-printed" cannot both be true at restaurant volume. 300 sheets is an afternoon of a printmaker's labour and one dinner service of covers. Either staff ration it at the check — friction at the exact moment the concept is about — or it becomes a reprint, at which point the blocks are SOURCE ARTWORK, not the artefact. That is fine, and he should be told it up front rather than discovering it in week three.

8. A STATED-AS-GREPPED FIGURE IS ALREADY WRONG. "I GREPPED EVERY .py IN THIS TREE: THERE IS NO FREESTYLE, NO GREASE PENCIL, NO AO PASS, NO CRYPTOMATTE." `/home/user/combi_render/line_pass.py` is in the tree and IS Grease Pencil Line Art (F332, shipped rev 77). The AO / normal / cryptomatte half still stands; the Grease Pencil half does not. Consequence for the pitch: the "it pays for shared infrastructure too" argument is worth half what it claims, because half is already banked. Consequence for the audit: a figure published without being watched print, in a document that lectures on exactly that.

9. SPEC 10.10, SCOPED HONESTLY. The rule-34 defence holds — 10.10's object is THE MODEL, and a derivative hand-cut print replicates nothing and asserts nothing about the vehicle. But note what sits next to that bar: a project that would not invent a 2 mm bay taper is inventing two hands, a tray, food and the galley's depth. The concept says so and calls it the point, and I accept that. What I do not accept is inventing them into an irreversible medium BEFORE the one person who can adjudicate the food has seen a drawing of it (see 3).
* WOULD FIX IT: SIX CONCRETE CHANGES, five of them free.

(1) STRIP THE CONFESSION OFF THE GIVE-AWAY. Cut into the key block only what a diner can use: the shop name and DE MANO EN MANO. Move "MANOS Y COMIDA — SIN MEDIR / NO EXISTE GEOMETRIA HUMANA EN EL MODELO" and the item-by-item measured/unmeasured key to the colophon of the signed 50 on rag, where the audience is the one that wants it. The honesty survives on the object that is kept and read; the give-away stops apologising to people who never asked.

(2) DECLARE THE LINE, PER F331. Say it in the pitch: signed rag 50 = ADULT/KEPT line; newsprint = the FREE line, and therefore it must be legible to a child across a table — bigger hands, bigger tray, fewer words, food readable as food at arm's length. If you will not simplify the give-away, drop it and pitch this as an adult-line object only. Either is defensible; straddling is what the ruling forbids.

(3) INVERT THE FIRST STEP SO THE INVENTION IS REFERENCED BEFORE IT IS IRREVERSIBLE. This week: go to the pass, draw the actual plate and the actual hand-off from life, ten drawings, in front of the cook; photograph the tray crossing the brass line. Put ONE sheet to him as multiple choice — "which of these three is your taco" — with the reference photograph attached, per the project's own asking protocol. THEN cut. The lino, the gouges and the Friday table test all survive, moved one week right, and the block that gets cut contains his food instead of a stock taco. This is the difference between the concept and a tenth emblem rejection.

(4) LIGHT THE COOK. In relief you cut away the light — so give the cook the cut. Make the cook's hand and forearm the white of the paper, lit by the galley strip you already measured 20 mm under the head rail (which lights the galley side — the current scheme is not even the truer lighting), and put the customer's hand in key ink against the daylight outside. Costs nothing, is more accurate to the fixture you measured, and reverses a reading you cannot control once it is printed.

(5) GET THE REAL WORDMARK FROM HIM AND SET IT — DO NOT CUT THE MODEL'S SCRIPT. Ask for the brand's own artwork file; letterpress or overprint it small in the lower margin, or put it alone on the reverse. Explicitly do NOT use the model's "Senor Tacombi" reconstruction: SPEC 10.10's table records that element as REJECTED BY DONALD.

(6) SPLIT THE MEDIUM FROM THE SUPPLY, AND SAY SO IN THE PITCH. The three hand-cut blocks and their hand-pulled edition are THE OBJECT. The give-away is a riso or offset reprint from scans of the three blocks, printed off-register on purpose so the misregistration survives at 3000 sheets and at a cost per sheet a restaurant can carry. Price both in the ask. That also kills the brand-red variance: pull the red properly on rag, and let the hand-pulled give-away trial be key-block only — one block, one ink, which is the cheapest possible proof and is exactly the TGP economics the concept says it honours.

AND ONE CORRECTION BEFORE IT IS SHOWN TO ANYBODY: delete the "no Grease Pencil" claim — `/home/user/combi_render/line_pass.py` exists (F332). Say what is still true and is a stronger argument anyway: there is no AO, no normal pass and no cryptomatte, the carving map needs the AO pass, and so does the sticker (F18), which is rank 1 in the rev-77 brief.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**DANGER — 3/4 — DANGER LENS — VERDICT: THE CHARGE SURVIVES, BUT THE CONCEPT HAS ALREADY DISARMED ITS MOST POLITICAL COMPONENT AND AIMED ITS CONFESSION AT THE SAFEST AVAILABLE TARGET.

WHAT IS GENUINELY DANGEROUS HERE, AND STILL PRESENT:

1. THE CLASS ASYMMETRY IS STAGED IN THE IMAGE ITSELF. "the cook's out of the dark, the customer's out of the light." That single clause is the most loaded thing produced anywhere in the 78-title bench. It is not a warning label — it is compositional, it is in the geometry (interior view, strip light above the cook, daylight through the aperture), and it cannot be removed without removing the picture. INTACT.

2. THE DISTRIBUTION IS THE CONTENT. The customer is handed an image of the transaction they are at that instant completing, from the SERVER'S side of the counter, at the moment they pay. That is genuinely uncomfortable and it is not decorative. INTACT — but the concept treats it as logistics ("handed over with the check"), not as the subject.

3. THE CONFESSION IN CUT LETTERS. I verified it: nothing human or edible exists in the mesh. `t1_detail.py` builds `handles()`, `englid_handle()`, `plate_1963()` — a door handle, a lid handle, a NUMBER PLATE. 229 objects, 366,490 faces, and the closest thing to a person is a latch. Forty-plus revisions built the counter, the plancha, `gal_tool`, `gal_warmer_tap`, and never once put anybody behind them. That is a real and mournful fact and the sheet says it in Spanish, backwards, permanently. INTACT.

4. THE INVENTION IS A GENUINE TRANSGRESSION IN THIS PROJECT'S OWN TERMS. A tree that refuses to publish a tread count, refuses a 2 mm bay taper, and printed "it cannot be recovered from what we hold" as a result, is about to hand-carve two human hands. That has teeth because the doctrine is real. INTACT.

5. IRREVERSIBILITY. A wrong gouge kills the block; the misregistration is kept. Small, but honest and unsanded.

WHERE IT HAS BEEN SANDED SMOOTH — AND THIS IS THE FAILURE MODE I AM HERE TO CATCH:

(a) "NO SKULL." This is exactly the plant-through-the-wheel-arch becoming a tended herb in a chipped tin. The concept correctly identifies the Posada / Taller de Gráfica Popular lineage, correctly identifies that this is where the discomfort lives, and then PRE-EMPTIVELY REMOVES IT and keeps the warning label ("CULTURAL. This sits in a real lineage with living heirs."). Nothing is put in the skull's place. What remains is the lineage's TEXTURE — cheap stock, one block, gouge marks — with its politics extracted.

(b) "AN HOMAGE TO THE ECONOMICS — ONE BLOCK, CHEAP STOCK, GIVEN AWAY." This sentence is the piece's escape hatch and it does not hold. TGP gave prints away because commerce was the adversary. Reproducing that form INSIDE a paid transaction, as a hospitality gift attached to the check, is not an homage to those economics — it is their inversion, and it is precisely the appropriation the paragraph claims to be avoiding. The concept gets halfway to saying this and stops.

(c) THE CONFESSION IS AIMED AT THE SOFTWARE. "NO EXISTE GEOMETRÍA HUMANA EN EL MODELO" indicts the 3D asset — a safe, self-deprecating engineering joke that costs the restaurant nothing. The version that costs something is the same sentence pointed at the business: the labour was never measured either. The concept picked the softer of the two available confessions.

(d) THE HANDS ARE UNSPECIFIED, THEREFORE STOCK. The concept is rigorous about the food ("drawn from THIS restaurant's actual plate, in front of the cook") and says NOTHING about whose hands these are. Two anonymous hands make the light/dark asymmetry decorative. A generic hand is a clip-art hand and the whole confession collapses into a gesture.

(e) "WATCH WHETHER ANYBODY TAKES ONE HOME." The first-step test is merch uptake. That single sentence reframes the entire piece as a marketing pilot and is the most sanding-prone line in the document.

ONE FACTUAL CORRECTION THAT BEARS ON CREDIBILITY (not my lens, but load-bearing): "I GREPPED EVERY .py IN THIS TREE: THERE IS NO FREESTYLE, NO GREASE PENCIL, NO AO PASS" is now FALSE — `line_pass.py` exists (commits `6d112e5`, `55e79cd`), and its own header records that the rev-76 programme's version of that grep was VOID (a basic-regex `grep` where `|` is a literal pipe — rule 50, caught by a rule-15 adversary). The AO/occlusion cutting map genuinely still does not exist; the line pass does. Restate it accordingly or the concept ships a refuted citation.

WHY IT IS NOT SCORED HIGHER: the most dangerous single decision available to this piece — inheriting a workers' print tradition and pointing it at the labour inside the restaurant that commissioned it — was identified, named, and then declined, in the same paragraph, with a label left behind to mark the spot.**

* ATTACKS: ATTACK 1 — "NO SKULL" IS THE TELL. You located the discomfort (a living political tradition, heirs, iconography), removed the part that carries it, and kept the sentence announcing that you thought about it. The skull is not the point — the point is that you deleted the tradition's teeth and substituted nothing. What is left is TGP as a texture: gouge marks, newsprint, three flat inks. That is style-lifting with a disclaimer.

ATTACK 2 — THE GIVEAWAY IS THE APPROPRIATION, NOT THE ESCAPE FROM IT. "Homage to the economics — one block, cheap stock, given away" reads as absolution. TGP's free print was anti-commercial by necessity. Yours is a brand gift stapled to a bill. You have taken the FORM of a workers' print (cheap, unlimited, handed over) and inserted it into the exact moment of commercial extraction. If you are going to do that — and you should, it is the interesting move — SAY THAT, on the sheet, in cut letters. Do not launder it as homage.

ATTACK 3 — TWO ANONYMOUS HANDS IS A STOCK IMAGE. You are forensic about the plate ("in front of the cook") and silent about the hands. Unspecified, they will be drawn as Hands: clean, generic, symmetrical, and the "dark/light" contrast becomes a lighting effect rather than a class fact. Every printmaker's instinct will smooth them, because a hand is hard and a generic hand is easy.

ATTACK 4 — YOU CONFESSED TO THE RENDERER, NOT TO THE RESTAURANT. "NO EXISTE GEOMETRÍA HUMANA EN EL MODELO" is a joke about Blender. It is true, it is verified (I checked: the nearest thing to a person in 366,490 faces is `handles()`), and it costs nobody anything. It lets the sheet be self-aware without being accusatory. The owner will laugh. Nobody will be uncomfortable.

ATTACK 5 — THE FIRST STEP IS A MERCH TEST. "Put them on the tables Friday with the check and watch whether anybody takes one home." You have written a conversion metric. The moment success is defined as uptake, every subsequent decision sands: the hands get prettier, the burn scars go, the Spanish line gets shorter, the newsprint gets nicer. This sentence is how the piece dies in month two.

ATTACK 6 — THE ONE PHYSICALLY CHARGED IMAGE IN THE PROGRAMME IS DESCRIBED IN ELEVEN WORDS. Two hands, food, contact across a brass line, one from darkness and one from daylight. That is the most intimate image anywhere in 78 titles and it gets one clause, while the fringe-ball pitch gets a figure to one decimal place. The measured box is fully specified and the human event inside it is a stub. That imbalance is itself the sanding: you retreated into the part you could cite.
* WOULD FIX IT: PUT BACK, CONCRETELY:

1. RESTORE THE BODY IN PLACE OF THE SKULL. You do not need Posada's calavera; you need something that costs the same. Specify both hands as PORTRAITS, not hands: the cook's forearm and hand drawn FROM LIFE with what the plancha has done to it — the scald line across the back of the wrist, the split nail bed, the blue catering plaster, the burn scars a cook accumulates in a year at 1455 mm. The customer's hand: clean, ringed, a watch, a phone tan. Cut the difference. Two hands that are merely dark and light are lighting; two hands that are scarred and unscarred are the subject. And name the cook — his name cut into the block beside his own hand, backwards, in his own spelling.

2. MAKE THEM TOUCH, OR ALMOST. Fix the geometry of the exchange: the tray crosses the 19.9 mm brass line and the two hands are within a finger's width over it. Contact across the counter is the whole piece. Decide it and state it; do not leave it to the carver.

3. RE-AIM THE MARGIN TEXT AT THE BUSINESS, NOT THE SOFTWARE. Keep MANOS Y COMIDA — SIN MEDIR. Add the line that costs something, and it is a verified figure you may print: 229 OBJETOS · 366,490 CARAS · 0 PERSONAS. Seventy-six revisions measured the arch-to-tyre gap to 39.7 mm and nobody's hands to anything. That sentence, in cut letters, on a print given away inside the restaurant, is an indictment rather than a joke — and it is TRUE, which is the only reason it is allowed.

4. PRICE THE DEBT IN MONEY AND OWNERSHIP, NOT IN CREDIT. Delete "commission and credit" and "homage to the economics." Replace with terms: the commissioned printmaker's name is CUT INTO the key block (not printed in a colophon), the printmaker KEEPS the three blocks, and the give-away edition is theirs to reprint and sell independently in perpetuity. The signed 50 on rag splits to the printmaker. If the answer to those terms is no, the piece should not be made — that is the test of whether it is homage or texture-lifting, and it is a cheap test to run this week.

5. NAME THE INVERSION ON THE SHEET. One cut line acknowledging that a free print made in the tradition of prints given away against commerce is here being given away WITH THE BILL. Put it in the lower margin next to SIN MEDIR. It is one line, it is uncomfortable, it makes the piece honest instead of tasteful, and it is the difference between citing a tradition and wearing it.

6. CHANGE THE RITUAL SO THE ASYMMETRY IS PERFORMED, NOT JUST DEPICTED. The print is handed over BY THE COOK WHOSE HAND IS IN THE DARK — he comes out from behind the pass and gives it to the customer himself. Not with the check, not by the server, not from a stack by the door. If it arrives anonymously on a bill tray it is merch, and the image's whole claim about who is visible is contradicted by its own delivery.

7. DELETE THE UPTAKE TEST. Strike "watch whether anybody takes one home." Replace the first-week question with: will the cook whose hand it is put his name on it? If he will not, the piece is wrong and you have learned that for US$9 of lino.

8. FIX THE VOID CITATION. Drop "I GREPPED EVERY .py IN THIS TREE: THERE IS NO GREASE PENCIL" — `line_pass.py` exists at rev 77, and `line_pass.py`'s own header records that the rev-76 form of that grep was a basic-regex search for one impossible string (rule 50). The honest and still-sufficient claim: the LINE pass now exists and is shared infrastructure you can build on; the AO/occlusion CUTTING MAP does not, and until it does the carver works from a value-inverted 96-spp render at the cost of one render.

WHAT NOT TO TOUCH: the measured box (aperture at 1:2, 107 mm slab, 18.6 % brass = 19.9 mm, 9 mm balls at 26 mm pitch, strip light 20 mm under the head rail — I confirmed CNT_NOSE_F = 0.1860 and FRINGE_R/PITCH/INSET = 0.0090/0.0260/0.0130 in `t1_detail.py`); the kept misregistration; the refusal to use the constructed (72,46,6); and the interior view that keeps the emblem out of frame by construction. Those are all correct and none of them is where the danger lives.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

### DIRECTO — audit mean 2.25/4

**FEASIBILITY — 2/4 — FEASIBILITY AUDIT — DIRECTO. Score 2/4. It is genuinely makeable and it is the most machine-unblocked concept on the bench, but its enabling gate is unverified, its execution plan contains a contradiction that makes the stated first step physically impossible, and its one derived figure disagrees with the project's own machine.

WHAT IS STRONG, AND I WANT IT ON THE RECORD FIRST.
This concept costs the machine NOTHING. It needs no line pass, no Grease Pencil route, no glTF export, no fix to the clipped front camera (ortho 3.55 -> 2.4406 m against a 3.132 m bbox), no deterministic render, no 5-min-a-frame queue, no emblem repair, and it explicitly declares no roundel so F191/F193 are untouched. Every other concept on the bench waits on infrastructure; this one waits on a painter. On a 4-core box with an impatient owner that is a real and rare advantage. It has no factory MOQ, needs no photographer, and needs no vehicle you cannot reach for FABRICATION (only for validation — see below). Its risk register is the most honest I have audited.

FAILURE 1 — THE ENABLING GATE IS UNVERIFIED, AND THE EVIDENCE FOR IT IS TWELVE YEARS OLD.
"It sits in a room on Elizabeth Street with an empty windscreen" is asserted, not measured. I read the EXIF this session: `ref_nolita_front34.jpg` is DateTimeOriginal 2014:04:05, SONY DSC-RX100, 700x467. `IMG_3840.jpeg` — the frame the address is to be read off — is 2010:10:04, Canon 5D II, 480x320. Today is 2026-09-05. So the newest evidence in this repository that the bus is in that room, whole, red, and with a clear screen is TWELVE YEARS OLD, and the address frame is SIXTEEN. I opened `ref_nolita_front34.jpg`: the bus is whole, both windscreen panes clear and empty, indoors in an active food-service room with staff and customers — the premise is corroborated FOR 2014 and for no later date. Nothing in this tree establishes the vehicle is still there.
Worse, the record says the owner cannot check it casually: "I have never stood in the bus" appears in a dozen briefs (rev 17, 22, 23, 24, 25, 26, 33, 34, 35, 39, 42, 56) and CLAUDE.md turns it into a standing method rule. So the concept's zero-cost validation step depends on physical access that this project has stated, repeatedly, for fifty-nine revisions, that it does not have. That is not fatal — it is one email to an operator — but it is UNASKED, and both the artefact (a) and the first step die if the answer is no.

FAILURE 2 — THE FIRST STEP CANNOT BE EXECUTED AS WRITTEN. TWO HARD BLOCKS.
(a) "print it 900 x 300 ... and stand it in the windscreen." The 900 x 300 is the WALL board. The windscreen pane is 585 wide. A 900 mm board does not go into a 585 mm aperture. The step tests the wrong object in the wrong place and physically cannot be performed.
(b) "one A1 sheet of paper" — A1 is 841 x 594. A 900 mm print does not fit on A1 at full size. You need A0 (1189 x 841) or a roll. The stated materials cannot make the stated artefact.
This is a $40 afternoon that currently cannot happen.

FAILURE 3 — THE ONE DERIVED FIGURE IS WRONG, AND THE MACHINE PRINTS THE RIGHT ONE.
The concept publishes "the pair across both panes plus divider = 1196.0 mm." I checked it against source and against `audit.py`. `t1_shell.py` places pane centres at `yc = s * (WS_DIV + WS_PANE_W / 2)`, so WS_DIV is a HALF-offset from the centreline and the FULL divider is 52.0 mm, not 26.0. `audit.py` says so in its own print statements — `(2 panes, divider %.3f) % (S.WS_DIV * 2)` and `total glass width = 2 * S.WS_PANE_W + 2 * S.WS_DIV`. That evaluates to 1222.0 mm. The concept computed 2W + D = 1196.0. It read the constant without reading its use. This is the project's own rule 5 firing on a concept document: a figure published without watching it print. It is not load-bearing for the artefact (the board sits in ONE pane), but it is the only derived number the concept offers and it is 26 mm wrong.

FAILURE 4 — THE MODEL CONTRIBUTION IS ZERO, NOT ONE, AND THE CONCEPT'S OWN MITIGATION PROVES IT.
The concept honestly says `WS_PANE_W, WS_PANE_H, WS_DIV, WS_R = 0.5850, 0.3770, 0.0260, 0.050` carries NO provenance comment — I confirmed this at `t1_shell.py`, the line sits bare under the WINDSCREEN banner with nothing beside it. It then says "Verify it against the actual bus before anyone cuts steel." Both are right, and together they dissolve the dependency: if a tape measure on the real screen must precede the cut, the model contributed nothing binding. The answer to "what were seventy-six revisions for" is not "the width of a windscreen" — it is zero. The concept braces for the owner finding one number annoying; he will find NO number worse.

FAILURE 5 — FABRICATION REALISM GAPS, SMALL AND CHEAP BUT UNADDRESSED IN AN OBJECT WHOSE ENTIRE VALUE IS AUTHENTICITY.
- MASS AND MOUNTING: 585 x 377 x 1.2 mm steel = 2.08 kg; the wall board = 2.54 kg. The concept specifies no mounting for either. `ref_nolita_front34.jpg` has a TODDLER standing at the front bumper. A 2 kg unsecured steel plate propped against glass in a public dining room is a liability item, not a detail.
- OIL-CANNING: a flat 1.2 mm panel spanning 900 mm with no return edge will visibly bow. Real rótulo lámina is hemmed or framed. Fix is a 10 mm fold all round; omitted, it reads as a school project rather than a sign.
- THE 50 mm RADIUS IS INHERITED FROM THE WRONG OBJECT. `WS_R = 0.050` is the APERTURE's corner. Copied to a 377 mm-tall placard it is 13% of the height — very rounded, and not the pesero idiom. That is rule 34's shape: a figure carrying its object with it.
- THE RAKE IS NEVER MENTIONED. `P_TOP (1.8340, 1.7745)` to `P_BOT (2.0400, 1.3755)` gives a screen raked 27.3 deg from vertical. The 377 is IN-PLANE; seen from directly in front the board's vertical rise is 335.0 mm. Lettering set flat and stood in that screen foreshortens ~11% vertically before you add the customer's own eye height. Sign painters compensate; this brief does not tell them to.
- FIT: a board cut at exactly 585.0 has zero clearance. The model's own frame runs `WS_PANE_W - 0.0055` inner, so ~579.5 mm is visible at best, and a board sized to the aperture cannot be raked without fouling the frame.

COST — THE BEST-COSTED CONCEPT I HAVE AUDITED, AND STILL OPTIMISTIC BY ~1.5-2x.
US$150-400/board is a shop rate for a plain rótulo. This brief specifies four to six colours, a painted food vignette, a signature and a credit — that is an ARTIST COMMISSION, which the concept itself argues it must be. Realistic US$300-800/board, the windscreen board with the vignette at the top. Steel, laser-cut radius, deburr, primer: cheap in MX. What is entirely absent from the budget is INTERNATIONAL SHIPPING AND CUSTOMS: five rigid boards, ~13 kg plus crate, MX->NY, US$250-500 freight, plus a broker call on whether hand-painted signs on steel classify as artwork or as articles of iron/steel with Section 232 tariff exposure. Landed realistic: US$3,000-4,500 against a stated ceiling of US$2,500. That is honest-but-optimistic, not fantasy. Timeline 2-4 weeks is likewise ~2x light: painter scheduling plus international freight plus customs is realistically 5-9 weeks.

CAPABILITY THE TEAM HAS NOT DEMONSTRATED. The delivery path is Spanish-language artist sourcing, a commission-and-credit agreement, payment across a border, and a customs entry. None of it is hard. None of it is AI-accelerable. All of it is new to this project, whose entire demonstrated capability is measuring pixels. Estimate 15-25 hours of the owner's OWN time over 6-8 weeks, mostly messaging and waiting — against a lotería card the AI can draft in-session. For an owner whose stated complaint is "we need to finally present something," this concept presents in two months, not two weeks.

ONE OVER-DECLARED RISK, IN THE CONCEPT'S FAVOUR. Risk THREE (the 251/267 address) does not apply to this artefact: the specified board text is CIUDAD DE MÉXICO / YUCATÁN (MUY DESPACIO) / PLAYA DEL CARMEN / [POR BARCO] / NUEVA YORK / TACOS · TORTAS · JUGOS / DIRECTO. No street number appears. The contradiction is real and live in the repo (SPEC.md line 344 states "251 ELIZABETH STREET / NUEVA YORK"; the refuter file records 267) but it gates a different object. Drop it from this concept's register and stop paying for it.

CEILING ON THIS AUDIT. I verified the source constants, the audit.py arithmetic, the EXIF dates, the A1/A0 dimensions, the steel mass and the rake by running them this session. I did NOT verify: current rotulista rates (no primary quote), current US customs treatment of hand-painted steel signage, or whether the bus is at that address today — the last is precisely the unverifiable that sinks the score, and "it cannot be recovered from what we hold" is the honest reading of it.**

* ATTACKS: SIX ATTACKS, EACH RUN AGAINST THE MACHINE OR THE FILES THIS SESSION.

ATTACK 1 — THE SITE MAY NOT EXIST. Ran EXIF on the reference set. `ref_nolita_front34.jpg` DateTimeOriginal 2014:04:05 13:20:23 (SONY DSC-RX100, 700x467). `IMG_3840.jpeg` 2010:10:04 16:00:11 (Canon EOS 5D II, 480x320). Today 2026-09-05. The concept's physical premise — a specific bus, in a specific room, with an empty windscreen — rests on a 12-year-old photograph, and the address on a 16-year-old one. I opened the 2014 frame: bus whole, screen empty, indoors, busy service room, a toddler at the bumper. True in 2014. Unestablished in 2026. Compounding: "I have never stood in the bus" is in twelve briefs (rev 17-56) and is codified in CLAUDE.md, so the one person who could check has stated for fifty-nine revisions that he cannot casually. BOTH object (a) and the entire first step are gated on an access request that has never been made.

ATTACK 2 — THE FIRST STEP IS PHYSICALLY IMPOSSIBLE AS WRITTEN, TWICE. (i) It says print 900 x 300 and stand it in the windscreen. 900 mm does not enter a 585 mm pane; the 900 x 300 is the WALL board. The mock tests the wrong object in the wrong aperture. (ii) It says one A1 sheet. A1 = 841 x 594; 900 > 841, so the print does not fit at full size. Needs A0 (1189 x 841, verified) or roll stock. The "free five-second test" currently cannot be run.

ATTACK 3 — THE ONLY DERIVED FIGURE IS WRONG, BY THE PROJECT'S OWN MACHINE. Concept publishes 1196.0 mm across both panes plus divider. `t1_shell.py`: `yc = s * (WS_DIV + WS_PANE_W / 2)` — WS_DIV is a HALF-offset, so the full divider is 52.0 mm. `audit.py` prints `(2 panes, divider %.3f) % (S.WS_DIV * 2)` and `total glass width = 2 * S.WS_PANE_W + 2 * S.WS_DIV` = 1222.0 mm. Concept computed 2W + D = 1196.0. Off by 26 mm; rule 5 firing on a concept sheet.

ATTACK 4 — THE MODEL DEPENDENCY DISSOLVES UNDER ITS OWN MITIGATION. Confirmed at `t1_shell.py` that `WS_PANE_W, WS_PANE_H, WS_DIV, WS_R = 0.5850, 0.3770, 0.0260, 0.050` sits with NO provenance comment. The concept correctly refuses to grade it MEASURED and correctly says verify on the real bus before cutting. Those two together mean the model's contribution is not "one number out of seventy-six revisions" — it is zero, because the in-person measurement supersedes it the moment it is taken. The concept braces for the wrong criticism.

ATTACK 5 — FABRICATION AND SAFETY, COMPUTED. 585 x 377 x 1.2 mm steel = 2.08 kg; 900 x 300 x 1.2 mm = 2.54 kg (7850 kg/m3). No mounting specified for either, in a public dining room the reference frame shows containing a small child. A flat 1.2 mm panel over 900 mm with no return edge will oil-can. The 50 mm radius is the APERTURE's `WS_R`, copied to a placard where it is 13% of the height. And the screen rakes 27.3 deg from vertical (P_TOP 1.8340/1.7745 to P_BOT 2.0400/1.3755, run 0.4490 m), so a 377 mm in-plane board rises only 335.0 mm to a viewer in front — an ~11% vertical foreshortening the type brief never mentions. Fit clearance is zero: the model's own frame leaves ~579.5 mm visible against a 585.0 board.

ATTACK 6 — COST AND CAPABILITY. US$150-400/board is a shop rate; a four-to-six-colour signed commission with a painted vignette is US$300-800. Absent entirely from the budget: MX->NY freight for ~13 kg of rigid steel (US$250-500) and a customs classification call (artwork vs. articles of iron/steel, Section 232 exposure). Landed US$3,000-4,500 against a stated US$2,500. Timeline 2-4 weeks against a realistic 5-9 including freight and customs. Delivery requires Spanish-language artist sourcing, a cross-border commission agreement and a customs entry — all new to this team, none AI-accelerable, ~15-25 hours of the owner's own time. AGAINST WHICH: it consumes zero machine time, needs no line pass, no export, no front-camera fix, no determinism, and touches neither F191 nor F193. That is why this scores 2 and not 1.

ONE ATTACK THAT FAILED, RECORDED IN THE CONCEPT'S FAVOUR: the address risk does not bind. The specified board text carries no street number (CIUDAD DE MÉXICO / YUCATÁN (MUY DESPACIO) / PLAYA DEL CARMEN / [POR BARCO] / NUEVA YORK / TACOS · TORTAS · JUGOS / DIRECTO). The 251-vs-267 contradiction is real in the repo (SPEC.md:344 vs the rev-50 refuter) but gates a different artefact. Over-declared.
* WOULD FIX IT: SEVEN CONCRETE FIXES. None touches the confrontational content — MUY DESPACIO, POR BARCO, the costume confession and the commission-credit-pay floor all survive intact.

1. MAKE THE ACCESS ASK THE FIRST STEP, BEFORE ANY PAPER. One email or call to the operator of the Elizabeth Street room, asking two things: is the bus still in place, and may we stand a temporary board in the windscreen for ten minutes to photograph. Cost zero, one day. If the answer is no or the bus has moved, this concept is DEAD as written and must be re-aimed at object (b) alone — four wall boards, which need no vehicle at all and survive the loss of the bus completely. Say that branch out loud in the concept so the owner sees the fallback before he sees the risk.

2. REWRITE THE MOCK-UP STEP SO IT CAN BE PERFORMED. Two mocks, not one, and name which board each is: (i) WINDSCREEN MOCK — print 545 x 340 mm on A2 or A3 tiled, mount to 5 mm foamboard, that size chosen as pane minus a 20 mm/18.5 mm per-side margin; (ii) WALL MOCK — print 900 x 300 on A0 (1189 x 841, which fits) or roll stock, mount, hang at the pass. Delete "stand the 900 in the windscreen" entirely; it cannot be done.

3. CORRECT THE FIGURE AND CITE THE MACHINE THAT PRINTS IT. Replace "1196.0 mm" with "1222.0 mm across both panes plus divider (2 x 585.0 + 2 x 26.0), full divider 52.0 mm — as `audit.py` prints it: `total glass width = 2 * S.WS_PANE_W + 2 * S.WS_DIV`. WS_DIV is a HALF-offset from the centreline, not the divider width." Then run `T1_SUB=2 blender -b -P audit.py` and quote the line you watched print, per rule 5.

4. STOP CLAIMING A MODEL DEPENDENCY AND MAKE THE MODEL EARN A DIFFERENT ONE. The honest line is: "this artefact needs ZERO numbers from seventy-six revisions, and I am saying so." Then, if you want the model in the object at all, put it somewhere a tape measure cannot go: set the board's type to the vehicle's OWN measured proportions — the 515.5 x 403.0 aperture ratio as the text block's ratio, the 17.75 mm/m rake as the baseline slope of the DIRECTO tab, the 2400 mm wheelbase as the module the itinerary lines are spaced on. Those are figures a painter cannot obtain and the machine can. That converts "the width of a windscreen" into a real answer.

5. FIX THE FABRICATION SPEC IN FOUR LINES. (i) 10 mm hemmed return on all four edges of both boards — kills the oil-can and is how real lámina is made. (ii) Windscreen board 545 x 340, corner radius 25 mm chosen for a placard, NOT the aperture's 50 mm — and say in the brief that 50 belongs to the hole, not the sign. (iii) Specify the mount: two 6 mm steel feet welded to the reverse, or a dashboard cradle, so 2.08 kg of steel is retained and not propped; a child stands at that bumper in the reference frame. (iv) Wall board: two keyhole slots, D-ring or French cleat, rated.

6. TELL THE PAINTER ABOUT THE RAKE. Add to the windscreen brief: "this board sits in a screen raked 27.3 deg from vertical; a viewer in front sees 377 mm of board as 335 mm. Set the cap heights ~11% taller than looks right flat on the bench, or letter it in situ." Sign painters do this routinely once told; nobody does it unasked.

7. RE-COST HONESTLY AND SPLIT THE BUY. State US$300-800 per board for a named, signed, credited commission (not the US$150-400 shop rate, which is the rate for the thing this concept forbids), plus US$250-500 freight, plus a customs line marked UNRESOLVED — hand-painted signage on steel may classify as artwork or as an article of iron/steel; get a broker's word before ordering five. Landed budget US$3,000-4,500, 5-9 weeks. THEN SPLIT IT: commission ONE wall board first, from one painter, for ~US$600 landed in ~4 weeks. It needs no bus, no access, no measurement, and no permission. If it is beautiful on the wall, the windscreen board and the other three follow with the risk already retired. That turns an unverified 9-week US$4,500 five-object programme into a verified 4-week US$600 test — which is the only version of this an impatient owner should be shown.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**ORIGINALITY — 2/4 — FRESH REGISTER, WORN OBJECT. DIRECTO is the only thing on the bench made of paint rather than vector, and that alone earns it a place — but the artefact it proposes sits at the intersection of three separate, heavily worked traditions (VW destination-board souvenir, pesero letrero as design fetish, commission-a-rotulista as brand ethics), and it is fresh in none of them. Its genuine novelty is about one line of copy long: MUY DESPACIO / POR BARCO. Worse, the repo's own SPEC §7 records that the Nolita bus ALREADY carries hand-painted place names — "251 ELIZABETH STREET / NUEVA YORK" on the rear corner, hand-painted menu names on the apertures, a chalkboard roof board — so this proposes adding one more hand-lettered panel, in the same idiom, to a vehicle that is already a hand-lettered object. The concept read that material (it quotes the lid rail and the menu strips) and did not treat it as prior art on itself. Acquitted on the "flat-measured-line under a new label" charge at the level of the artefact; convicted at the level of rhetoric.**

* ATTACKS: 1. THE OBJECT IS A SOUVENIR FORM. "Hand-painted destination board propped in a split-screen windscreen" is one of the densest categories in VW memorabilia — Etsy has standing markets for split-screen/split-window bus signage, Garage Art sells a Volkswagen sign category, eBay carries carved destination boards. The ironic destination ("DIRECTO" on a route that took a boat) is that genre's DEFAULT gesture, not a subversion of it: NOWHERE, PARADISE, WOODSTOCK, GONE TO THE BEACH. A viewer meets the joke shape before they meet the joke. See https://www.etsy.com/market/split_screen_vw_bus and https://www.garageart.com/product-category/signs/volkswagen/

2. THE MEXICAN REFERENT IS ALREADY A DESIGN FETISH, AND HAS BEEN FOR A DECADE. Pesero letreros are collected and reproduced at scale — Pinterest runs "letreros para combis" as a standing ideas category, EPiK Center of Design has pesero vector illustration in circulation, TikTok has a letrero-de-autobuses tag. The concept's own cited lineage, Alex Kurunis's Rotulismo Mexicano (It's Nice That, 2022, https://www.itsnicethat.com/articles/alex-kurunis-photography-230322), is a mainstream documentary project. The inversion the concept sells as its insight — "the English-speaking VW world has flower power, the Mexican referent is a working vehicle" — is the standard framing in that literature, not a discovery.

3. THE ETHICAL MOVE HAS A CANONICAL PRECEDENT AND THE CONCEPT NAMES IT. Francis Alÿs commissioned Mexico City rotulistas from 1993 — thirty-three years ago. "Commission, credit, pay" is now the boilerplate corrective position in this exact discourse; PRINT Magazine has run the class-conflict argument (https://www.printmag.com/graffiti-and-street-art/mexicos-hand-painted-signs-inspire-class-conflict-but-not-how-you-might-think/). And the negative case the concept defines itself against — the "Mexican-look font" — literally exists as a free download: Sam Bumbalo's "Rotulista", a font inspired by Mexican sign painting (https://www.behance.net/gallery/55890321/Rotulista-Free-Font-Inspired-by-Mexican-Sign-Painting). Refusing that font is a well-rehearsed position, not a fresh one. Being right is not the same as being new.

4. IT MAY BE THE CLIENT'S OWN EXISTING PRACTICE. Tacombi's identity is already built on hand-painted Mexican signage and on this bus (https://tacombi.com/company/story). If the brand has ever commissioned a rotulista — and its store aesthetic strongly suggests it has — then DIRECTO is not a fresh idea for this client, it is a repeat of work they have already bought. The concept did not check. That check costs one search and it is the single highest-leverage thing missing.

5. INTERNAL DUPLICATION — THE BENCH ALREADY HOLDS THE ROTULISMO SLOT. ROTULISTA is in the next-eight and is the same craft, same practitioner, same cultural engine; DIRECTO is its static-object sibling. And the itinerary content is already claimed three ways on the bench: NUEVA YORK, PLAYA DEL CARMEN, THE CROSSING, plus HORARIO / ABIERTO / LA CARTA / LA BASE working the same text-on-object territory. Strip what is already spoken for and the residue is: one ironic top-left word and two parentheticals.

6. THE HOUSE TIC IS INTACT, JUST IN ENAMEL. The board's payload is a CONFESSION OF IMPRECISION — it took a peninsula slowly and then a boat, and the concept says the confession is its only defence. That is structurally identical to THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE's open brackets and NO LEGIBLE's unreadable specimens. The bench's actual signature is not flat measured line, it is "the artefact admits what the record cannot support," and DIRECTO transposes that unchanged. The concept text leaks it too: "585.0 × 377.0 mm ... PROVENANCE UNSTATED IN SOURCE, therefore not graded measured" is grading vocabulary applied to an object the concept itself says needs no measurement.

7. THE MODEL CONTRIBUTION IS ZERO, NOT ONE. The concept admits "one number out of seventy-six revisions" and deserves credit for saying so — but it undersells its own problem. Its own instruction is "verify it against the actual bus before anyone cuts steel," which concedes that a tape measure held against the real windscreen OUTRANKS the mesh. So the model supplies a number that must be discarded before use. The brief asks for a program built on the model; this is a program built on a taqueria and a painter, and it would be exactly as good if this repository did not exist.

8. WE CANNOT TEST ITS FRESHNESS AND NEITHER CAN THE OWNER FROM HERE. The FIRST STEP requires physical access to Elizabeth Street. The pitch IS the photograph, and nobody in this loop can take it. Every other bench item can be drafted and looked at; this one converts into a purchase order and a plane ticket before anyone can tell whether it reads as vernacular or as kitsch — which is precisely the question the concept says the photograph will answer in five seconds.

9. THE COSTUME RISK IS UNDER-PRESSED, NOT OVER-PRESSED. The concept's defence is that the text confesses. But a route board is a PASSENGER artefact — its whole content is where people get off. This vehicle carried tacos, not people. The pesero referent that actually fits is "works for a living," and that referent needs no destination board at all. Meanwhile a US hospitality group installing a Mexico City working-class transit signifier in a Nolita dining room is the exact object of the class-conflict critique above, and commission-credit-pay is the defence every brand doing this already claims.
* WOULD FIX IT: 1. KILL THE BOARD, KEEP THE PAINTER, AND MOVE HIM TO THE OFF SIDE. SPEC.md grades the off-side elevation "E (never photographed)" — an entire flank of this vehicle that no frame in the reference set shows. Commission the rotulista to paint THAT side. The object becomes "the surface the record cannot check," which nobody else on earth can make because nobody else has a vehicle with a documented unphotographed flank. And it inverts the model's role from one discarded number to the whole substrate: the mesh is the ONLY source for that side's aperture positions (515.5 x 403.0, three, equal, r55), the pillar rhythm, the rake at 17.75 mm/m, the belt line. Same craft, same commission ethics, same enamel — zero prior art, and the model finally earns its place. Do this one.

2. IF THE BOARD SURVIVES, MAKE IT THE THING A PESERO BOARD IS NEVER ALLOWED TO BE: A FARE BOARD. The name pesero IS the fare — one peso. Paint the price face: the 2006 fare, and what a taco costs on Elizabeth Street today, in the same hand, same board, same enamel. That is outside the souvenir genre entirely, and it puts the class question ON the object instead of parked in a risk register where only we read it.

3. DELETE "DIRECTO" FROM THE TOP-LEFT. It is the genre's reflex joke and the viewer has met it before. The pesero top-left slot carries the BASE — the terminal you return to. Paint PLAYA DEL CARMEN there and let the itinerary run away from it, so the board reads as a vehicle a very long way from home rather than as a wink. Keep MUY DESPACIO and POR BARCO; they are the only genuinely fresh copy in the concept and they do the irony without announcing it.

4. RUN THE PRIOR-ART CHECK BEFORE THE COMMISSION, NOT BEFORE THE PAINT. Three searches, this week, free: (a) Tacombi's own store-signage back catalogue and press — has the brand already hired rotulistas? (b) Etsy/eBay "VW destination board" — photograph the ten nearest matches and put them beside the sketch. (c) "letreros para combis" image sets — confirm the stacking idiom is being used correctly and not as pastiche. If (a) comes back positive, this concept is retired, not revised.

5. FIX THE ADDRESS BEFORE THE PITCH, NOT BEFORE THE STEEL. SPEC §7 says 251 Elizabeth Street; IMG_3840 reads 267. That is one phone call to the restaurant. Carrying an unresolved contradiction into a pitch as a declared risk is the project's habit, and here it is unnecessary — the answer is obtainable in a minute by someone who is not us.

6. DROP THE WINDSCREEN CONSTANT FROM THE CONCEPT ENTIRELY. Do not cite 585.0 x 377.0 or the 1196.0 derivation. An ungraded constant reached for to justify the object's presence on this bench is weaker than the honest sentence: "the model contributes nothing to this one; it belongs here because the program needs an object made by a human hand and this is it." Say that to the owner. He asked for fresh, not for a dependency.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**OWNER-AND-BRAND — 2/4 — A genuinely on-brand object with the best first step on the bench — and, as written, four things that would land badly in front of this specific owner, one of which is a wrong number that becomes steel. Tacombi's identity IS hand-painted signage, so a commissioned rotulista board is closer to his brand than almost anything else here, it puts FOOD and a NAMED HUMAN back into a programme F331 records as still missing both, and its A1 mock-up answers "we need to finally present something" this week with no render queue. But it lectures a Mexican-American founder about appropriating Mexican craft, it hangs "MUY DESPACIO" by the pass of a restaurant, it violates SPEC §7's livery bar in its one artwork move, it silently manufactures a fourth livery state that contaminates the reference set the whole project runs on, and the single number it takes from seventy-six revisions of measurement is wrong — so the board as dimensioned does not fit the hole it is cut for.**

* ATTACKS: 1. THE ONE NUMBER IT TOOK FROM THE MODEL IS WRONG, AND IT IS A FABRICATION ERROR IN STEEL. `t1_shell.py` reads `WS_PANE_W, WS_PANE_H, WS_DIV, WS_R = 0.5850, 0.3770, 0.0260, 0.050` and builds each pane at `yc = s * (WS_DIV + WS_PANE_W / 2)` — WS_DIV is a HALF-gap from the centreline, so the divider is 52 mm and the pair spans 1222.0 mm. The concept publishes 1196.0. Worse, the board is specced at 585.0 x 377.0, i.e. EXACTLY the cutter pane, while `windscreen_seals()` cuts its inner rrect at `WS_PANE_W - 0.0055` x `WS_PANE_H - 0.0055` = 579.5 x 371.5 mm. The board is 5.5 mm too big in both axes and cannot sit inside the pane. The concept's own pitch line is "seventy-six revisions of geometry contribute one number, and that fact belongs in front of the owner." In front of an owner who asks "Why do we keep doing Revs that don't ship geometry", the sentence that actually arrives is: seventy-six revisions contributed one number, we got it wrong, and we cut steel to it. (Credit where due: the 50 mm corner IS correct — `T.rrect(pane_w, pane_h, WS_R, seg=8)` — and the concept is right that these constants carry no provenance comment and are not graded measured.)

2. "MUY DESPACIO" BY THE PASS. Object (b) is a 900 x 300 wall board hung "by the pass," in a fast-casual taqueria, reading VERY SLOWLY in the language most of the kitchen speaks. The concept names three risks and this is not one of them. The joke's target is speed; the room's operational sin is speed. Guests waiting for tacos read it as a comment on their wait; expediters read it as a comment on them. It works in the windscreen, where it is about a journey. On the wall by the pass it is an own-goal, and it is the object that gets multiplied "one per site."

3. THE JOKE IS THE FOUNDER'S AND WE ARE CASTING IT IN ENAMEL FOR HIM. "wheeled it slowly (very slowly) through the Yucatan peninsula" is his self-deprecation about his own bus. A founder is entitled to that line. A vendor rendering it permanent, unattributed, signed by a third party, and hanging it where his customers eat is a different speech act. And the headline word DIRECTO is then a lie played for irony: a design-literate adult gets it in three seconds; a family reading a destination board that says DIRECT over a route that isn't just reads it as wrong.

4. THE APPROPRIATION FRAMING INVERTS ON THIS READER. "Buying four boards from four painters is the cheapest possible act of not being a company that Photoshops a Mexican-look font" is written for an Anglo design audience. Addressed to Dario Wolos it is an accusation he is at risk of doing that, delivered by us. Related: Tacombi's real venues are already full of hand-painted signage; proposing a rotulista commission as an ethical breakthrough to a brand built on commissioned hand-painting risks explaining his own business to him.

5. IT VIOLATES SPEC §7 IN ITS ONLY ARTWORK MOVE, AND CITES THE VIOLATION AS A FEATURE. SPEC §7 "Do not mix references": the Nolita bus has "no folk art, no side script, glazed apertures with gold frames and hand-painted menu names, a chalkboard roof board. Any photograph showing those features is the wrong LIVERY STATE." The concept puts a "painted food vignette copied from the bus's own menu strips" — PINK 206/118/124, GREEN 96/128/54, CREAMV 232/224/198, folk-art-livery artwork — into the NOLITA bus's windscreen. That is precisely the paint-across-livery-states transfer rule 11 forbids and that killed F99, F100 and F140. SPEC §7.1 settles same VEHICLE; it explicitly does not license artwork transfer: "folk art, script, aperture glazing and roof-board treatment may never be carried across states."

6. IT MANUFACTURES A FOURTH LIVERY STATE AND NOBODY DECLARED IT. This is the project-specific harm and it is the one I would put in front of the owner first. The moment a board WE invented sits in that windscreen, every future photograph of the Nolita bus contains a painted element with no photographic source — under a standing requirement (SPEC §10.10, "a hard bar, not a preference") that "every painted element on this vehicle must be REPRODUCED from photographs of the actual combi. Not approximated. Not invented." We would be inventing an element and then photographing it into the reference set, and a later revision would reason about our own fiction. This is F309's disease exactly — planting removable event gear permanently into the record — and F309 retired the roof loudspeakers for it.

7. THE F191 CLEARANCE IS OVERSTATED. "no roundel appears — the board is not the bus — so F191 is untouched" is false for object (a). The pitch photograph of a board propped in the split screen is a photograph of the bus's FACE, roundel included; that is the whole point of the shot. The clearance probably still holds — it is his own vehicle and the artefact reproduces no roundel — but the declaration as written is wrong, and this project retracts wrong declarations rather than shipping them.

8. WHAT SURVIVES THE ATTACK, STATED FAIRLY. F331 (RULED-rev77) splits the audience deliberately and CONFIRMS the deadpan adult register as half the programme, so "it skews adult, he asked for children" is no longer an available attack — it is a sanctioned line. F331 also leaves critic finding 4, "NO FOOD, NO PEOPLE, in a programme for a taqueria," explicitly open, and this concept is one of the few that answers both: TACOS · TORTAS · JUGOS, a food vignette, and a credited living painter. Its FIRST STEP — an A1 stand-in mounted on foamboard, stood in the windscreen, photographed from where a customer sits — is the strongest thing on the sheet: it costs nothing, needs no render queue, and produces a presentable photograph in a week. Under US$2,500 for five objects is real money against nothing this programme has shipped. Would he be proud to hand someone the windscreen board? Yes. The wall board, as worded, no.
* WOULD FIX IT: CONCRETE, IN ORDER:

1. RE-DERIVE THE PANE BEFORE ANYTHING IS DRAWN. Publish 1222.0 mm across the pair (2 x 585 + 2 x 26), not 1196.0, and say in the concept that WS_DIV is a half-gap — quote `yc = s * (WS_DIV + WS_PANE_W / 2)` so the next reader can check it. Spec the board at 565 x 357 mm, i.e. 10 mm clearance inside the seal's 579.5 x 371.5 opening, and state the clearance as part of the dimension. Keep the 50 mm corner; it is correct. Keep the concept's own ceiling — these constants carry no provenance comment and are not graded measured — and add: TAPE-MEASURE THE ACTUAL BUS BEFORE STEEL IS CUT, because a 5.5 mm error already got through once.

2. RE-WORD OBJECT (b). Strike MUY DESPACIO from the wall board entirely. Let the contradiction between the word DIRECTO and the route carry the irony; on the windscreen board keep [POR BARCO], which is a fact and reads as one. If the slow gag must survive on the wall, it goes as a dated quotation attributed to the founder, not as an adverb in the route.

3. ATTRIBUTE THE JOKE. Set his own words in quotes with a date on the reverse alongside the rotulista's signature, or don't make the joke. That single change turns "a vendor mocking the founder's bus" into "the founder's own line, painted by a named person" — which is the object the concept thinks it is describing.

4. DELETE THE MORAL FRAMING FROM THE PITCH. Keep commission–credit–pay as a budget line and as a kill condition ("if it becomes printed vinyl, scrap it"). Cut the Photoshopped-font sentence. Before proposing painters, ask whether Tacombi already has one — if they do, commission theirs and say so; that is stronger, cheaper and removes the condescension entirely.

5. FIX THE LIVERY VIOLATION. Either (a) drop the food vignette and let lettering alone carry the board — the pesero idiom is lettering, not illustration, so this costs nothing — or (b) source the vignette from the NOLITA frames' own "hand-painted menu names" (SPEC §7) rather than from the folk-art-livery menu strips. Do not carry PINK/GREEN/CREAMV across states. State the rule and the choice on the face of the concept, because SPEC §7 is a standing bar and a reviewer will check it.

6. WRITE THE ARCHIVE STEP INTO THE CONCEPT AS A PRECONDITION, NOT A NICETY. Before the board goes into that windscreen: photograph the bus WITHOUT it, from every angle, prioritising the OFF SIDE that SPEC grades "E (never photographed)"; commit those as `ref_nolita_*`; and give every post-board frame a distinct prefix so no future revision mistakes our object for the vehicle. Add a line to the concept saying plainly that this artefact CREATES A FOURTH LIVERY STATE under SPEC §10.10 and that the archive pass is what keeps it from contaminating the record. That turns the concept's worst hidden liability into its second-best contribution — a free reference-set pass on the one elevation nobody has ever shot.

7. CORRECT THE F191 SENTENCE. Replace "no roundel appears — the board is not the bus" with: the ARTEFACT reproduces no roundel, so F191 and F193 are untouched; but the pitch photograph of object (a) shows the vehicle's face including the roundel, and that is a photograph of his own vehicle, not a reproduction of the mark.

8. NO ADDRESS ON STEEL, AND SAY WHY IT IS MOOT. Keep the existing prohibition, and add that SPEC §7 already records "251 ELIZABETH STREET / NUEVA YORK" as artwork ON THE BUS's rear corner while `probe_scratch/rev50/refuter/REFUTATION_rev50.md` reads 267 — the vehicle carries a number already, so the board never needs one and the contradiction can stay open.

9. LABEL THE LINE. F331 requires every future shortlist to say which line each item is in. DIRECTO is ADULT LINE. Say so, and stop defending it on child appeal.

10. SHIP THE FIRST STEP AS THE PITCH. Do the A1 mock-up, stand it in the windscreen, shoot it from a customer's seat, and put THAT PHOTOGRAPH in front of him with the corrected dimensions beside it. It is the only item on this bench that can be in his hands this week without a render queue, which is the thing he actually asked for.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**DANGER — 3/4 — The danger is intact but domesticated — the object still carries a genuine class and migration charge that cannot be removed without scrapping it, and the DIRECTO / MUY DESPACIO / POR BARCO confession sits on the artefact's own face rather than in its caption. But three of its sharpest available elements have migrated from the steel into the prose: the fare is explained and not painted, the painter's name is on the reverse where no customer sees it, and the layout is art-directed into a tasteful designer's version of an idiom whose whole character is that it is not tasteful. Worse, the single largest charge in the concept — a Mexico City to New York route board hung in Manhattan, narrating the frictionless version of the most contested journey in the hemisphere — is present in the object and absent from the five-item risk list. That is a danger left live and undefended rather than one that was sanded off. Credit where it is due: the kill condition and the scrap condition are real and unhedged, and risk FOUR turns the knife on the person who has to approve it. This is one of the few concepts on the bench with something actually at stake. It is not a 4 because it moved its best content into the footnotes and hid the painter on the back.**

* ATTACKS: WHAT IS ACTUALLY DANGEROUS HERE (name it before judging whether it survives):

(1) CLASS. A pesero is not folklore. It is precarious, informal, commission-driven transport for people who cannot afford anything else — in Mexico City it carries a reputation for robbery, for drivers racing for fares, for deaths. It is not charming there. Hanging its placard in a room on Elizabeth Street where a taco is six dollars states the brand's founding transaction out loud: a working-class Mexican vehicle idiom, monetised in Manhattan. That is the live wire and it is genuinely brand-risky.

(2) MIGRATION, AND THE CONCEPT NEVER NAMES IT. The board reads CIUDAD DE MÉXICO ... NUEVA YORK. That is the most travelled and most contested route line in the hemisphere, and this object draws it on steel and hangs it in Manhattan. The version narrated is the frictionless one — POR BARCO, MUY DESPACIO, a founder's amusing road trip, no border, no visa, no one else on the vehicle. Every restaurant worker in that room who took the other version of that route will read this board. The five-item risk list does not contain this. An unnamed danger is the worst kind: it is fully present in the object and completely undefended in the room where it gets attacked. This is my single biggest finding.

(3) THE JOKE IS THE OPERATOR'S OWN. DIRECTO on a pesero board is a lie everybody knows is a lie, and using it over MUY DESPACIO and POR BARCO is a real, sharp, structural irony — at the operator's expense, and the operator is the founder. That part is genuinely uncomfortable and it is ON THE OBJECT, not in the caption. It survives.

WHERE IT HAS BEEN SANDED — three specific places, and each is the prior round's exact failure mode in miniature:

(a) THE FARE IS IN THE CAPTION AND NOT ON THE BOARD. The pitch spends a whole sentence on "the name IS the fare: one peso." Peseros put the fare on the board — it is the single most characteristic thing the idiom does. The concept omits it. A price painted on a route board, hanging in a room whose menu is three metres away, is the one element that would actually sting: it forces the comparison. Explaining the fare in prose and leaving it off the steel is precisely "the plant became a tended herb in a chipped tin."

(b) THE PAINTER IS ON THE REVERSE. "Signed and dated on the reverse by the rotulista." The whole ethical claim of this concept is commission–credit–pay, and the credit is put where no customer will ever see it. Real rotulistas sign the FACE, at size, with a phone number — that is the trade's own convention and this concept has replaced it with a gallery convention. It makes the object's politics deniable.

(c) IT IS A DESIGNER'S PESERO BOARD. Real ones are over-outlined, drop-shadowed, saints and football clubs and stickers and tinsel, colours from whatever tin was open. This one is a clean stacked itinerary with a tidy footer and DIRECTO neatly top-left. "Four to six colours, whatever the painter mixes" is permissive about palette and silent about layout — and the brief as written specifies the layout, so what comes back will be tasteful. Tasteful is the failure state.

(d) A FOURTH, SMALLER: THE MOURNFUL READING IS LEFT ON THE TABLE. A destination board on a vehicle that will never move again is an epitaph. The concept plays it entirely as comedy.

WHAT IS NOT SANDED, AND SHOULD BE CREDITED: the kill condition is real and unhedged ("if the budget is cut to a printed rotulista-style vinyl board, KILL THIS CONCEPT rather than value-engineer it"), and so is the scrap condition if anyone straightens the route into three clean cities. Risk FOUR is a genuine self-indictment aimed at the approver — 76 revisions of geometry contribute one number, and it says so rather than manufacturing a dependency. That is the opposite of sanding and most of the bench does not do it.

TWO NON-LENS DEFECTS I WILL NOT LET PASS: the first step prints the board at 900 × 300 and stands it in a 585 × 377 pane — the wall-board size in the windscreen, so "that photograph IS the pitch" would be a photograph of the wrong object. And the wall board — one per site, hung by the pass, four of them — is the SAFE placement: above a counter it reads as branding. The windscreen board is the one that carries the charge, because it puts a vehicle that is now furniture back to work.
* WOULD FIX IT: FOUR CONCRETE CHANGES, none of which costs money:

1. PUT THE FARE ON THE BOARD. Bottom right, where a pesero carries it, in the idiom's own hand: a fare. Whether it is $1.00, or the 2006 Mexico City pesero fare in pesos, or the price of one taco in the room it hangs in — that is a decision the owner should be made to take deliberately, and the concept should present it as such rather than avoid it. This single element restores the class charge to the object's face.

2. THE PAINTER SIGNS THE FACE. Name, city, year, at the size the trade actually uses, in the lower left of the board — not the reverse.
