If the house style cannot survive a stranger's name painted at 40 mm on an object in the dining room, then the commission was decoration and the ethics paragraph was a label. This is the test of whether the concept's stated politics are real.

3. NAME THE MIGRATION READING IN THE RISK SECTION, AND DECIDE WHETHER THE BOARD ANSWERS IT. Add it as risk SIX, in these terms: this board draws Mexico City to New York and hangs it in Manhattan, and the route it narrates is a founder's, not a worker's. Then take a position on the object. The strongest available move is to let the board admit whose route it is — a line at the foot in the pesero idiom naming the boat, the year, and one passenger, so that the object is explicitly about ONE vehicle's crossing and does not silently stand in for anybody else's. What must not happen is shipping it with this unnamed.

4. DO NOT ART-DIRECT THE PAINTER'S LAYOUT. Give the rotulista the itinerary, the word DIRECTO, the fare and the food words, and the board size — and nothing else. No stack order, no "important stop biggest", no colour count. Accept what comes back even if it embarrasses the house style, and write that acceptance into the commission. A brief that specifies the composition produces a graphic designer's pesero board; a brief that specifies only the content produces a pesero board.

AND FIX THE MOCK: print the stand-in at 585 × 377 so it actually sits inside the pane. Photograph both the windscreen board in the screen and, separately, the wall board by the pass, and put them side by side — the comparison will show that the windscreen is the object that carries the idea and the wall board is the one that becomes decor. Consider dropping the wall board entirely, or making it a different object rather than the same board four times.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

### GRACIAS POR SU PREFERENCIA — audit mean 2.12/4

**FEASIBILITY — 2/4 — BUILDABLE, BUT THE CAMERA IT SPECIFIES DOES NOT EXIST ON THIS VEHICLE — AND THE ONE NUMBER THAT DECIDES WHETHER ITS HERO ELEMENT IS IN FRAME IS NEVER STATED.

Two things first, in the concept's favour, because they are real and most of the bench gets them wrong. (1) The plate is ONE STILL. "Nothing in the world moves except hands" means the Blender cost is four passes off one locked camera — about 20 minutes — not 192 frames at 5 min each (16 hours, with F308's non-determinism flickering every frame). That is the single most important feasibility judgment in the piece and it is correct. (2) The premise handed to me is stale in the concept's favour: `/home/user/combi_render/line_pass.py` SHIPPED at rev 77 (Grease Pencil Line Art -> camera-normalised polylines, with `--svg`), and `design_out/loteria_la_rueda.svg` proves the chain end-to-end. The keystone is built. And the 1:4 first-step arithmetic is right in all four places — I checked 515.5/4=128.9, 403.0/4=100.8, 55/4=13.8, 19.9/4=5.0.

Then it walks into the geometry.

I measured the staging off the constants (`t1_shell.Z_SILL/Z_HEAD/BAY_CX`, `t1_core.rake_drop`, `t1_detail` van_floor prism). The concept's own sill figure reproduces EXACTLY — 1323.2 mm AG at the middle bay — so its measured half is honest. The rest does not survive:

    van_floor top       511.2 mm AG   <- the cook stands here
    aperture sill      1323.2 mm AG   =  812.0 mm above that floor
    aperture head      1726.2 mm AG   = 1215.0 mm above that floor
    aperture centre    1524.7 mm AG   = 1013.5 mm above that floor

A 1.70 m cook's eye is 1580 mm above the floor they stand on. The TOP OF THE HOLE is at 1215 mm. The cook's eye is 365 mm ABOVE THE HEAD RAIL (275 mm for a 1.60 m cook, 460 mm for a 1.80 m). A standing adult cannot see out of this hole at eye level at all; they look down into it. To put an eye on the aperture's centre axis you need a person about 1.09 m tall. "A locked camera at the cook's eye, inside the middle bay, looking out" is not a pose choice — it is a viewpoint the vehicle does not have.

And the concept pre-declares this as unmeasurable — "the camera height is a DECLARED POSE and the end card says UNMEASURED." That is the error, not the defence. The floor is measured and the head rail is measured, so eye height is BOUNDED FROM BELOW by the geometry, and the bound refutes the staging. This is CLAUDE.md rule 7 exactly: a guard written against a pose encodes that pose — ask the geometry, never the pose it happens to be in. The concept asserted an unmeasurable where a measurement was waiting.

Then the brass line, which is the film's whole spine — "the only thing in the picture that is never touched, and every tray crosses it." From a camera on the aperture axis, the slab top (CNT_ZT) sits 118.0 mm BELOW the sill, so the sill edge occludes it, and what you see depends entirely on how far back you stand:

    cam 0.30 m inboard -> 115.3 mm of slab visible
    cam 0.40 m inboard ->  56.8 mm
    cam 0.50 m inboard ->   0.0 mm   <- SLAB AND BRASS BOTH GONE
    cam 0.60 m and back->   0.0 mm

At half a metre back the counter is not in the picture. No gold line, no surface, nothing for a hand to rest on, and no brass for a tray to cross — the entire action of the film is gone. The stated "bottom sixth" (67.2 mm of 403.0) lands at roughly 0.39 m inboard. So the composition IS achievable, at exactly one camera station, and the concept never names it. The single number that decides whether the hero element exists is the one number missing.

Worse, the figure attached to it is imported from the wrong view. "19.9 mm of gold in elevation, 18.6% of a 107.0 mm slab edge, measured over 113 columns" is `t1_detail.CNT_NOSE_F`, and its own comment says it was measured on `ref_side.jpg` — an OUTSIDE elevation. The nosing profile in `t1_detail` starts at `z0 = 0.0015` ("crown, just proud of the top") and rolls from y 0.0000 to 0.0070 before dropping down the face. From INSIDE, above the slab, you see that ~7 mm crown roll at a grazing angle — you never see the 19.9 mm face at all, because it points away from you. A measured figure carried across a view change without a new ruler is rule 38, and it is the project's most expensive recurring defect.

Then the self-contradiction, and it is in one paragraph: "the OCCLUSION PASS, so the shadow the cook's own body throws across the slab is sampled rather than invented" — against, eleven lines later, "no human geometry exists anywhere in this tree." Both cannot hold. To sample that shadow you must build a body proxy, which is invented geometry published as measured — the exact thing the piece's own end card promises not to do.

And the share of frame the model actually carries. The camera looks OUT. Beyond the hole there is no exterior world in this tree — the backdrop is `studio.cyclorama()`. So the picture is: the aperture reveal (real — the body IS solidified, `build.py` step 5, so the double edge exists and will read), a band of slab and brass of AT MOST 67 mm at the bottom, and roughly 80% void that a hired illustrator must fill with hands, food, paper, change and a background that does not exist anywhere. The piece is sold as machine-true line meeting hand-drawn hand; the machine's actual contribution is a border and a gold line. The "one afternoon in Blender" estimate is accurate precisely BECAUSE there is almost no Blender in it — and that inverts the cost story, because every pixel the model does not carry is a pixel someone is paid to draw.

Smaller, but all real: there is NO ENCODER ON THIS BOX — `which ffmpeg` returns nothing, and both the silent-safe MP4 and the ProRes master need one (solvable with `pip install imageio-ffmpeg`, prores_ks for the master, but it is currently a stated deliverable with no path). `T1_ALPHA` is built and guarded and HAS NEVER BEEN RUN, and it is what comps drawings over the plate. The sound — plancha, bottle cap, coins on the slab — is a fourth claimed register with zero budget and no source; nobody has recorded a plancha and the bus is not accessible, so it is library SFX or the owner field-recording at a Tacombi counter, and neither is costed. And the flipbook does not flip: 24 pages at 12 fps is 2.0 seconds, well under the ~40-60 pages a flipbook needs to read as motion, and "saddle-glued at the spine" is not flipbook binding — they are padded/fan-glued on the edge.

On money the concept is closer to honest than most: 192 drawings on twos for 16 s is arithmetically right (6 cycles x 32), $8-15k is a plausible-to-slightly-light freelance 2D rate, and it SAYS it is the most expensive and the only one needing a hire. I would call 3-4 weeks optimistic — 4-6 is realistic for keys, inbetweens, clean-up and flat colour — and registration/compositing over the locked plate is unbudgeted on top.

Nothing here needs a photographer, the inaccessible bus, or a 5000-unit factory minimum. Nothing needs a capability nobody has built except an encoder and an illustrator. The failures are SPECIFICATION failures, not capability gaps — which is why this scores 2 and not 1: every one of them is fixable in an afternoon at a desk, before anyone is hired. But as written, three of its stated figures do not survive contact with its own model, and the piece cannot be shot.**

* ATTACKS: 1. THE VIEWPOINT DOES NOT EXIST — measured, not argued. van_floor top 511.2 mm AG; aperture head 1726.2 mm AG; so the top of the hole is 1215.0 mm above the floor the cook stands on, while a 1.70 m cook's eye is 1580 mm above it. THE COOK'S EYE IS 365 mm ABOVE THE HEAD RAIL (275 mm at 1.60 m, 460 mm at 1.80 m). A camera on the aperture's centre axis sits at the eye height of a 1.09 m person. "A locked camera at the cook's eye ... looking out" cannot be shot on this vehicle.

2. IT DECLARES THE REFUTABLE THING UNMEASURABLE. "the camera height is a DECLARED POSE and the end card says UNMEASURED" — but the floor and the head rail are BOTH measured and jointly bound eye height from below. Rule 7: ask the geometry, never the pose. The concept asserted an unmeasurable where a measurement was waiting to refute it.

3. THE HERO ELEMENT VANISHES AT AN UNDECLARED CAMERA STATION. CNT_ZT is 118.0 mm BELOW the sill, so the sill edge occludes the slab. Visible slab band by camera distance inboard: 0.30 m -> 115.3 mm; 0.40 m -> 56.8 mm; 0.50 m -> 0.0 mm; 0.60 m and back -> 0.0 mm. At half a metre the counter, the brass line and the surface the hands rest on are ALL out of frame, and no tray can cross anything. The stated "bottom sixth" (67.2 mm) needs ~0.39 m. The one number that decides whether the film's spine is in the picture is never stated.

4. THE 19.9 mm IS FROM THE WRONG VIEW (rule 38). `t1_detail.CNT_NOSE_F` was measured on `ref_side.jpg` — an OUTSIDE elevation, 113 columns. The profile in `t1_detail` starts at `z0 = 0.0015`, "crown, just proud of the top", and rolls y 0.0000 -> 0.0070 before falling down the face. From inside and above you see a ~7 mm crown roll at a grazing angle; the 19.9 mm face points away from you and is never visible from the cook's side.

5. FLAT SELF-CONTRADICTION ON THE OCCLUSION PASS. "the shadow the cook's own body throws across the slab is sampled rather than invented" against "no human geometry exists anywhere in this tree" — same paragraph. Sampling it requires a body proxy, i.e. invented geometry published as measured, which is what the end card promises not to do.

6. THE MODEL CARRIES ~1/6 OF THE FRAME. The camera looks out; there is no exterior world in this tree (backdrop is `studio.cyclorama()`). Picture = aperture reveal + a band of at most 67 mm of slab/brass + ~80% void that the hired illustrator must fill with hands, food, paper, change AND a background that exists nowhere. Sold as machine-true line meeting hand-drawn hand; the machine's share is a border and a gold line. "One afternoon in Blender" is accurate because there is almost no Blender in it — and every pixel the model does not carry is a pixel someone is paid to draw.

7. NO ENCODER ON THE BOX. `which ffmpeg` -> nothing. Both stated masters (silent-safe MP4, ProRes) need one. Solvable via `pip install imageio-ffmpeg` + prores_ks, but it is a stated deliverable with no path today.

8. THE COMP SWITCH HAS NEVER BEEN RUN. `T1_ALPHA` is built and guarded in `studio.py` and has never been executed; it is exactly what comps 192 drawings over the locked plate.

9. SOUND IS A CLAIMED REGISTER WITH NO SOURCE AND NO BUDGET. Plancha, bottle cap, coins on the slab. Nobody has recorded them, the bus is not accessible, and neither library SFX nor an owner field-recording at a Tacombi counter is costed.

10. THE FLIPBOOK DOES NOT FLIP. 24 pages at 12 fps = 2.0 seconds, against the ~40-60 pages a flipbook needs to read as motion; and "saddle-glued at the spine" is not flipbook binding — flipbooks are padded/fan-glued on the edge. The physical object, as specced, does not work as the thing it is named.

11. SCHEDULE IS LIGHT AND THE COMP IS UNBUDGETED. 192 clean drawings plus keys, inbetweens and flat colour in 3-4 weeks by one person is tight; 4-6 weeks is realistic. Registration and compositing over the locked plate is extra days nobody has costed.

12. IT IS THE ONLY CONCEPT NEEDING A HIRE, AND IT ADVANCES NO OPEN WORK. $8-15k+ and 4-6 weeks of external dependency, on the piece that by its own honest admission cannot touch the emblem, for an owner whose stated complaint is that revisions do not ship. Declared, to its credit — but it is still the slowest and most expensive slot on the bench.

WHAT SURVIVED THE ATTACK, STATED FAIRLY: the still-plate insight is correct and is the difference between 20 minutes and 16 hours of non-deterministic render; the line pass now EXISTS (`/home/user/combi_render/line_pass.py`, rev 77, with `design_out/loteria_la_rueda.svg` proving the chain); the sill figure 1323.2 mm reproduces EXACTLY off the constants; all four 1:4 first-step figures are arithmetically right; the body IS solidified so the machine-true reveal is real; and the first step is the best gate on the bench.
* WOULD FIX IT: All five substantive fixes are desk work, before anyone is hired. None needs a render, a photograph or a purchase.

1. RE-STAGE THE CAMERA AND STOP CALLING IT THE COOK'S EYE. Put it on the aperture's centre axis at 1524.7 mm AG (1013.5 mm above the van floor) and name it for what it is: THE TRANSACTION'S OWN LEVEL — the height of the hole, not of a person. Or commit to the truth I measured and make it the film's idea: the cook's eye is 365 mm ABOVE the head rail, so a standing cook looks DOWN through this hole, and the honest shot is a high-angle onto the slab with the head rail cutting the top of frame. That is a better film than the one written, and it is free.

2. DECLARE THE CAMERA STATION AS A NUMBER, AND PRINT ITS CONSEQUENCE. Fix it at 0.39 m inboard of the flank to land the stated 67.2 mm band, and put the sweep in the concept the way the project puts a ceiling on a measurement: "at 0.30 m, 115.3 mm of slab; at 0.40 m, 56.8 mm; at 0.50 m the counter and the brass line leave the frame entirely." One line, and the piece stops depending on an unstated number.

3. RE-MEASURE THE BRASS FROM THE SHOOTING VIEW, OR RE-LABEL IT. Delete "19.9 mm of gold in elevation" from the description of an interior shot. Either quote the ~7 mm crown roll (`t1_detail`'s profile, y 0.0000 -> 0.0070) as seen from above, with its foreshortening at the declared station, or state plainly that 19.9 mm is the OUTSIDE elevation figure and carries no claim about this frame. Two rulers, named — rule 38.

4. RESOLVE THE OCCLUSION CONTRADICTION IN THE CONCEPT'S OWN FAVOUR. Cut "the shadow the cook's own body throws across the slab is sampled rather than invented." Replace it with what the model can honestly give from inside: the aperture reveal's own self-shadow and the slab's contact occlusion under the nosing roll, both sampled off the mesh. Keep "no human geometry exists anywhere in this tree" — it is the more valuable sentence and it is true.

5. STATE THE MODEL'S SHARE OF THE FRAME, AND EITHER OWN IT OR RAISE IT. At the declared station the model carries the reveal plus ~67 mm of 403 mm — about a sixth — and the rest is void the illustrator fills. Own it: make the void the point, a flat measured ink field, and the piece becomes cheaper and more austere and matches the programme. Or raise it: turn the camera 15-20 degrees down the flank so the reveal, the second bay's pillar and the fringe enter frame, and the machine's share triples for zero extra render.

THEN THE PRODUCTION FIXES:
6. FLIPBOOK: go to 48 or 60 pages (4-5 s at 12 fps) and bind it PADDED / FAN-GLUED ON THE EDGE, not saddle-glued at the spine. Still under $2 at 500.
7. ENCODER: `pip install imageio-ffmpeg` now, and prove the round trip on a 10-frame dummy at 2062 x 1612 (both dimensions are even, so h.264 is clean) before the deliverable list promises a ProRes master.
8. RUN `T1_ALPHA=1` ONCE, THIS WEEK. It has never been executed and it is the whole comp path. A single side render proves it or finds the defect while it is free to find.
9. COST THE SOUND OR CUT IT. Either budget library SFX (a few hundred) or hand the owner one job he can actually do — a phone recording of a plancha, a cap and coins at a Tacombi counter, which needs no access to THIS bus.
10. RE-COST THE HIRE AT 4-6 WEEKS AND ADD A COMP/REGISTRATION LINE. $8-15k is roughly right for the drawing; it is not right for drawing plus registration plus clean-up in three weeks.

AND KEEP THE FIRST STEP EXACTLY AS IT IS — it is the best thing in the concept. But fix items 1-3 BEFORE the card is printed, because a card printed at the wrong viewpoint gets a "yes" to a film that cannot be shot, and one printed with the brass at 19.9 mm promises a gold line the camera will not see. Add one line to the back of the card naming the station and the eye height, so the owner's two-second yes is a yes to the real thing.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**ORIGINALITY — 2/4 — ORIGINALITY: FAILS AT ITS OWN HEADLINE, SURVIVES ON A KERNEL IT NEVER EXECUTES. The concept's stated thesis — "the seam between machine-true and hand-drawn is the film" — is Disney's PAPERMAN (2012) logline and SPIDER-VERSE's (2018) design bible, and after 2018 that seam is the default look of an entire generation of animation. "Nothing in the world moves except hands" is the CINEMAGRAPH, a named and dated 2011 invention. Hands-only food POV with room sound and no music is the single most saturated format in food media (Tasty overhead hands 2015–, the entire street-vendor POV genre, Peaceful Cuisine ASMR, ISLE OF DOGS' sushi sequence, THE BEAR's pass). A Mexican fast-casual brand commissioning a wordless animated short with sound design and no dialogue is CHIPOTLE's owned ground three times over (Back to the Start 2011, The Scarecrow 2013, A Love Story 2016) — that is the comparison every person in food branding will make in the first sentence. And the title is the most-printed sentence in Mexican retail: it is on receipts, plastic bags and taquería walls, it is the Mexican "Thank You Have A Nice Day" bag, and it is already a decade-old merch cliché. Four of the concept's own claimed registers are borrowed from genres that own them outright. It ALSO fails the second charge: strip the hands and the rendered plate is exactly the programme's existing flat-measured-line look — a rounded rectangle, a slab edge, a gold line — i.e. TRES HUECOS with a person behind it; the only new material is subcontracted to a hired illustrator, as its own MODEL paragraph concedes ("THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES NOTHING TO THE HANDS"). One thing in it is genuinely unclaimed by anyone, anywhere: the seam is EPISTEMIC, not stylistic — machine draws what was measured, hand draws what was not. Nobody has done that. The concept states it as a production fact and then makes it invisible.**

* ATTACKS: == 1. THE HEADLINE CLAIM IS TWO FAMOUS FILMS' THESIS STATEMENTS ==
"The seam between a machine-true aperture and a hand-drawn hand is visible in every single frame. That seam is the film." That is PAPERMAN (Disney, 2012, dir. John Kahrs) — a short whose entire reason for existing was hand-drawn line laid over CG so the two would be visible against each other, and which won an Academy Award for exactly that. It is then SPIDER-MAN: INTO THE SPIDER-VERSE (2018), which industrialised the visible seam — hand-drawn line over CG, deliberately on twos, deliberately refusing to blend — and after which the technique became the house style of ARCANE, PUSS IN BOOTS: THE LAST WISH, MITCHELLS VS THE MACHINES and TMNT: MUTANT MAYHEM. "On twos" as a legible authorial mark is now so common it is a joke in the animation trade. The concept proposes as its central novelty the most-copied production decision of the last eight years.

== 2. "NOTHING MOVES EXCEPT HANDS" IS A NAMED, DATED, CREDITED INVENTION ==
The cinemagraph (Jamie Beck and Kevin Burg, 2011): a still frame in which exactly one element loops. The concept's core compositional rule — locked camera, dead world, one moving thing, seamless loop — is the cinemagraph's definition, and cinemagraphs were exhausted as an advertising device by about 2015. Sixteen seconds and a loop point does not change the category.

== 3. THE CAMERA POSITION IS THE MOST OCCUPIED IN FOOD MEDIA ==
Cook's eye, inside, looking out through the service window at hands: this is the native format of the vendor-POV clip, of which there are millions, and it is what a GoPro on a taquero produces by default. Adjacent and equally close: the overhead hands-only cooking video (Tasty/BuzzFeed, 2015–, hundreds of millions of views, no faces, no voice, looping); CHEF'S TABLE's hands; the counter-level framing of MIDNIGHT DINER (Shinya Shokudō); and, most damagingly for a 2026 piece, THE BEAR (2022–), which made "the pass" a mainstream visual vocabulary — a film built on the pass now reads as post-Bear whatever its provenance. In animation the nearest neighbour is ISLE OF DOGS' sushi sequence (2018): locked frame, hands only, machine-precise, no dialogue, and the most-cited "hands making food" animation of the decade. Wes Anderson also owns the locked-off symmetrical rectangular aperture as an authorial signature (the concierge desk, the ticket window, THE FRENCH DISPATCH's hand-drawn inserts cut into live action) — so "locked frame + hand-drawn insert" is doubly his before it is yours.

== 4. FOR THIS BRAND SPECIFICALLY, CHIPOTLE ALREADY DID IT ==
A Mexican fast-casual brand making a wordless, musicless-or-scored, hand-crafted animated short about the honesty of its food and the people who make it: BACK TO THE START (2011), THE SCARECROW (2013), A LOVE STORY (2016). This is the closest prior art in the whole audit because it matches on CATEGORY, not just craft. Señor Tacombi is the same shelf. The first thing any agency, journalist or investor will say is "Chipotle did this, with a bigger budget, fourteen years ago." The concept does not name the comparison and therefore has no answer to it.

== 5. THE TITLE IS FOUND SIGNAGE, AND ALREADY MERCHANDISED TO DEATH ==
"Gracias por su preferencia" is printed on more objects in Mexico than any other sentence: receipts, plastic bags, till rolls, shop shutters, bus placards. Titling the piece with it is not authorship; it is picking up the most common piece of commercial furniture in the country and putting a frame round it. It is the Mexican equivalent of the "THANK YOU HAVE A NICE DAY" bodega bag — a graphic that has itself been re-merchandised into totes, tees and gallery prints for fifteen years, and whose Latinx counterpart ("GRACIAS" bags, rotulismo-lettered thank-you graphics) is a fully worked seam in Latinx design merch. The programme's own ROTULISTA already stakes the found-signwriting register. Worse: the phrase's real habitat is the RECEIPT, and the concept puts a transaction on screen and then leaves the phrase in the title instead of in the object where it actually lives.

== 6. THE AESTHETIC PAIRING HAS A LIVING AUTHOR ==
Measured architectural/mechanical line + flat colour + tiny human figures + deadpan trade-catalogue register, in print AND in motion, is CHRIS WARE's territory — and he took it into animation. Anyone with a design education will read the rendered plate as Ware-adjacent before they read it as "the model." That is not fatal, but it means the plate is not a fresh look; it is a recognised one, and the concept claims it as native.

== 7. THE SECOND CHARGE — OLD AESTHETIC, NEW LABEL — LANDS ==
(a) Delete the hands and what remains is vector line, flat colour, sampled occlusion: the programme's existing house style, showing a rounded rectangle, a slab edge, a gold line and a fringe. That is TRES HUECOS with somebody standing behind it.
(b) Every register the concept claims the programme does not own — motion, hand-drawn line, sound, the hand-off — is delivered by a hired illustrator and a Foley library. The concept is a brief for someone else's originality. Its own MODEL paragraph says so in capitals.
(c) THE MEASUREMENT IS INVISIBLE, which is the programme's chronic disease imported into a medium even less able to show it. 4 px/mm, a 220 px corner, 18.6 % nosing, sill 1323.2 mm: not one is perceptible in a moving image. LA LOTERÍA and THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE at least PRINT their figures. This one hides them and then cites them as its reason to exist. The single visible consequence — a 2062:1612 frame, i.e. ~1.28:1 — reads as "roughly 5:4, an old photo ratio" and is indistinguishable from an arbitrary choice.
(d) Bench collision is severe. On the programme's own 78, this concept's non-hand content is already claimed by: LA VENTANA (which is also a card face in the shortlisted lotería deck, carrying the identical 515.5 × 403.0 / r 55), LA APERTURA, APERTURA, TRES HUECOS, THE PASS, ¿QUÉ HAY ADENTRO?, THE OTHER SIDE OF THE HATCH, THE COUNTER IS THE HORIZON, COUNTER HEIGHT, THE BRASS LINE, TOUCHED, MISE EN PLACE, SERVICE TEMPERATURE. Thirteen titles, verified by grep in DESIGN_PROGRAM_rev76.md §4. The aperture and the counter are the most-worked ground on the bench; this concept adds hands to it.
(e) The flipbook is not a differentiator. Flipbook-as-merch is fully worked. Harmless, but it should not be counted.
(f) "Room sound only, no music, no voice" is not a register the programme is seizing — it is the default of the exact genre it is entering (ASMR cooking, Chef's Table, every vendor POV clip). Claiming it as a fourth unowned register is inflation.

== 8. WHAT IS ACTUALLY ORIGINAL, STATED PRECISELY SO IT ISN'T LOST ==
The authorship split proposed here is EPISTEMIC, and that is unclaimed. Paperman's seam is expressive (warmth against precision). Spider-Verse's is graphic (print artefact against render). Nobody has made the seam a claim about EVIDENCE: this side of the line is what we measured, that side is what we could not, and the edge between them is where the record stops. That is native to seventy-six revisions of this specific project and to nothing else in the world. It is also the only idea here a hired illustrator could not have had.
THE CONCEPT DOES NOT EXECUTE IT. It appears once, as a production note, and is then invisible in the artefact. If the seam is the film, the audience must be able to see which side of it they are on; as written, the only person who will ever know is the animator.

== 9. CEILING ON THIS AUDIT ==
This is a knowledge-based prior-art sweep with no web search run in this session, so it is a lower bound on collisions, not an upper one: I can name the genre-owners with confidence but cannot rule out a closer single piece. Named works and dates are from recall; the bench-collision list is the only part measured (grep, DESIGN_PROGRAM_rev76.md §4, this commit). Treat 1–6 as "these are the comparisons the room will make," not as proof of an identical prior artefact.
* WOULD FIX IT: Seven concrete surgeries. 1–3 are the ones that decide whether this is fresh.

1. MOVE THE CAMERA WHERE ONLY THIS ASSET CAN PUT IT — TRUE ORTHOGRAPHIC. Kill the cook's-eye interior POV: it is the most occupied camera in food media, it collides with four titles on the programme's own bench, and it is a shot a phone makes for free. Shoot the hand-off in the SIDE ORTHOGRAPHIC camera that already fits the vehicle today — dead-on, no perspective, no vanishing point, the aperture a measured rectangle in a measured flank, hands entering from off-frame left and right. No camera on earth can make that image; a 366k-face model with a working ortho cam can. It severs the piece from every live-action citation in §3 in one edit, it removes the DECLARED-POSE eye-height problem entirely (there is no station to declare in ortho), and it turns the model from decoration into the only possible author of the shot. Bonus: it makes the frame-is-the-aperture rule TRUE rather than nominal, because in ortho the aperture's 515.5 × 403.0 is the frame at exact scale with no projection to argue about.

2. MAKE THE EPISTEMIC SEAM THE MECHANISM, NOT A PRODUCTION NOTE. Two devices, both cheap:
   (a) Give the two sides different PHYSICAL inks and let them mis-register by a measured amount — the programme already has the title for it (MISREGISTRO). The machine side sits pixel-locked and dead still; the drawn side judders on twos and floats a stated fraction of a millimetre off plate. The viewer sees the boundary without being told.
   (b) ONE FRAME IN SIXTEEN SECONDS: at the loop point, hold a single frame in which the drawn hand is replaced by its own outline in the 40 % tint and open brackets that THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE already uses — the hand rendered as [UNMEASURED]. That is 1/24th of a second, it costs one drawing, it is unlike anything in §§1–6, and it is the entire idea delivered as an image instead of as a paragraph. Do this and the piece stops being Paperman-with-tacos and becomes the only film in the world whose cut is a statement about evidence.

3. RETITLE OFF THE SPLIT, AND PUT THE FOUND PHRASE WHERE IT IS ACTUALLY FOUND. Drop "Gracias por su preferencia" as the title — it is the most-printed sentence in Mexican retail and a fifteen-year-old merch cliché. Print it where it genuinely lives: tiny, at the foot of a drawn receipt that crosses the brass line in one of the six hand-offs. Cliché becomes citation, and the transaction gets its artefact. Take the title from the seam instead: LO QUE NO MEDIMOS / HASTA AQUÍ MEDIMOS / the flat catalogue register UNMEASURED: TWO HANDS. Any of these is unoccupied; the current one is furniture.

4. REPLACE THE FIRST STEP — IT TESTS THE WRONG THING. "One A5 card, aperture printed true from the model, two hands drawn over it" asks the owner whether he likes hand-drawn line over vector. Paperman answered that yes in 2012 and he will also say yes, and the yes tells you nothing about THIS piece. Print TWO identical A5 cards, differing in one element: card A with the hand fully drawn, card B with the same hand as a bracketed 40 % outline. Ask him which one is the film. One sitting, same cost, and it tests the only thing here the world has not already seen. If he picks A, the concept is a well-made genre piece and should be ranked as one; if he picks B, you have something nobody owns and the budget is justified.

5. ANSWER CHIPOTLE IN THE CONCEPT, OR IT WILL BE ANSWERED FOR YOU. Add one sentence that states the category collision and the difference: Chipotle's shorts are parables about sourcing, made of invented worlds; this is a measured record of one real vehicle in which the drawn part is explicitly the part that could not be measured. If that sentence cannot be written honestly, the concept is a Chipotle short and should be scored as one.

6. RESOLVE SKIN AND MONEY BY COMMISSION, NOT BY TASTE. "Pick one and live with it" is the one place the concept abandons the project's own method. The transaction has a real subject: the cooks. Either the hands are drawn from life from named cooks with a credit and a release — which makes the skin question a portrait rather than a guess, and makes the cash question documentary — or no human hand appears and something else crosses the brass line (a tray alone, pushed from off-frame). The in-between version, an anonymous invented hand in a chosen colour, is precisely the version that becomes the story.

7. STOP COUNTING BORROWED REGISTERS AS OWNED ONES. Cut "sound" and "flipbook" from the claim of what the programme does not own — diegetic-room-tone-only is the default of the genre being entered, and flipbook merch is fully worked. Claim two registers honestly (motion; hand-drawn line against machine line) and the concept reads as rigorous rather than inflated. On this project's own rules that is not a cosmetic edit: an overstated claim of novelty is the same defect as an unwatched figure.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**OWNER-AND-BRAND — 2.5/4 — CONDITIONAL — the register is right and the subject is right, but as specified this is a brand film with none of the brand in it, it is the most expensive thing in the pool, it hires out the face of the brand with no ownership named, and its headline claim is false at HEAD. Everything wrong with it is fixable in a day of editing; nothing wrong with it is fixable after an illustrator is hired.

WHAT IT GETS RIGHT, AND IT IS NOT SMALL. (1) It is the only large concept in this programme with ZERO Volkswagen exposure — no roundel, no T1 silhouette, no profile cut in metal. The rev-77 completeness critic's TOP finding was that "THE ENTIRE MERCHANDISE PROGRAMME IS BUILT ON A THIRD PARTY'S TRADE DRESS AND NOBODY HAS CHECKED"; this concept is the one item that finding does not touch, and it says so honestly instead of claiming credit for it. (2) It is the actual transaction. He runs a business that hands hot food through a hole; the programme had drawn the hole eleven times and never the hand (F331's ruling on audience explicitly left "NO FOOD, NO PEOPLE" OPEN, so this is answering a live gap, not inventing one). (3) Its FIRST SHIPPABLE STEP is the best in the pool: one A5 card, this week, costs the paper, and he says yes or no in two seconds before anyone is paid. Against "we need to finally present something," that is the correct shape.

WHY IT IS NOT A 4 ANYWAY: on the OWNER-AND-BRAND lens the piece is thin exactly where it claims to be strong, and it collides with a ruling he made three weeks ago.**

* ATTACKS: 1. A BRAND FILM WITH NO BRAND MARK IN A SINGLE FRAME — AND THE CONCEPT NEVER NOTICES. The camera is inside the middle bay looking out and "the frame IS the hole." Everything this company has spent 77 revisions reproducing under SPEC 10.10 is on the OUTSIDE of that flank and is therefore not in the picture: `script_L`/`script_R` (the hand-painted "Señor Tacombi"), `calidad_L` (the 100% Calidad burst), the mural lid, the flank paisley, the three `menucard`s, the rear-lid lettering — all in STATE.md's inventory, none in frame. What identifies the brand in sixteen seconds is a rounded rectangle, a gold stripe and a bobble fringe. That is a thousand food windows in New York. Be precise about the rule (rule 34): SPEC 10.10's object is "every PAINTED element on this vehicle," so hands and food are outside its scope and this is NOT a 10.10 violation. But 10.10's stated purpose is — verbatim — "the bar is that the owner recognises HIS OWN VEHICLE," and this film gives him nothing to recognise. He has looked straight at the one wrong thing nine times running. He will look straight at the fact that his bus is not in it.

2. THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES A MASK, A STRIPE AND A FRINGE — AND THE CONCEPT'S OWN JUSTIFYING CLAIM IS IMPOSSIBLE ON THIS TREE. "WHAT THE MODEL CONTRIBUTES — Everything the hands touch" is true and irrelevant; what matters is what the VIEWER sees, and with the aperture as the picture edge that is the bottom sixth (counter slab plus 19.9 mm nosing), the fringe at the top edge, and then invented street and drawn hands for the rest. Worse, the one thing named as unfakeable — "the OCCLUSION PASS, so the shadow the cook's own body throws across the slab is sampled rather than invented" — cannot exist twice over. (a) There is no AO pass: `grep -rlnE "use_pass_(normal|ambient)|ambient_occlusion|cryptomatte" --include=*.py .` returns ONLY `line_pass.py`, and the hit is the docstring QUOTING the owner's sentence, not code; F332's own closing clause says "the SECOND half of the owner's sentence … has no normal or AO pass yet." (b) There is no cook: the concept itself states "no human geometry exists anywhere in this tree," so a body-shadow cannot be sampled from a body that is not there. Against "Why do we keep doing Revs that don't ship geometry," this is a 77-revision measured vehicle reduced to a crop path — and the one claim offered to rebut that is self-contradicting.

3. THE HEADLINE IS FALSE AT HEAD, AND HE HAS ALREADY BEEN SHOWN THE THING THAT FALSIFIES IT. "the owner's own locked style clause executed for the first time in seventy-six revisions" and "once the line pass exists." `git log --oneline` at HEAD: 6d112e5 "SHIP SESSION B — THE LINE PASS EXISTS, AND CARD ZERO IS DRAWN THROUGH IT", b7cc1b7 "F332/F333 — the line pass". `line_pass.py` and `la_rueda.py` are tracked. The rev-77 critic already caught four concepts on this same stale-tree error. This one matters more than the others because primacy IS the pitch, and because F330 records that he was shown the Sheet 3 artefact when he fired F18. Claiming first-ever to the man holding the earlier one is the single fastest way to lose his confidence in the round.

4. IT IS UNASSIGNED UNDER A RULING HE MADE AT REV 77, AND THE ASSIGNMENT IS SITTING RIGHT THERE. F331: "Split it deliberately — kids get one line, adults another," and its licence clause is explicit — "EVERY FUTURE SHORTLIST MUST SAY WHICH LINE EACH ITEM IS IN." This concept says neither. It is in fact TWO objects in two different lines and does not know it: a ProRes master and a 2062x1612 loop are trade/adult; a 90x60 mm one-ink flipbook of a hand giving you a taco, handed across the counter, is the closest thing in the entire 75-concept bench to "spark joy … for children" (F18's own words, the oldest live row in the register). Presenting them as one artefact wastes the children's half.

5. COST AND SEQUENCE ARE INVERTED AGAINST HIS STATED IMPATIENCE. $8–15k, three to four weeks, and — its own words — "the most expensive of my three and the only one that needs a person hired." The cheap, keepable, giveable object (the flipbook) is described as a by-product of the expensive one. That is backwards for a man who has said "we need to finally present something."

6. NOBODY OWNS THE FACE OF THE BRAND. 192 drawings by a hired illustrator become this company's motion identity and, if the flipbook ships, its most-handled physical object. The concept names five risks and IP is not among them. With no written work-for-hire assignment executed before the first pencil stroke, the brand's face is licensed rather than owned, and the illustrator's hand — which IS the style — walks to a competitor intact. This is the one commercial exposure the piece creates that the pool's trade-dress finding does not already cover, and it is free to close.

7. A FOOD-SAFETY DEPICTION NOBODY HAS CHECKED. The film is sixteen seconds of hands, food and — by its own risk 3 — possibly coins. A brand-published loop showing bare-hand contact with ready-to-eat food, or a hand taking cash and then a tray, is the kind of frame that gets screenshotted. I cannot verify NYC Health Code from this tree and will not pretend to; the honest statement is that this is a question for their operator, it is cheap to ask, and it must be asked BEFORE 192 drawings exist, not after.

8. THE END CARD IS THE WRONG REGISTER FOR THIS ARTEFACT, AND IT IS THE PROGRAMME'S TIC. "the end card says UNMEASURED." The rev-77 critic already measured this habit: "CONFESS about thirty times out of seventy-five — a universal risk-absorber printed in 4 pt that changes no design decision." On the Uncertainty Plate, confession IS the design. On a warm sixteen-second film about being handed lunch, closing on a card that tells the viewer the company does not know how tall its own cook is reads as anxiety, not rigour. He will not hand that to anyone.

9. THE TITLE SPENDS SOMEONE ELSE'S WORDS WHILE THE BRAND'S OWN GO UNUSED. "Gracias por su preferencia" is the stock phrase on every tiendita bag and receipt in Mexico — correct register, but generic, and unverified as anything this brand says. Meanwhile SPEC 10.10's own table lists this vehicle's real recorded language — the Señor Tacombi script, "100% Calidad", the menu strips, the rear-lid lettering — reproduced at cost over multiple revisions and used in nothing here.

10. RISK FIVE IS STATED BACKWARDS AND THAT MIS-RANKS THE PIECE. "it will not match the rest of the programme. It will be warm, and the austerity will look colder next to it." For a taqueria, warmth is the spine and the drafting-office register is the sub-brand. The concept treats the correct answer as a liability, which tells you it has ranked itself against the programme rather than against the business.

11. A CEILING WORTH CARRYING, NOT A DEFECT: DEPTH IS ON THE UNMEASURED AXIS. `t1_detail.galley_dressing()`'s own docstring: "DEPTH (Y) IS NOT MEASURABLE … Y is therefore chosen, not measured." The concept puts the camera inside that box and points it along Y. Every recession in frame is on the one axis the source declares chosen. The concept's declared-pose paragraph covers the camera HEIGHT and does not cover this. It does not sink the piece — the frame is the aperture, not the room — but it must be in the ceiling, and there is no interior camera in `studio.py` to shoot it with either: the set is front / hero / hero34r / front34 / side / rear / detail_f / low34 / topdown / playa, and `playa` stands AT the counter looking IN.
* WOULD FIX IT: TEN CONCRETE CHANGES. The first four are free and can be made before this is shown to him; the rest change what gets built.

1. PUT THE BRAND IN THE FRAME, ONCE, AND MAKE IT THE PAYOFF. Do not widen the shot — the locked aperture is the idea. Instead: the LAST beat of the loop, the hand that reaches in holds a wrapped taco or a bag carrying the brand's own reproduced lettering, and it is the only printed thing in sixteen seconds. That is one drawing, not a redesign, it uses `script_gen`/`cal_gen` output the project has already paid for, and it converts "a hole in a bus" into "this hole in THIS bus." Alternatively — and this is the stronger version — hang the fringe and let ONE menu card (`menucard`, three of them exist in STATE.md) swing at the top-left edge of frame, drawn from the model. Either way the film stops being anonymous.

2. RETITLE IT IN THE BRAND'S OWN RECORDED WORDS. Drop the stock phrase. Take the title from what is actually painted on this vehicle — the SPEC 10.10 table names the candidates. If nothing there works as a title, the film has no title card and the flipbook's spine carries the script. Not a generic receipt line.

3. DELETE THE END CARD FROM THE FILM. Move every UNMEASURED confession — the cook's eye height, the camera station, the form of the food, the constructed #482E06 — into the flipbook's colophon, in 6 pt, on the inside back board, where the reader has chosen to ask. The film ends on the hands. The rigour is retained in full and stops being the last thing the audience feels.

4. STRIKE THE TWO FALSE CLAIMS AND RE-STATE WHAT THE MODEL ACTUALLY GIVES. Replace "executed for the first time in seventy-six revisions" with "the second consumer of the line pass shipped at rev 77 (F332), and the first that moves." Replace the occlusion sentence with the truth: THE MODEL GIVES THE CROP PATH, THE COUNTER SECTION, THE 19.9 mm NOSING AND THE FRINGE — a true rounded rectangle nobody eyeballed and a gold line at a measured 18.6 % — AND THE OCCLUSION HALF OF THE OWNER'S CLAUSE DOES NOT EXIST YET AND IS NOT ASSUMED. Then either drop the body-shadow entirely or state it as HAND-DRAWN, since there is no human geometry to sample. A claim he can check and that survives checking is worth more here than a claim that flatters the model.

5. SPLIT IT UNDER F331, EXPLICITLY, AND LEAD WITH THE CHILDREN'S HALF. Write on its face: "CHILDREN'S LINE — the flipbook, free at the counter. ADULT LINE — the loop and the ProRes master." Then invert the build order to match: the FLIPBOOK is the deliverable and the film is what the flipbook becomes if he likes it. Twelve drawings, one ink, 90 x 60 mm, in his hand in a fortnight for a few hundred dollars is a shippable object under his own "present something"; $8–15k of animation is a second decision he takes afterwards, with the flipbook already working.

6. ROUTE THE SKIN QUESTION TO HIM, AS A QUESTION, BEFORE ANYONE IS HIRED. The concept is right that there is no honest abstention and right that it cannot be measured. That makes it a founder's call, not an illustrator's, and this project's own method says how: one crop, one mark, one sentence, MULTIPLE CHOICE, with the reference attached, asked with the question tool. Put three drawn hands on one A5 card beside the aperture and let him choose. Do not let this be settled by whoever is holding the pencil in week three.

7. GET THE WORK-FOR-HIRE ASSIGNMENT SIGNED BEFORE THE FIRST DRAWING. All 192 drawings, the flipbook artwork, and the style, assigned in writing, with the roughs and the clean-up layers delivered as source. Add it to the concept as risk SIX. It costs a form and it is the difference between owning the face of the brand and renting it.

8. ASK THE OPERATOR THE FOOD-HANDLING QUESTION IN WEEK ONE. Two lines: does a hand touch ready-to-eat food on camera, and does money cross the counter in the same shot? Get the answer from someone who runs the counter, then draw. Answering it after 32 drawings exist means redrawing them.

9. ANSWER RISK THREE RATHER THAN OFFERING HIM THE CHOICE. Money does not cross. The loop is six hand-offs of food only, and the sound carries the coins — the plancha, a bottle cap, coins on the slab is already in the spec. You get the commerce in the ear and keep it out of the eye, the advertisement reading disappears, and the fantasy reading is closed by the sound. One decision, taken, instead of a risk handed back to him.

10. RE-RANK RISK FIVE AS A FINDING. Rewrite it: THIS PIECE IS WARM AND THE REST OF THE PROGRAMME IS COLD, AND FOR A TAQUERIA THAT IS THE RIGHT WAY ROUND — this sets the register and the drafting-office line becomes the specialist sub-brand. State it that way and let him rule on it. If he agrees, it re-ranks half the bench; if he disagrees, you have learned something worth more than the film.

AND ONE THING NOT TO TOUCH. The seam between a machine-true aperture and a hand-drawn hand is the whole idea and it is a good one. Do not sand it, do not "unify the style," and do not let anyone render the hands. The danger in this piece — a real hand, a real colour, real food, and no measurement to hide behind — is the only thing in seventy-seven revisions that is about the business rather than about the vehicle. Keep it, and fix everything around it.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**DANGER — 2/4 — THE DANGER IS NAMED, QUARANTINED IN A "STATED RISK" BLOCK, AND ABSENT FROM THE ARTEFACT. This is the hottest concept in the bench — the only one that puts a human being in the frame, and it stands the viewer in the WORKER's eye socket rather than the customer's — but it makes no commitment on any of the four things it correctly identifies as dangerous. It names the skin problem and does not choose a colour. It names the money fork and writes "pick one and live with it" — i.e. does not pick. It names the food problem and defers it to a hire. Its FIFTH stated risk is that the piece will be WARM. When a concept's own risk register ends with "it might be too likeable", the sanding has already happened and the label is what is left. Two live wires survive in the text and neither is deliberate: the project has 76 revisions of measurement of a BUS and zero of a PERSON, and the loop rate is one hand-off every 2.67 seconds, forever, seen from inside the hole. Score 2, not 1, because the raw material is genuinely hot and one of its claims is dangerous by accident; not 3, because every decision that would cost the author something has been routed to production.**

* ATTACKS: 1. THE SKIN DECISION IS DECLARED UNAVOIDABLE AND THEN AVOIDED. "There is no honest way to abstain — a grey hand is a decision too, and a worse one." Correct, and then the concept abstains. The ink list is RED #C43124, CREAM #CED0C8, GOLD #EFC245, strip yellow #AC9011, PINK #CE767C, GREEN #608036. SIX INKS AND NOT ONE OF THEM CAN DRAW A HAND. So either the hands are drawn in the vignette PINK — a Mexican brand's cooks rendered in the pink used for a garnish, which is a decision with enormous freight — or a seventh ink exists and the concept does not name it, does not sample it, does not say who chose it. The piece's entire discipline is that every colour is measured off this vehicle and labelled when it is not (#482E06 is labelled CONSTRUCTED). The one colour that cannot be measured is the one it declines to print.

2. THE MODEL SECTION CONTAINS A CONTRADICTION THAT IS ALSO THE BEST IDEA IN THE PIECE. It promises "the OCCLUSION PASS, so the shadow the cook's own body throws across the slab is sampled rather than invented" and then, four lines later, "no human geometry exists anywhere in this tree". Checked against the inventory: 229 mesh objects — seat_back, seat_base, seat_welt, pedal_a, furniture with no occupant — and no torso, arm, hand or figure anywhere. You cannot sample an occlusion off a body you have not built. As written the claim is FALSE and that shadow is invented, which is precisely the eyeballing the concept exists to refuse.

3. THE CAMERA IS THE MOST POLITICAL MOVE IN THE WHOLE 78-CONCEPT BENCH AND THE CONCEPT DOES NOT KNOW IT. Locking the lens at the cook's eye sells the brand by making the buyer stand where the labourer stands. Then: six hand-offs in sixteen seconds — 2.67 s each — looping without end. That is a service rate no human sustains and a shift that never closes. Either that is the point, and the title curdles against it, or it is a GIF convention. The concept says "and it loops" — a formal shrug. It has built a treadmill and filed it as a rhythm.

4. THE TITLE IS THE SHARPEST OBJECT HERE AND IT IS SPENT AS A SIGN-OFF. "Gracias por su preferencia" is receipt-bottom boilerplate, the hollow institutional courtesy a business prints at you. Laid over one worker's hands repeating forever it turns. The concept treats it as warmth.

5. SIX IDENTICAL CLEAN HAND-OFFS IS A MACHINE, NOT A PERSON. Nothing is dropped, nothing is wrong, nothing waits, nothing drips, no order comes back. The concept admits nobody here has ever drawn food and then specifies "a tray" — the most sanitised possible object. Grease is in PAPEL DE GRASA and absent from the one concept that actually serves the food.

6. HANDS-ONLY IS THE ERASURE THE PIECE CLAIMS TO CORRECT. "The programme has drawn the hole eleven times and never once the hand." True and good — but a hand with no wrist, no sleeve, no burn, no ring, no taped finger, and no repeated identity across the six cycles is not a person either. It is a pair of gloves. The piece ends one step from complicity in the thing it diagnoses and does not take the step away.

7. THE MONEY FORK IS THE CONCEPT'S OWN TEST AND IT FAILS IT. "If a hand takes cash it reads as an advertisement; if no money crosses it reads as fantasy — pick one and live with it." Meanwhile the sound design already carries "coins on the slab". So money DOES cross, in the audio, unseen. That is not a choice; that is having it both ways.

8. THE FIRST STEP DE-RISKS THE SAFE HALF. An A5 card of two hands crossing the brass line, shown for a two-second yes/no, tests COMPOSITION. He will not veto composition. He would veto a skin colour, a body in his tree, a price on screen, a dropped lid. The one gate before hiring has been aimed away from everything that is actually at stake.

9. CREDIT WHERE IT SURVIVES, BECAUSE IT DOES: the seam premise is real and genuinely uncomfortable — a vehicle measured to a tenth of a millimetre beside a human being no frame in the reference set gives a stature for. And the FOURTH stated risk is the only fully honest line in the block: the roundel is in no frame, this cannot advance the emblem, F191 is untouched, and it says so instead of pretending. That is the standard the other four risks should have met.
* WOULD FIX IT: 1. PRINT THE SKIN INK, IN THE CONCEPT, NOW. One flat brown, hex named, on the end card in the same row as #482E06 and with the same label: CONSTRUCTED — UNMEASURED. Add one line to the card: it is the only colour in the film that came from a person's decision rather than from this vehicle, and name who decided it. That turns the abstention into the thesis instead of a production ticket.

2. BUILD THE COOK PROXY AND SHIP IT AS GEOMETRY. A blocked-in torso-and-forearm volume in the tree at the declared eye height, so the shadow on the slab is genuinely sampled. It is the project's FIRST human geometry in 76 revisions, it is unmeasured by construction and says so, and it discharges rule 55 in the same edit. Then the seam is IN THE RENDER — a measured machine and a guessed body sharing one occlusion pass — not in a caption. If the proxy is refused, STRIKE the occlusion claim from MODEL; as written it is false.

3. DECIDE THE LOOP AND WRITE IT DOWN. State it: the shift does not end. 2.67 s a customer, sixteen seconds that never close, seen from where the cook stands, and let "Gracias por su preferencia" land on top of that. If the brand will not carry it, slow to two hand-offs and admit in the concept that it is now a warm brand loop and the charge was traded away. Do not keep both.

4. BREAK ONE OF THE SIX. Five clean, one wrong: a dropped lid, an order handed back, a hand withdrawn, or four seconds of nothing coming. About 32 drawings, no new cost, and it is the entire difference between a person and a mechanism.

5. SHOW THE MONEY, COUNTED. Coins cross the brass line drawn to a real denomination, or a price holds for one frame. If the client vetoes a visible price, that veto is the finding and belongs in the concept.

6. GIVE THE HANDS ONE IDENTIFYING MARK, REPEATED ACROSS ALL SIX CYCLES — a burn inside the wrist, a taped finger, one ring. It must be the same person for the full sixteen seconds. Anonymous hands are the erasure this piece was written to end.

7. DELETE THE FIFTH STATED RISK. "It will be warm and the austerity will look colder next to it" is a compliment in a risk's coat, and listing it is how the concept persuaded itself it had been brave. Replace it with the real fifth risk: the camera puts the customer inside the worker's body, and a brand may not want to be reminded what it is selling.

8. RE-AIM THE A5 CARD so it carries the decided skin ink, the identifying mark, and the one wrong hand-off. Two seconds on THAT is worth the fortnight; two seconds on a well-composed pair of gloves is not.
* **DANGER INTACT: False**

### BUENAS NOCHES, COMBI — audit mean 1.88/4

**FEASIBILITY — 3/4 — MAKEABLE, AND CHEAPER ON THE MACHINE THAN IT CLAIMS — BUT ITS COST IS 2-5x LOW, ITS SCALE IS DERIVED FROM THE WRONG QUANTITY, IT NEEDS ONE CAPABILITY NOBODY HAS BUILT (FLAT COLOUR SEPARATION), AND IT SILENTLY BREAKS A MEASURED DIE-CUT RULE ALREADY IN THIS TREE.

This is one of the two or three most physically makeable things on the 78-title bench. Nothing here needs a photographer, the bus, a factory minimum, or a new machine. But three of its five load-bearing numbers do not survive contact: the print cost, the ink count, and the 200 mm scale that the concept says is "decided by the phosphor, not by taste."

WHAT I VERIFIED AND IT HELD. The lamp figure is right and I watched it print: t1_detail.BULB_X0=T._aft(-1.8000)=-1.6368, BULB_X1=1.7000, BULB_PITCH=0.0286 gives n=117, so 118 lamps at a realised 28.5197 mm. The concept's 118 and 28.52 are correct — and note they REFUTE the task brief's own "115 festoon lamps." Every scale figure recomputes: 4065/200=20.325; 28.52/20.325=1.403 mm; 515.5/20.325=25.36 and 403.0/20.325=19.83, r=2.71; 86.9/20.325=4.28; at 90 mm the pitch is 0.631. All watched.

WHAT MAKES IT CHEAP ON THIS MACHINE, WHICH THE CONCEPT UNDERSELLS. The task brief you were given is a rev-76 snapshot and is now stale in the concept's favour. (a) THE LINE PASS EXISTS: /home/user/combi_render/line_pass.py, 278 lines, shipped rev 77 as F332, and `bpy.ops.wm.grease_pencil_export_svg` is in it under `--svg` and was watched writing 3.46 MB. (b) THE CUT PATH EXISTS TWICE: /home/user/combi_render/trace_outline.py's `trace(mask)` returns "list of closed contours ... outermost boundary of each connected component" with a selftest on shapes "whose area and perimeter are known BY CONSTRUCTION"; and F161 records deliver.py's `trimmed/` output as "the vehicle cropped tight to its own alpha, on transparency" — so T1_ALPHA has been run, contrary to the brief. (c) THE SHUT POSE IS ONE EXISTING SWITCH PLUS A LIST: t1_shell.py's `T1_REAR_OPEN=0` prints "lateral, **SHUT**" and the ablation table reads "0 fail (HONEST close)"; counter, tail_board, tail_board_stay, tb_bulbs, tb_bulbflex are separately named objects in STATE.md and line_pass.py already has an OBJECT route over a duplicated-and-joined copy, so "shut for the night" is an exclusion list, not new geometry. (d) RENDER COST IS ~NIL: one side ortho plus one alpha, ~10 minutes total, on the ONE camera that is not clipped. Compare TWENTY-FOUR SECTIONS or anything needing front/rear elevations. (e) /home/user/combi_render/sheet.py + la_rueda.py already emit print-ready mm-unit art at 300 dpi, so the output chain is not hypothetical.

SO THE HONEST MACHINE COST IS ~1-2 DAYS, NOT A REVISION — for everything except the one thing nobody has built.**

* ATTACKS: 1. THE SCALE IS DERIVED FROM THE WRONG QUANTITY, AND THE SCALE IS THE CONCEPT'S SPINE. It says "Scale is decided by the phosphor" and then reasons entirely from PITCH: 0.63 mm bad, 1.40 mm good. Bridging is governed by the GAP, not the pitch. t1_detail.BULB_R=0.0110 m is a radius, so a lamp is 22.0 mm across; at 1:20.3 that is a 1.083 mm dot on a 1.403 mm pitch — a 0.32 mm GAP. A thick strontium-aluminate screen deposit (150-300 um wet, coarse mesh, needing volume to charge) does not hold a 0.32 mm gap. The row will flow into a continuous glowing rail. The arithmetic is right; the criterion is wrong, and the number the whole artefact is sized by moves when you fix it.

2. THE COST IS 2-5x LOW AND OMITS REGISTRATION ENTIRELY. "A few hundred dollars for the die and a short run" does not survive: 4-6 screens at $30-60; a custom kiss-cut steel-rule die at $150-350 (rotary magnetic $400-900); press setup and registration $150-400; specialty glow ink at MOQ; and shops that combine multi-colour screen + phosphor + custom die typically hold MOQ 250-500. A realistic first run is $900-2,000 with 3-5 weeks lead and 1-2 proof rounds at $150-300 each. Worse, the concept never states a registration tolerance: a manual screen press holds +/-0.25-0.5 mm, which against a 1.40 mm pitch is up to HALF A PITCH of misregister between the pearl bulbs and the glow bulbs. This is the one project on this bench where PAPEL DE GRASA is explicitly about back-to-front register, and this concept has a tighter register problem and does not mention it.

3. THE INK COUNT IS UNDERSTATED AGAINST THIS PROJECT'S OWN MEASURED SEPARATION. /home/user/combi_render/AUDIT_rev43.md sec.5 — the full art-direction spec for THIS EXACT ARTEFACT, which F18 says "already exists and is NOT owed" and which the concept never cites — carries a severity-3 finding: "COLOUR SEPARATION — the whole vehicle is ONE 70 deg hue wedge plus four neutrals. Seven inks printed; five if cut." The concept proposes RED + CREAM + warm dark + phosphor = four. A shut SIDE elevation still shows the folk-art flank triptych, the Senor Tacombi script and the measured menu yellow (172,144,17). Three colours either loses them or is a fifth-through-seventh pass nobody costed. Two more screens, two more registration steps.

4. "KISS-CUT TIGHT TO THE SHUT SILHOUETTE" BREAKS A MEASURED RULE ALREADY IN THE RECORD. Same file, severity 4: "DIE-CUT OUTLINE — bridge the underbody with the cast shadow, and let no feature narrower than 0.159 m touch the cut line." At 1:20.3 that floor is 7.8 mm. The drip rail, the 1.08 mm lamps, the mirrors and the handles all sit far under it and the concept routes the cut straight through them. They will shear off the backing or tear in the hand of the five-year-old the concept is for. The rule exists, it is measured, it is in the sticker's own spec, and this concept neither cites nor obeys it.

5. FLAT COLOUR IS THE ONE CAPABILITY THAT GENUINELY DOES NOT EXIST, AND THE CONCEPT ASSUMES IT. F332 built LINE. Nothing in this tree produces closed, fillable colour REGIONS: no cryptomatte, no material-ID pass. line_pass.py emits open strokes — F332's own measurement is 2845 strokes / 19471 points for the SCENE arm, and F333 records that any edge on a surface near-tangent to the view fragments into tens of arcs with "no chaining setting" repairing it. A side elevation's silhouette is exactly that tangent case at roof, rocker and both arches. So the sentence "a closed cut path is what a cutter wants and what Grease Pencil Line Art emits" is FALSE AS MEASURED — line art emits neither closed paths nor fills. It is recoverable (trace_outline + an ID render via the existing T1_CLAY / T1_BAREMAT flat-material switches), but that is the concept's real schedule item and it is unnamed: call it 3-6 days, one revision, not an art step.

6. THE VIEWPOINT SILENTLY DEPARTS FROM THE ONLY SPEC THIS ARTEFACT HAS. AUDIT_rev43.md sec.5, severity 5: "VIEWPOINT — 18 deg front three-quarter from the serving side, eye height 1.55 m." The concept is a 0 deg side elevation and does not say so. The departure HELPS feasibility (side ortho is the one camera that fits; a 3/4 re-arms the roundel and F191) — which is exactly why it must be declared rather than arrived at.

7. THE BADGE LEGIBILITY CLAIM IS AN UNVERIFIED SCALE EXTRAPOLATION. F333's observation that the mark "traces cleanly and legibly in line" was made on la_rueda.py's card, where ECX/ECY/ER = W/2, 48.0, 26.0 on a 90x140 mm sheet — the tyre at 52 mm for a measured 664.9 mm, i.e. 1:12.8, putting the badge at ~6.8 mm, digital, one ink, 300 dpi. The sticker asks for 4.28 mm through a screen. That is 0.63x linear on a different process. The concept is honest that we do not know the mark is RIGHT; it is not honest that we do not know it is LEGIBLE at that size. And an illegible smudge of the mark he has rejected nine times, four times over, on his kid-facing deliverable, is a bad place to find out.

8. MINOR, BUT IT IS AN HONESTY GAP AGAINST ITS OWN RISK 2. The daylight description says "three pale glazed panels" and "dull pearl bulbs." Its own risk 2 correctly says the phosphor is chalky and cannot be invisible. Glow stock and glow ink are pale yellow-GREEN in daylight, not pearl. Write the daylight state as what it will be.

WHAT DOES NOT WORRY ME, AND SHOULD NOT WORRY YOU. No photographer. No bus. No factory minimum. No new machine. No dependence on the clipped front or rear camera. No dependence on photoreal fidelity, gloss (0.412 against a 0.60 bar) or the emblem holds — F191 and F193 gate the photoreal render and a line-and-flat-colour vinyl object does not touch what they gate, exactly as F330 states. And the FIRST STEP is the best on this entire bench: a home printer, a scalpel, a fridge and a scrap of off-the-shelf glow vinyl is genuinely zero cost, genuinely this week, and it puts both states in his hand. An owner who asks "why do we keep doing Revs that don't ship geometry" gets a physical object he can hold, from a project that has shipped none.
* WOULD FIX IT: SEVEN CONCRETE CHANGES. None is a redesign; every one is a correction.

1. SWITCH THE PROCESS: GLOW VINYL AS SUBSTRATE, DIGITAL PRINT, PLOTTER CUT — NOT SCREEN + DIE. Print white underbase + colours by UV/eco-solvent ONTO glow stock and knock out the 118 lamps, the three apertures and the galley strip so the substrate shows through. This kills all four of the process problems at once: registration becomes INHERENT (the glow cannot mis-register against itself, because it is not a pass), tooling goes to ZERO (the cut path is the SVG the machine already emits — no die), MOQ drops to 25-50, and cost drops to roughly $120-300 for 50-100 units in 5-10 business days. The honest trade you must state: a printed glow-vinyl substrate carries less phosphor than a thick screen deposit, so it is dimmer and its afterglow is shorter. Test that on the scrap strip in the first step and let the measurement decide.

2. RE-DERIVE THE SCALE FROM THE GAP, NOT THE PITCH — AND SAY WHAT YOU DECIDE. Print the number: at 200 mm the lamp is 1.083 mm across (BULB_R 0.0110 m, doubled, over 20.325) on a 1.403 mm pitch, so the GAP is 0.320 mm. Then pick one branch in the open: (a) hold discrete lamps and go to ~280-300 mm, where the gap reaches ~0.45-0.48 mm; or (b) KEEP 200 mm and accept a continuous glowing rail, which is arguably truer to the object — probe_rev71_bulbs.py's own point is that a lit festoon is what no photoreal frame ever produced, and a lit festoon at any real viewing distance reads as a LINE, not as beads. Branch (b) is my recommendation and it costs nothing. Either way, the sentence "scale is decided by the phosphor" must be backed by the quantity that actually decides it.

3. SEPARATE THE CUT PATH FROM THE DRAWING, AND OBEY THE 7.8 mm FLOOR. The cut path is NOT the line pass. Render the shut side elevation with T1_ALPHA=1, threshold the alpha, and run trace_outline.trace() — it already returns closed contours and has a construction-checked selftest. Then apply AUDIT_rev43.md sec.5's measured rule at this scale: no feature narrower than 0.159 m (= 7.8 mm at 1:20.3) touches the cut line. In practice: dilate/simplify the outline so the drip rail, lamps, mirrors and handles are DRAWN inside a robust silhouette rather than cut around, and bridge the underbody as the spec says. Print the achieved minimum cut feature in mm beside the artwork.

4. BUILD THE FLAT-COLOUR SEPARATION AS A NAMED ITEM, AND COST IT AS A REVISION. Assign flat emission materials per colour class (the T1_CLAY / T1_BAREMAT switches are the existing hook), render one ID pass per ink on the same side ortho camera, threshold each into a mask, and run the same trace_outline over each to get closed fill regions. Check the separation against AUDIT_rev43.md sec.5's measured "seven inks; five if cut" rather than asserting three, and re-project its sacrifice rule ("48 of the flank's 66 gold components ... every one lands under 0.40 mm. Delete all 48") at 1:20.3, since that figure was computed at a different sticker scale and its scale is not stated in the finding.

5. DECLARE THE VIEWPOINT DEPARTURE ON THE FACE OF THE PROPOSAL. Say: AUDIT_rev43.md sec.5 specifies an 18 deg front three-quarter at 1.55 m eye height; this is a 0 deg side elevation; the reason is that the side orthographic camera fits and the front clips, and that a side elevation puts the nose roundel edge-on and therefore does not re-arm F191. That is a good reason. It stops being a defect the moment it is written down.

6. TURN RISK 1 FROM A BLOCKER INTO THE QUESTION THAT IS NOW DUE. F18's scene is locked as "nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado" — but F18 also carries his own words, "Leave it open, I'll decide when the sticker is actually being built", and F330 fired that trigger at rev 77. So the papel-picado/sun question is not a departure to apologise for; it is a question that became askable last revision. Put it to him as multiple choice with the daylight proof attached, per CLAUDE.md's asking procedure: sun and papel picado as printed; papel picado only; neither, because the bus is asleep; or both, with the sun as the thing that has SET. One crop, one sentence, the question tool.

7. STATE THE BADGE'S CEILING AT THE RIGHT SCALE AND TEST IT FIRST. F333's legibility observation is at ~6.8 mm on a 1:12.8 digital card (la_rueda.py, ER 26.0 on a 90x140 sheet); this asks 4.28 mm through a print process. Put four badges at 4.28 mm on the step-1 home print, look at them with a loupe, and print what you see. If they close up, draw the hubcap badge as a filled disc with a knocked-out mark at the largest weight that holds 0.15 mm — sec.5's own line-weight floor — and say on the backing card that the badge is drawn to a floor, not to the mesh. That keeps the concept's best sentence ("so it will draw, and we do not know it is right") true instead of half-true.

WHAT I WOULD DO THIS WEEK, IN ORDER. Day 1: side-elevation line pass with T1_REAR_OPEN=0 and the counter/tail_board/tb_* exclusion list, plus the alpha silhouette through trace_outline. Day 2: hand-fill three colours in Inkscape over the traced line as a THROWAWAY, print at 200 mm, scalpel it out, glow scrap alongside, fridge. Ask him. Only if he says keep it do you spend the revision on item 4, which is the only real engineering in the whole proposal.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**FEASIBILITY — 2/4 — BUILDABLE, AND IT DODGES EVERY CLASSIC FEASIBILITY KILLER — no photographer, no bus access, no factory minimum, no capability nobody has built. Its arithmetic is exact: I reproduced 118 lamps, 28.5197 mm realised pitch, 1.403/0.631 mm, aperture 25.36 x 19.83 r 2.71, badge 4.28 mm, and emit=(1.000, 0.918, 0.790) at GAL_LUM 3.04 off t1_detail.py and t1_core.py. That is the "watched it print" standard, met. BUT THREE OF ITS FOUR LOAD-BEARING TECHNICAL CLAIMS ARE FALSE ON THIS TREE, and the production route is the wrong one, mis-costed by 3-5x. The scale decision — the concept's proudest move — is derived from the wrong number. None of this is fatal; all of it is one revision's work. As specified it would not survive contact with a printer or a cutter. As fixed below it ships in ~5 days and satisfies rule 55 with real geometry.**

* ATTACKS: 1. THE SCALE IS SET BY THE WRONG NUMBER, AND THE FAILURE IT GUARDS AGAINST HAPPENS ANYWAY AT 200 mm. The concept refuses 90 mm because pitch lands at 0.63 mm and "a glow deposit -- thick, coarse -- will bridge into a smear." The quantity that governs bridging is not pitch, it is the GAP between adjacent deposits. Computed and watched: BULB_R = 0.0110 (22.0 mm lamp) on a 28.5197 mm pitch leaves 6.52 mm of air; at 1:20.33 that is a lamp 1.082 mm wide with a GAP OF 0.321 mm. Screen-printed strontium aluminate wants a coarse mesh and a thick film, and dot gain on gloss vinyl runs 0.1-0.3 mm per edge. Two edges of gain close a 0.321 mm gap completely. So at 200 mm, screen-printed, the drip rail reads as ONE CONTINUOUS GLOWING LINE, not 118 bulbs -- the exact failure 90 mm was refused for. The headline artefact promise, "all 118 bulbs come on," does not survive its own chosen process at its own chosen size.

2. "A CLOSED CUT PATH IS WHAT GREASE PENCIL LINE ART EMITS" IS FALSE ON THIS TREE, AND IT IS THE CONCEPT'S STATED JUSTIFICATION FOR EXISTING. line_pass.py contains no closing, joining or union logic -- the word "closed" does not appear in it. It emits open polylines in camera-normalised coordinates. F333 measured the fragmentation directly: one hubcap crease returned 461 -> 337 -> 182 strokes across a chaining sweep, longest continuous run 156 points, and "it still fragments"; use_geometry_space_chain made it worse. A cutter needs ONE closed loop with no self-intersections. The reliable route exists and is not the line pass: deliver.py's alpha trim (F161), marching squares on the alpha channel, one closed contour. But deliver.py REFUSES without T1_ALPHA=1 (its own rule-37 refusal at line 154), and T1_ALPHA=1 HAS NEVER BEEN RUN in 77 revisions. So the concept's technical argument — "this is what F18 has been waiting for since rev 44" — points at the wrong mechanism, and the right one is unexercised.

3. THE SHUT BUS DOES NOT EXIST, CANNOT BE MADE BY FILTERING, AND THE CONCEPT NEVER MENTIONS IT. This is the largest unstated build item. (a) The three serving apertures are OPEN HOLES: SPEC.md, "the THREE stock side windows with the glass removed"; STATE.md, "open serving apertures on +Y: 3". glass_bay0/1/2 exist on _R only — the non-serving flank. There is no glazing, no shutter and no closure on the show side. "Three pale glazed panels where the serving hatches are" is geometry that is not in the model. (b) Nine parts project past the sheet metal and STATE.md names them: counter, counter_nosing, counter_top, tail_board, tail_board_stay, tb_bulbflex, tb_bulbs, tb_edge_dark, tb_edge_red. Length is 4.317 m with them and 4.065 m without. All nine must go. (c) AND YOU CANNOT SIMPLY EXCLUDE THEM: F332 measured that source_type="COLLECTION" is a SILENT NO-OP on this build — the collection arm "returns FEWER points and still draws the WHOLE VEHICLE," caught only by drawing it and looking, when 718 "wheel" strokes rendered a complete bus. The only working filter is duplicate-and-join into one object with source_type="OBJECT". For a wheel that was 11 parts; for a shut bus it is ~200 of 229. (d) And hiding the galley does not shut an aperture, it turns it into a hole through the vehicle — line art will draw the far shell through it. Something must occupy the aperture plane.

4. NO ARBITRARY-POLYGON FILL EXISTS, SO THE FLAT-COLOUR HALF OF THE OWNER'S LOCKED STYLE IS UNBUILT. sheet.py's fill() takes (x, y, w, h) and emits an axis-aligned SVG <rect>; the PNG backend does the same. There is no path fill and no polygon fill. The locked style is "vector line AND FLAT COLOUR, shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset." The line half shipped at rev 77. The colour half — a red flank region, a cream lower body region, three panel regions, as closed vector areas — has no producer anywhere in this tree, and screen separations need exactly that. The concept specifies three colour screens and never says where the colour regions come from.

5. THE COST IS UNDERSTATED 3-5x AND THE PROCESS IS THE WRONG ONE. "A few hundred dollars for the die and a short run" is right about the die (a steel-rule kiss-cut die for a 200 mm irregular silhouette is $150-350) and wrong about everything else. Four screens at $25-60 each plus per-colour setup is $100-240 before a single sticker. Four-colour screen runs carry 250-500 piece minimums. A realistic quote for 250 x 200 mm four-pass including a specialty phosphorescent is $2.50-5.00/unit. All-in first article: $1,000-2,000, not "a few hundred." Worse, four-colour screen registration on vinyl is +/-0.15-0.3 mm, and the glow pass must register to a warm-dark line pass drawing the SAME 1.082 mm lamps — a 28% misregistration on the artefact's hero element.

6. THE UNIT COST CONTRADICTS THE AUDIENCE, AND THE CONCEPT'S OWN RISK LIST MISSES IT. F18's audience is CHILDREN AT THE RESTAURANT — handed out, at volume. F331 splits the programme: free/handed-to-you is the children's line, paid/kept is the adult line. This concept declares itself children's register (F331) and then specifies a $2.50-5.00 four-pass screen-printed object. You cannot hand that to every kid in a taqueria. The concept's three stated risks are the scene departure, the phosphor hue, and the child-preference bet — all real, all honestly retained — but the cost/audience collision is not among them and it is the one that decides whether the thing gets made twice.

7. CHARGING IS A PLACEMENT CONSTRAINT NOBODY STATED. Strontium aluminate needs light in to give light out. Fridge, bedroom wall, restaurant window — fine, charges from room light, useful glow for minutes to an hour, decaying. Laptop lid or water bottle in a bag — dead artefact. The promise "turn the light out and all 118 bulbs come on" is reliably true only for the wall/fridge placement. F18's own words ("something families keep") happen to name that placement, so this survives — but it is a condition on the object, not a property of it, and it belongs on the backing card beside the hubcap-badge disclosure the concept already agreed to print.

WHAT IS GENUINELY EASY, AND THE CONCEPT UNDERSELLS IT. The one orthographic camera that works is exactly the one this needs: studio.py's "side" sits at loc=(0.0, +26.0, 1.52), looking at the +Y SHOW flank — the side with the three apertures and the bulb string on it. bulb_string() is called at default side=1, so the 118 lamps are on that flank and nowhere else. The clipped front and rear cameras are irrelevant here. No 3D export is needed. And no 5-minute render is needed for a line artefact at all — line_pass pins the bake to one frame, and the module's own docstring records that the unpinned spike did 250 frames in 220 s, so one frame is seconds. This concept is one of the very few on the bench that costs the machine almost nothing.
* WOULD FIX IT: 1. CHANGE THE PROCESS: GLOW IS THE SUBSTRATE, NOT THE FOURTH PASS. Print digitally (eco-solvent or UV) onto off-the-shelf glow-in-the-dark vinyl, with the three apertures and the 118 lamps KNOCKED OUT to bare substrate, then contour-cut on a plotter that reads a registration mark. This single change kills five of the seven attacks at once: no die and no screens (tooling goes to zero), no minimum run (order 10), no glow-pass registration error at all because the glow is not a pass, no colour separations needed because a digital printer eats a 600 dpi raster, and the cost falls from $1,000-2,000 to roughly $3-8 each at 10 units and $0.60-1.00 at 500 — which makes the children's-line giveaway economics work and resolves attack 6. Keep the screen-print version as the adult-line object later if the object earns it.

2. FIX THE LAMP SIZE, NOT THE SHEET SIZE, AND KEEP 200 mm. The measured quantity is the PITCH (28.6 +/- 1.0 mm, FFT, twice, blind, and t1_detail.py says so). The lamp DIAMETER is not measured — the same source comment says "Pitch, bulb size and the fact that it is show side only are INFERRED." So draw the lamp as a 0.55-0.65 mm dot on the measured 1.403 mm pitch: gap 0.75-0.85 mm, printable and cuttable by any process, 118 discrete bulbs, and no measured figure violated. State on the backing card that pitch is measured and dot size is a drawing choice. That is this project's own rule 12 applied to a print spec.

3. BUILD THE SHUT STATE AS THREE PLATES AND SHIP IT AS GEOMETRY. Add three flat rounded-rect closures in the aperture plane (515.5 x 403.0, r 55, the figures are already in STATE.md and already equal), behind a T1_BAY_SHUT switch, with a verify row WATCHED FAILING on the open build before you believe it passing (rules 3 and 13). This is maybe 20 lines in t1_shell.py. It is also the answer to "why do we keep doing revs that don't ship geometry" — this concept is the only one on the bench whose print spec REQUIRES new vehicle geometry, and it is 20 lines. Lead with that.

4. GET THE SHUT SELECTION THROUGH F332's KNOWN-GOOD ROUTE, AND DRAW IT BEFORE YOU BELIEVE IT. Duplicate-and-join the shut set into one object and bake with source_type="OBJECT", copying wheel_source()'s existing pattern verbatim. Do NOT use COLLECTION. Spend one experiment first on whether hide_render + SCENE filters correctly — it may, it is untested, and it would save the join. Either way, PRINT THE OBJECT LIST and look at the drawing, because F332's failure mode was a filtered-looking number over an unfiltered window and it took 718 strokes rendering a whole bus to catch it.

5. GET THE CUT PATH FROM ALPHA, NOT FROM LINE ART, AND SAY SO.
