Run T1_ALPHA=1 for the first time in the project's history (it is built and guarded and has never been exercised — that alone is worth a finding), take deliver.py's trimmed output, marching-squares the alpha at the shut state, simplify to a cutter-friendly closed path with no self-intersections, and keep the line pass for the DRAWING, which is what it is good at. Then rewrite the concept's MODEL paragraph: the line pass is the drawing, the alpha is the cut. The claim as written would have sent a context down a route that cannot close a loop.

6. ADD A poly_fill PRIMITIVE TO sheet.py, OR SKIP VECTOR FILLS ENTIRELY. If you go digital (fix 1) you need no vector fills — render flat colour from Blender with emission-only materials at 600 dpi (200 mm = 4724 px, one render, ~5 min, which this machine can spare exactly once) and place the line over it. If you ever go back to screens, sheet.py needs an arbitrary-polygon fill: an SVG <path fill> plus ImageDraw.polygon, about 20 lines, in the same edit as its first use.

7. FIX THE FIRST STEP SO IT TESTS THE ACTUAL BET. As written it prints the DAYLIGHT state only and hands him a scrap of glow vinyl separately — that tests the weaker half. The bet is the two-state reveal. For the same zero cost: print the daylight state on paper AND print the night state (apertures plus the 118-dot lamp line) as white-on-black on a second sheet, and hand him both, plus the vinyl scrap. He then holds the actual proposition in one hand. And test the hue fix on that scrap in the same minute: lay a piece of amber gel or a swipe of amber marker over half the glow vinyl in the dark. Standard SrAl2O4:Eu,Dy glows ~520 nm green and no ink changes that, but an amber overprint filters it warmer at a real brightness cost — that is a measurable trade you can put in front of him this week rather than a risk you carry to production.

8. PUT THE COST AND THE AUDIENCE IN THE SAME SENTENCE WHEN YOU SHOW HIM. "Digitally printed on glow vinyl, contour cut, no tooling: about a dollar each at 500, so it can be handed to a child. Screen-printed with a die: $1,000-2,000 to start and $3 each, so it is a shelf object for adults." That is an F331 question — which line is this in — and it is his to answer, not yours to assume.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**ORIGINALITY — 1.5/4 — DERIVATIVE AT THE CORE MOVE, FRESH AT ONE UNCLAIMED EDGE. The device — a building/vehicle drawn shut and dark, whose windows and lights come on when you turn the light out — is a named, dated, commercially published children's genre roughly 35 years old, and the substrate is an off-the-shelf commodity with its own marketplace search category. The concept presents this as discovered and cites no neighbour. Worse, its majority state is stock: in daylight it deletes the counter, the tail board, the mural, the script and the lit festoon, leaving a generic red-and-cream 1963 T1 — the most merchandised vehicle silhouette in existence. THE SPECIFIC VEHICLE IS ONLY VISIBLE IN THE STATE YOU CANNOT SEE. One genuinely fresh element is present and unclaimed: the glow layer is not "windows at night", it is three measured SERVING APERTURES plus a galley strip — closed shop becoming open shop, which is what a taquería actually does at dusk. The title then contradicts it. "Buenas noches" files the object onto the single most crowded shelf in bilingual children's publishing and reframes commerce as bedtime. It is not the old flat-measured-line aesthetic wholesale — colour, child and time are real departures — but the register leaks back in through the backing card, and one sentence proves it.**

* ATTACKS: ORIGINALITY LENS ONLY. Seven attacks, prior art named.

**1. THE CORE MOVE IS A PUBLISHED GENRE WITH A DATE ON IT.** "Glow in the Dark Spooky House", Joanne Barkan / Rose Mary Berlin, Golden Books, 1990: a building, drawn shut and dark on the page; charge it under a lamp, turn out the light, the windows come on. That is this concept's entire mechanism, aimed at this concept's exact audience (a child, at bedtime), thirty-five years ago, and it was a Golden line, not a one-off — "Wake Up, Night" (Capucilli, glow-in-the-dark lift-the-flap) and "Lights Out, Night's Out" are the same shelf. The concept describes the reveal as if it were derived from the model's lamp stations. It was derived from the model's lamp stations; it was not invented there. A concept that does not name its nearest neighbour cannot claim distance from it.

**2. THE SAME TWO-STATE RHETORIC IS ALSO AN INDUSTRIAL PRINT CATEGORY.** Day/Night backlit film (4C + white barrier + 4C) exists precisely so a sign reads one image unlit and a second, richer image when the light comes on, and it is sold for building and signage work. Different physics, identical device: one artefact, two states, the viewer changes it with a light. The concept's framing — "the artefact is in two states and the viewer changes it by turning off a light" — is a description of an existing commercial product category.

**3. THE SUBSTRATE IS A COMMODITY AND THE SUBJECT IS SATURATED.** "Glow in the dark van" is an Etsy *market category*, not a gap. Every custom sticker house sells die-cut photoluminescent vinyl; the trade literature already gives the exact design guidance this concept re-derives ("heavy inks behave like shutters… reserve open areas or use lighter inks", "unprinted white areas are recommended, glow is strongest where no ink is applied"). Meanwhile the VW T1 side elevation in red-and-cream is plausibly the most reproduced vehicle silhouette on earth — Hot Wheels, tin toys, cookie jars, the Mystery Machine, a thousand van-life decals. Glow vinyl + VW bus + fairy lights is not a concept; it is three search terms that already return results. And a glowing light-string is, after stars and moons, the single most common thing a glow layer is asked to do.

**4. THE DAYLIGHT STATE IS STOCK, AND THE CONCEPT'S OWN COPY IS THE EVIDENCE.** Its description of the always-visible state: "Red flank, cream lower body, three pale glazed panels where the serving hatches are, a row of dull pearl bulbs along the drip rail, nothing projecting past the sheet metal." No Señor Tacombi script. No folk-art mural. No counter. No propped tail board. Those are the features that make this the Señor Tacombi combi and not any 1963 T1, and every one is absent from the state the object occupies ~99% of its life. The distinguishing content lives in a state that requires a charge, a dark room, and lasts under an hour. Cover the phosphor and ask whether the remaining image could be any split-screen bus. It could. That is a structural originality failure, not a tuning problem — and it is invisible to the concept because the concept evaluates the object in its rare state.

**5. IT TAKES A TITLE FROM MEXICAN TRADITION AND NOTHING ELSE.** "BUENAS NOCHES, COMBI" is Spanish-language dressing on an Anglo-American board-book device. *Buenas noches* is the most saturated phrase in bilingual children's publishing (*Buenas noches, Luna* alone). The graphic language proposed — flat red/cream, dark line, die-cut silhouette, glow reveal — owes nothing to any Mexican tradition. And there is a real one sitting unused directly under this idea: the nocturnal *puesto* under a single bare bulb, the *farol*, the tin-and-glass *nicho* built to hold an actual light, Posada's night scenes, the ex-voto's lit interior. The project's own bench already reaches for that territory (AZULEJO 1963, NUNCA VOLVIÓ A CAMINAR). This concept reaches for a Golden Book and puts a Spanish title on it.

**6. THE OLD REGISTER IS NOT GONE — IT MOVED TO THE BACKING CARD, AND ONE SENTENCE CONVICTS IT.** The concept's own words: *"STATE.md still prints 'hubcap badge is SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY'… That sentence goes on the backing card."* A children's sticker whose accompanying card carries an epistemic disclaimer about the provenance of the hubcap badge is the deadpan-catalogue register wearing a child's coat. So is "rim flange ⌀439.6 (sixteen inch, not fifteen)" on an object for a five-year-old. F331 split the lines on purpose; this one is filed children's and is carrying the adult line's furniture. Related: the scale argument. "Scale is decided by the phosphor, not by taste" is the measured-line move applied to a decision that is genuinely about a child's hand, a fridge door and a laptop lid — 200 mm is a very large sticker, and deriving it from lamp pitch rather than from where it goes is the old aesthetic taking a decision it should not own.

**7. ONE FACTUAL CLAIM PUBLISHED WITHOUT A TEST, AND IT IS THE ONE DECLARED UNFIXABLE.** Stated risk 2 ends *"the night bus will glow GREEN, not warm… no ink fixes it."* That is wrong as stated, or at least untested. Standard practice in the pigment trade is to carry strontium aluminate in a transparent or light-coloured resin and shift perceived emission with a translucent tint; formulations that read orange in daylight and emit yellow-gold are sold off the shelf. It costs brightness — that is a real argument — but "no ink fixes it" is a materials impossibility asserted with no swatch, in a project whose rule 5 forbids publishing a figure you have not watched print. The asymmetry is the tell: the concept REFUSED 90 mm on computed arithmetic and ACCEPTED 200 mm on the same arithmetic without ever printing a phosphor pass at 1.40 mm pitch, where a coarse-mesh high-film-build glow deposit is genuinely at its limit.

**CREDIT, SO THIS IS NOT A HATCHET JOB.** The 118 lamps at 28.52 mm realised pitch check out against source — I recomputed them: `t1_detail.BULB_X0 = t1_core._aft(-1.8000)` = −1.6368, X1 = 1.7000, pitch 0.0286 gives n = 117 segments, **118 balls, realised pitch 28.52 mm**. The programme brief's "115 festoon lamps" is the photographic FFT estimate in `t1_detail.py`'s own comment ("~115 bulbs"), a different object from the built count; the concept is more precise than the brief but does not flag that it is quoting a different quantity — cite both or say which. And the fresh element is real: a glow layer whose lit elements are three *serving apertures* and a galley strip means OPEN FOR BUSINESS, not asleep. I cannot find that. It is the only part of this concept I cannot find, and the concept buries it under a goodnight title that says the opposite.

Sources: [Glow in the Dark Spooky House, Golden 1990](https://www.amazon.com/Spooky-Golden-Joanne-Barkan-Hardcover/dp/B010726VVC) · [Wake Up, Night (glow-in-the-dark lift-the-flap)](https://www.amazon.com/Night-Glow-Dark-Lift-Flap/dp/1588650820) · [Lights Out, Night's Out](https://www.amazon.com/Lights-Out-Nights-Glow-dark/dp/1449402364) · [Day/Night backlit printing](https://www.eacgs.com/2025/04/29/day-night-printing-backlit/) · [Day/Night backlit film](https://www.power-graphics.com/printing-for-indoor-use/backlit-graphics/day-night-backlit-film.html) · [Etsy "glow in the dark van" category](https://www.etsy.com/market/glow_in_the_dark_van) · [Glow sticker design guidance — ink as shutter](https://customstickers.com/community/blog/design-mistakes-to-avoid-with-custom-glow-in-the-dark-stickers) · [Glow vinyl material notes](https://www.carstickers.com/sticker-materials/glow-in-the-dark-vinyl/) · [Strontium aluminate in transparent/tinted resin systems](https://www.ispigment.com/blog/how-to-choose-strontium-aluminate-glow-in-the-dark-powder-for-paint-coatings-and-plastics_b181) · [Goodnight-vehicle children's book shelf](https://www.amazon.com/Best-Sellers-Children's-Books-on-Cars,-Trucks-Buses/zgbs/books/3291)
* WOULD FIX IT: Seven concrete moves. The first three are the ones that change the score.

**1. KILL THE GOODNIGHT FRAME. RETITLE TO THE COMMERCE STATE.** The one thing here I cannot find prior art for is *closed shop becomes open shop* — three measured serving apertures and a galley strip lighting up is a taquería opening at dusk, not a bus going to sleep. "Buenas noches" throws that away and files the object on the most crowded shelf in children's publishing. Retitle to the transition: **YA ABRIÓ**, **SE PRENDE**, or **ABIERTO HASTA TARDE**. Then invert the daylight fiction: the shut state is *before service* (siesta, shutters down, the street empty), not bedtime. Identical physics, different genre — and the genre it moves into, the lit night stall, is nearly empty in sticker form and is where the Mexican tradition actually lives.

**2. PUT THE VEHICLE'S IDENTITY IN THE MAJORITY STATE.** Print the "Señor Tacombi" flank script, the folk-art mural panel and the propped tail board in the three daylight colours. The glow layer must add LIGHT, not IDENTITY. Acceptance test, and run it literally: mask the phosphor pass, hand the daylight-only print to someone who does not know the project, and ask what vehicle it is. If the answer is "a VW bus" rather than "that taco bus", it is stock and the concept has not shipped. Right now, by its own written description, it fails that test.

**3. MAKE THE GLOW LAYER DO THE THING GLOW LAYERS DO NOT DO — GLOW FEWER THINGS.** Every glow sticker on earth lights its windows and its fairy lights. Break the reflex: glow ONLY the three apertures and the galley strip, and **leave the 118 lamps in daylight ink so they visibly do not light.** This is truer to the record — `t1_detail.bulb_string`'s own comment reads "Rendered as unlit pearl glass: this project has no emissive material" — it puts the light where the FOOD is, and a festoon that is present and dark while the serving hatches blaze reads unmistakably as a working counter rather than a Christmas decoration. It also solves the phosphor's coarse-deposit problem for free by deleting the 1.40 mm pitch from the glow pass entirely.

**4. PROVE THE 200 mm BEFORE QUOTING IT.** The 90 mm refusal is computed; the 200 mm acceptance is the same arithmetic with no print behind it. Screen one strontium-aluminate swatch at the intended film build, charge it, photograph it dark, and measure whether 1.40 mm features resolve or bridge. If they bridge, the scale chain reopens and 200 mm was taste after all. (If move 3 is taken, this test only has to clear the aperture edges, which is trivial.)

**5. RETRACT OR TEST "NO INK FIXES IT."** Print two more swatches: bare phosphor, and phosphor under a translucent warm tint. Publish the measured brightness loss and the perceived hue. Then either state the trade with its number, or drop the claim. Asserting a materials impossibility with no swatch, in the same document that refuses a scale on computed arithmetic, is the project's own rule 5 broken in the direction that flatters the concept.

**6. GET THE ADULT FURNITURE OFF THE CHILD'S OBJECT.** F331 split the lines deliberately. Move the figures card to the adult line as a separately-sold paired object (that pairing is itself a stronger idea than burying it), and leave the child exactly one number: **1:20.3, and the real length**, so the sticker is a scale model they can hold up against a real bus. That is the only figure a five-year-old can use. "Rim flange ⌀439.6 (sixteen inch, not fifteen)" and the hubcap-provenance disclaimer are the old register with a new label on it, and they should be nowhere near this object.

**7. CITE THE NEIGHBOUR ON THE FACE OF THE CONCEPT.** Add one line naming Golden's *Glow in the Dark Spooky House* (1990) and the day/night backlit print category as the prior art, and state in one sentence what this does that they do not — the apertures, not the windows; open for business, not asleep; a real vehicle's measured lamp stations rather than a drawn house. A concept that names its nearest neighbour and holds ground against it survives an originality audit. One that presents a thirty-five-year-old device as derived from a lamp-pitch calculation does not, and will not survive contact with anyone who owned that book.

**IF ONLY ONE THING IS DONE:** move 1 plus move 3. Retitle to the opening, and glow the apertures while leaving the festoon dark. Those two together convert this from "a glow-in-the-dark VW bus sticker" — which exists, in quantity — into an object about a shop turning its lights on, which I could not find anywhere.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**ORIGINALITY — 1/4 — OCCUPIED. The central conceit is not fresh — it is a bestselling children's object from a major publisher, and the concept collides with it on all four axes at once (title formula, audience, subject, mechanism). Chronicle Books published GOODNIGHT, GOODNIGHT, CONSTRUCTION SITE: GLOW IN THE DARK EDITION (Rinker/Lichtenheld, ISBN 9781452152158) — work vehicles at the end of the day, drawn in flat colour, overprinted with a phosphorescent pass so that "special glow-in-the-dark ink keeps sleepy construction fans company even after the lights go out." That is this concept's engine, prior, for the same five-year-old. And the title is the Spanish edition of the genre's founding text: BUENAS NOCHES, LUNA is Goodnight Moon (1947). "BUENAS NOCHES, COMBI" is the most-copied title formula in children's publishing with the noun swapped for a vehicle — the slot already taken by the franchise above. What survives as this concept's own is the scale derivation and one measured tension. That is craft, not originality.**

* ATTACKS: FIRST, WHAT I CHECKED AND WHAT IS TRUE. The figures are right and better than the brief's. I recomputed the lamp count off live source rather than taking it: t1_detail.py's `BULB_X0, BULB_X1, BULB_PITCH = T._aft(-1.8000), 1.7000, 0.0286`, with `_aft` re-spacing on O_NEW 0.773, gives BULB_X0 -1.63681, span 3.33681 m, n = 117 intervals and `for i in range(n + 1)` = 118 balls at a realised pitch of 28.5197 mm. The concept's 118 and 28.52 are correct; the project brief's "115 festoon lamps" is the stale prose (it traces to t1_detail.py's own comment "~115 bulbs"). The 1:20.3 chain checks: 4065/200 = 20.325, 28.5197/20.325 = 1.403 mm, 515.5/20.325 = 25.36, 403.0/20.325 = 19.83, 55/20.325 = 2.71, 86.9/20.325 = 4.28, and at 90 mm 28.52/45.17 = 0.63. The citation is real too — probe_rev71_bulbs.py line 4 carries "the drip-rail bulb string renders unlit pearl white. In both in-service..." verbatim. I am scoring this a 1 on originality with all of that granted.

(1) THE DIRECT COLLISION, NAMED. Chronicle Books, 2016: GOODNIGHT, GOODNIGHT, CONSTRUCTION SITE — GLOW IN THE DARK EDITION. NYT-bestselling franchise. Working vehicles shutting down for the night, flat-colour vector-ish illustration, phosphorescent overprint, and the interaction is the child turning the light off. Audience: identical. Register: identical (child, bedtime, time-based, two states). Mechanism: identical (a glow pass over a flat-colour vehicle drawing). Emotional promise: identical ("keeps sleepy fans company after the lights go out" vs "the bus you thought was closed is open"). This is not a distant analogue that a designer can argue past; it is the same object with a different vehicle, and it has sold in the millions. https://www.abebooks.com/9781452152158/Goodnight-Construction-Site-Glow-Dark-1452152152/plp

(2) THE TITLE IS A TRANSLATION OF THE THING IT IS COPYING. Buenas noches, Luna is the Spanish Goodnight Moon. "BUENAS NOCHES, [NOUN]" is a formula, not a title — Goodnight iPad, Goodnight Brew, Goodnight Goodnight Construction Site, a thousand parodies. Putting it in Spanish does not make it a Mexican graphic gesture; it makes it the American bedtime-book formula translated. There is no Mexican graphic tradition in this artefact at all: the concept invokes none, and the only Mexican element in the whole family — papel picado — is explicitly absent because it belongs to F18's locked scene, which this concept departs from.

(3) THE SUBSTRATE'S STOCK DEMO IS THIS DESIGN. Glow vinyl is 6 mil off-the-shelf stock (carstickers.com sells it by the sheet), and the print trade's standard way of SELLING the material is exactly this reveal — Prodigi's product copy: "areas defined as pure white or transparent will emit a luminous green-blue glow," gallery print by day, glowing statement at night. "Windows glow when the lights go out" is the material's marketing demo, not an idea applied to it. The concept says the material is off-the-shelf as a COST virtue; it is also an ORIGINALITY debt, and only the second half is unstated. https://www.prodigi.com/products/glow-in-the-dark-prints/ https://www.carstickers.com/sticker-materials/glow-in-the-dark-vinyl/

(4) THE VEHICLE + THE MATERIAL ALREADY SHIP TOGETHER. Etsy carries dense markets for both "vw bus decal" and "car decal glow in the dark"; ifloortape sells 54- and 84-piece glow vinyl decal kits marketed specifically for RVs, motorhomes and campervans. The T1 side elevation is, as this project's own bench admits elsewhere, "the most exhausted graphic object in the field." The intersection of the most exhausted silhouette and a commodity novelty material is where originality goes to die. https://www.etsy.com/market/vw_bus_decal https://ifloortape.com/ifloortape-glow-in-the-dark-vinyl-decal-sticker-kit/glow-var4-54pk/

(5) BRAND-ADJACENCY MAKES IT WORSE, NOT BETTER. Tacombi's name is taco + combi; the 1963 combi IS the logo and the origin story; they run shop.tacombi.com. A die-cut side-elevation combi sticker is not a fresh proposal to this client, it is their default merch SKU — plausibly already extant. So the concept's entire novelty budget must be carried by the glow, which is items 1–4. https://www.tacombi.com/our-story https://shop.tacombi.com/

(6) SECOND LENS — IT IS THE FLAT MEASURED LINE UNDER A NEW LABEL, AND THE LABEL IS F331. It claims CHILDREN'S LINE. Its persuasion is entirely adult: the backing card "carries the figures"; "That sentence goes on the backing card" about STATE.md's self-consistency caveat; three declared risks; a scale defended by phosphor deposit physics. That is the deadpan-catalogue confession register — which rev 77's own completeness critic already faulted as "a universal risk-absorber printed in 4 pt that changes no design decision," running ~30 times in 75 concepts. F331 ruled "kids get one line, adults another; NEITHER PRETENDS TO BE THE OTHER." This one pretends. A five-year-old does not read a tolerance. Strip the card and what remains for the child is a bus sticker whose windows glow.

(7) AND THE DRAWING IS A THIRD PASS AT ONE DRAWING. Side elevation, line + flat colour, three apertures at 515.5 x 403.0 r55, four hubcap badges off la_rueda's radius histogram — that is Card Zero re-scaled. The bench already holds die-cut side-elevation sticker concepts (the f18-sticker family; the 1:33.9 one that cuts the three holes through). Against its own bench-mates this concept's only differentiator is, again, the glow. Within the pool it is a variant, not an entry.

(8) THE ONE FRESH MOVE, CREDITED PRECISELY. "Scale is decided by the phosphor, not by taste" — refusing 90 mm because a coarse glow deposit bridges at 0.63 mm pitch, and letting the material set the artefact's size — is a genuinely material-first derivation and I could not find it as a stated method anywhere in the decal or print-on-demand field. It is real and it is checkable and I checked it. But it answers HOW BIG, not WHAT. It is a manufacturing constraint doing the work of an idea.

(9) THE ONE OTHER THING THAT IS ITS OWN, AND THE CONCEPT BURIES IT. "The render has never managed either" — the festoon renders unlit pearl white in every photoreal frame while both in-service photographs show it lit and warm. That sentence is unique to this project, belongs to nobody else on Earth, and is currently a supporting argument for a device Chronicle Books shipped in 2016. It is the strongest material in the concept and it is in the third paragraph.

(10) A SMALLER ONE. "STATE.md still prints 'hubcap badge is SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY'" is quoted correctly and is a good instinct — but F333 already measured the badge cross-checked to CAP_EMBLEM_D within +0.5 mm, so "we do not know it is right" is weaker than the record now supports, and putting the weaker sentence on the card sells the confession rather than the finding.
* WOULD FIX IT: Five concrete moves. The first alone changes the verdict; it is the one no prior-art search can return.

(A) GLOW THE SIDE THAT WAS NEVER PHOTOGRAPHED. SPEC.md grades the OFF SIDE elevation "E (never photographed)" — an entire flank of this vehicle that no frame in the reference set shows, and which exists only because a 3D asset was built and 77 revisions argued about it. Print the PHOTOGRAPHED near side in the three daylight inks. Put the phosphor on the OFF SIDE. Lights out, and the child is holding the side of the bus that nobody has ever seen, drawn from geometry alone. Chronicle cannot make that object; Etsy cannot make that object; it requires exactly the measured model this programme has and cannot be faked. It also converts the backing card's "we do not know it is right" from a disclaimer into the artefact's subject, which is the only honest way that sentence belongs on a child's toy.

(B) IF IT MUST BE THE LAMPS, MAKE THE GLOW THE DEFECT. The daylight face is what the RENDER can do (pearl white, unlit, probe_rev71_bulbs.py line 4). The night face is what the PHOTOGRAPH shows (lit, warm). The sticker is then the machine confessing the one thing it has failed at for 77 revisions, in a form a child charges by holding it under a lamp. Same physical object, an idea that is the project's own rather than the material's.

(C) KILL THE TITLE. It is the Spanish Goodnight Moon pointed at the vehicle slot a bestseller already holds. Take the title from the vehicle's record instead. "118" is the strongest candidate — a number only this project possesses, correct, watched printing, and meaningless until the light goes out and the child counts them.

(D) MAKE THE PHOSPHOR DO SOMETHING ITS OWN DEMO DOES NOT. Two candidates, both needing a real test before any figure is published: (i) DIFFERENTIAL DECAY — strontium aluminate holds for hours, zinc sulphide for minutes. Print the 118 lamps in the short phosphor and the galley strip in the long one, and the bus CLOSES while the child watches, over ten unpowered minutes, the galley the last thing lit. Time as a printed material property; I cannot find it in the field. (ii) CHARGE-BY-SHADOW — the phosphor only charges where the daylight inks are not opaque, so the night image is the day image's own negative, self-registering. Either one moves the mechanism off the shelf.

(E) OBEY F331 OR CHANGE LINE. Take every figure off the child's object. Either the child gets a plain sticker and the adult buys the plate separately as its own artefact, or this is declared ADULT LINE and stops claiming a five-year-old as its reader. Right now it is an adult confession card with a child's sticker attached.

AND FIX THE FIRST STEP. "Print the daylight state, hand him a scrap of glow vinyl" tests whether he likes a bus sticker — which is not in doubt and is not what is wrong. The question that decides this concept is whether the reveal is anything more than the material's stock demo. Test that: put the daylight print on the table between a printout of the Chronicle glow edition's cover and one Etsy glow campervan decal, and ask him whether ours is a different object or the same object with better tolerances. Zero cost, same afternoon, and if the answer is "same object," (A) is already waiting in the envelope.

Sources: [Goodnight Goodnight Construction Site, Glow in the Dark Edition](https://www.abebooks.com/9781452152158/Goodnight-Construction-Site-Glow-Dark-1452152152/plp) · [Prodigi glow-in-the-dark prints](https://www.prodigi.com/products/glow-in-the-dark-prints/) · [carstickers.com glow vinyl](https://www.carstickers.com/sticker-materials/glow-in-the-dark-vinyl/) · [Etsy VW bus decal](https://www.etsy.com/market/vw_bus_decal) · [ifloortape camper glow decal kit](https://ifloortape.com/ifloortape-glow-in-the-dark-vinyl-decal-sticker-kit/glow-var4-54pk/) · [Tacombi our story](https://www.tacombi.com/our-story) · [Tacombi shop](https://shop.tacombi.com/)
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**OWNER-AND-BRAND — 2/4 — OWNER AND BRAND FIT — 2 of 4. He would not reject it outright, and its arithmetic is honest, but as written it is not a thing he would be proud to hand someone, for three independent brand reasons and one procedural one.

WHAT SURVIVES MY ATTACK, AND I CHECKED IT RATHER THAN ASSUMING IT.
Its colours are real: `t1_mats.RED = sRGB(196,49,36)` is exactly #C43124 and `CREAM = sRGB(206,208,200)` is exactly #CED0C8. Its scale arithmetic reproduces: 4065/200 = 1:20.3, 28.52/20.3 = 1.405 mm, 86.9/20.3 = 4.28 mm. It cites F333 WITH its ceiling ("we do not know it is right") rather than laundering it, which is more discipline than most of this project's own prose. The FIRST STEP is genuinely the best thing in it — a home print, a scalpel, a fridge, this week, zero dollars — and that is the correct answer to "why do we keep doing Revs that don't ship." Glow-in-the-dark is a real, proven child delight and does discharge "spark joy... for children" and "something families keep." I am not scoring 1.

WHY IT IS NOT 3.

1. THE BRAND'S NAME IS NOT ON IT, AND THE CONCEPT NEVER MENTIONS THE PROBLEM. Three inks: RED, CREAM, a warm dark. The flank carries the hand-painted "Señor Tacombi" script, and the owner RULED its colour himself — *"Bright silver, same as Tacombi"* (F157, `script_gen.SENOR_TARNISH = 0.0`), a ruling that overrides SPEC §3's weathered lock for that word specifically. The concept's palette cannot carry silver, and it also has no menu yellow (172,144,17) and no folk-art mural. The described daylight artefact — red flank, cream lower body, three pale panels, a pearl bulb row — is A RED VW BUS. It is not HIS bus. SPEC §10.10 is a standing requirement titled ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ARTWORK, and the one element that makes this vehicle a taqueria rather than a Volkswagen is silently absent. A restaurateur will see that in one second and it is the first thing he will say. Reducing the artwork for a 3-colour screen print is a legitimate design decision; not naming that you are doing it, to the one artefact that carries his business's name, is not.

2. FOR A RESTAURANT, THE DAYLIGHT STATE IS A PICTURE OF BEING CLOSED. "A bus shut for the night... nothing projecting past the sheet metal." The three serving apertures — the thing that makes this vehicle a taqueria, and the one measurement the record calls solid (515.5 × 403.0, r 55, THREE, EQUAL) — are rendered as dead pale glazed panels. The counter is gone, the tail board is down, nobody is being served. In the venue where the sticker is handed over, that is his shop with the shutters down. And the reveal that redeems it requires DARKNESS, which never happens in a lit dining room or a daytime queue: the moment of delight is deferred to a child's bedroom, hours later, out of the brand's sight. The concept's own stated risk (3) circles this and then bets against it without a measurement. My attack is stronger than its own: it is not just that a five-year-old may prefer the open bus — it is that the CLIENT's business is the open bus.

3. THE SCALE BREAKS THE PREMISE OF THE APPROVAL IT IS BUILT ON. F330 records exactly why his *"yes — start it now"* was answerable with the emblem still wrong: **"a die-cut sticker at 60–80 mm cannot resolve the roundel"** — the asker's own reasoning, offered in the option text, and F330's own ceiling flags that he never withdrew F191. This concept ships at 200 mm, 2.5–3.3× that, and then argues affirmatively that the mark he has rejected NINE times will be legible, citing F333's "the VW badge traces cleanly." At 1:20.3 the four hubcap badges are 4.28 mm; at 80 mm they are 1.71 mm. This concept walks the emblem back into visibility on the first artefact built after the trigger fired, and it does so on a phosphor-deposit tolerance rather than on anything the owner said. Note also that the hubcap badge is a DIFFERENT OBJECT from the nose roundel and `STATE.md` still prints "hubcap badge is SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY — CAP_EMBLEM_WFRAC has never been compared to a frame." So the enlargement buys visibility for a mark that is both owner-rejected in its family and ungrounded in its own right.

4. IT MIXES BOTH REGISTERS IN ONE OBJECT, THE REVISION AFTER HE RULED THEM APART. It declares "Children's line (F331)" and then ships a backing card carrying the figures and the sentence "we do not know it is right." F331's chosen option is *"Split it deliberately — kids get one line, adults another"*, and its licence says **neither pretends to be the other**. A five-year-old's giveaway with an epistemic disclaimer on the back is the deadpan-catalogue adult register bleeding into the children's line one revision after he separated them. It also straddles "Sold or given" — F331 makes free/handed-to-you the children's half and paid/kept the adult half; the concept must pick.

5. A SCENE DEPARTURE, REQUESTED AT THE WORST POSSIBLE MOMENT — and this one it DOES declare, honestly, which is why it is item 5 and not item 1. F18's scene is owner-LOCKED: *"nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado."* A bus asleep has no sun. After 33 revisions of deferral he finally said start it, and the first thing back is a request to change his spec plus a backing card he did not ask for. Stating it plainly, as this concept does, is the right handling — but the cost is another approval round trip on a man whose recorded complaint is revisions that ship nothing.

6. IT IGNORES A LOCKED LINE OF HIS OWN SPEC. `NEXT_CONTEXT_PROMPT_rev39.md` §7, inside the F18 brief: *"I like how the wheels were drawn in the earlier cartoon version."* This concept draws the wheels from F333's measured true circles — a measurement route, not the cartoon he named. The one place he volunteered a positive aesthetic preference about this exact artefact, and the concept does not mention it.

7. TWO SMALLER ONES, NAMED SO THEY ARE NOT LOST. (a) THE SHUT POSE DOES NOT EXIST IN THE MODEL. `T1_REAR_OPEN=0` shuts the hatch, but `TB_TILT_DEG` is built at 38.0 with no shut pose, and `STATE.md` prints "open serving apertures on +Y: 3" — the apertures are cut holes with no shutters modelled at all. So the concept's PRIMARY state is a drawing decision the model cannot hold, which undercuts its own claim that "the model supplies the exact... aperture geometry." (b) THE LAMP COUNT IS TWO NUMBERS. 118 is the built count from exact arithmetic on constants (`AUDIT_rev43.md`: "118 lamps, 28.6 mm pitch"); `t1_detail.py`'s own comment records "~115 bulbs" as the PHOTOGRAPHED estimate at 211 px/m, pitch 28.6 ± 1.0. Printing "118" on a backing card next to a record that says ~115 is precisely the transcription defect this project punishes. Say which is which, and I did not watch "28.52 mm realised" print.

8. OPERATIONS, BRIEFLY, BECAUSE HE RUNS A RESTAURANT. Four screen passes plus a kiss-cut die on specialty phosphor vinyl is expensive per unit for a thing handed to children; the same object digitally printed and kiss-cut in one pass costs a fraction. And strontium aluminate needs a real charge — under warm restaurant lighting, in a pocket, on a card, it will underperform the sample he was shown. Neither is fatal; both will be his first two questions.

WOULD IT DAMAGE THE BRAND? Not damage — dilute. A red T1 with no name on it, shown shut, glowing green. The green is the concept's own honest admission (~520 nm against the measured galley warm (1.000, 0.918, 0.790)); the shut and the missing name are not.**

* ATTACKS: 1. THE NAME IS MISSING. Three inks cannot carry the "Señor Tacombi" script, which the owner ruled *"Bright silver, same as Tacombi"* (F157). No menu yellow, no folk art. What is described is a generic red VW T1 under a standing requirement literally titled SPEC §10.10 ABSOLUTE REPLICATION OF ARTWORK. The concept never names the omission.
2. SHUT = CLOSED. For a taqueria, the daylight face is his shop with the shutters down and the three serving apertures dead. The reveal needs darkness, which never occurs in the venue where the artefact is handed over.
3. THE SCALE BREAKS THE APPROVAL. F330 records that his yes was answerable with the emblem wrong because "a die-cut sticker at 60–80 mm cannot resolve the roundel." This ships at 200 mm and argues the four 4.28 mm hubcap VW marks WILL be legible — reopening F191 on a mark rejected nine times, and one `STATE.md` still calls "SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY."
4. IT VIOLATES F331 ONE REVISION AFTER HE RULED IT. Declares itself the children's line, then attaches a figures-and-caveat backing card — the adult deadpan-catalogue register — and hedges "sold or given" across both halves of a split he just made deliberate.
5. SCENE DEPARTURE AT THE WORST MOMENT (declared, to its credit). F18's locked scene is "nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado." First thing back after a 33-revision deferral is a request to change his spec.
6. IGNORES HIS ONE POSITIVE AESTHETIC RULING ON THIS EXACT ARTEFACT: "I like how the wheels were drawn in the earlier cartoon version" (rev39 §7, inside the F18 brief). It draws measured circles instead and never mentions it.
7. THE SHUT POSE DOES NOT EXIST IN THE MODEL — `TB_TILT_DEG` is built at 38.0 with no shut pose, and the apertures are cut holes with no shutters (`STATE.md`: "open serving apertures on +Y: 3"). The primary state is a drawing decision, which undercuts the concept's own "the model supplies the aperture geometry."
8. LAMP COUNT IS TWO NUMBERS: 118 built (`AUDIT_rev43.md`, exact on constants) vs ~115 photographed (`t1_detail.py`, 211 px/m, 28.6 ± 1.0 mm). Printing 118 on a backing card without saying which is which is the transcription defect this project punishes; "28.52 mm realised" was not watched print here.
9. VW TRADE-MARK EXPOSURE ON A COMMERCIAL ITEM. Four hubcap roundels, argued to be legible, on merchandise a business gives or SELLS. Low risk at 4.28 mm, non-zero if sold, and nobody has asked.
10. UNIT COST AND CHARGING. Four screen passes + phosphor vinyl + a die is expensive for a child's giveaway against digital-print-and-kiss-cut; and the phosphor will underperform in warm restaurant light, in a pocket, on a card.
11. THE FIRST STEP TESTS THE WRONG HALF. He is shown the shut bus (the weakest state) plus an unrelated green scrap. The product claim — the reveal — is never in front of him.
VERIFIED AND NOT ATTACKED: #C43124 and #CED0C8 are exactly `t1_mats.RED` and `CREAM`; 1:20.3, 1.405 mm, 4.28 mm all reproduce; F333 is cited with its ceiling; the stated risks are not filed off.
* WOULD FIX IT: 1. INVERT THE STATES. Daylight = the bus OPEN and WORKING — three hatches up, counter out, tail board propped, the bus he actually runs. Phosphor = the 118 lamps and the galley strip ONLY. Turn the light out and the lamps stay on after the shop shuts. Same phosphor pass, same physics, same cost, same joy — and the daylight face is now an open taqueria instead of a closed one. This kills attack 2 outright, kills its own stated risk (3), matches both in-service photographs (which show the string LIT and warm, `t1_mats.py` line 2211), and turns "the render has never managed either" into "the sticker shows the state the render cannot."

2. PUT THE NAME ON IT, AS A DECISION, IN WRITING. Either a fourth pass in metallic silver honouring F157's *"Bright silver, same as Tacombi"*, or an explicit reduction of the script to the warm dark line — and say which, cite SPEC §10.10 as the bar you are departing from, and show him both on the fridge print. Add the menu-yellow strips (172,144,17) if a fifth pass is affordable, or state that they reduce to cream. Silence on the brand mark is the one unforced error here.

3. TAKE THE SCALE BACK TO 60–80 mm, OR RE-ASK IT PROPERLY. His yes was framed on 60–80 mm precisely because the emblem cannot resolve there (F330). At 80 mm the lamp pitch is 0.561 mm, so the festoon becomes a continuous glow line or a coarser dotted rhythm rather than a 1:1 lamp count — state that as the design consequence, do not let the phosphor tolerance silently overrule the constraint that bought the approval. If you still want 200 mm, print BOTH sizes for the fridge and put it to him as one multiple-choice with the F191 consequence written on its face: "at 200 mm the VW badges become visible; at 80 mm they do not."

4. SPLIT THE REGISTER CLEANLY (F331). The sticker is the children's line: no figures, no backing card, no "we do not know it is right." Move every figure, the F333 badge caveat, the 118-vs-115 count and the phosphor-vs-warm-light departure into a companion ADULT item — a specimen card or a plate — where the deadpan register is confirmed. And pick one: FREE and handed to a child, or SOLD and kept by an adult.

5. DISCHARGE THE WHEELS RULING. Find the earlier cartoon version he named, put its wheels beside `la_rueda.py`'s measured circles in one crop, and let him choose. Five seconds of his time, and it closes a locked line of his own spec that no revision has ever acted on.

6. PRICE THE GREEN BEFORE PRINTING, DO NOT CONCEDE IT. Warm/amber-emission phosphor vinyls exist — dimmer, dearer, and much closer to the measured (1.000, 0.918, 0.790). Get a scrap of each, charge both under the same lamp, and photograph them side by side. Present a measured choice, not "it will glow green and no ink fixes it."

7. ASK THE TRADE-MARK QUESTION IN THE SAME BREATH AS THE FIRST ASK. One line to him: free-to-children versus sold changes the VW exposure on four reproduced roundels and on T1 trade dress. It is his and his counsel's call, not a design decision, but it belongs in the first conversation and not after a die is cut.

8. REBUILD THE FIRST STEP SO IT TESTS THE THESIS. Print the OPEN daylight state at both 80 and 200 mm on the home printer. Hand-paint the lamp row and galley strip with craft-shop glow paint, charge it under a desk lamp for sixty seconds, and switch the light off in front of him. Same week, same zero dollars, and the question he answers is the real one — does the bus still serving in the dark make you want to keep it — instead of "does a shut bus and a green scrap appeal to you."

9. ONE HOUSEKEEPING FIX: on any card that survives, print the lamp count with its provenance — "118 built; ~115 read off the photograph at 211 px/m" — and watch "28.52 mm realised" print before you publish it.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**OWNER-AND-BRAND — 1.5/4 — OWNER AND BRAND FIT — REJECT AS FILED, SALVAGEABLE. The two-state move is genuinely joyful and is the right register for a child. But on my lens this artefact has four defects, and two of them are the kind that end with a lawyer's letter or with him not putting it on the counter.

1) IT PUTS HIS BRAND ON NOTHING AND VOLKSWAGEN'S BRAND ON EVERYTHING — AND THE LEGAL RISK IS NOT IN ITS RISK LIST. The palette is RED, CREAM, a warm dark for line, and phosphor. Four passes. Nowhere in the ARTEFACT, FIGURES or MODEL paragraphs does the words "Señor Tacombi", the flank paisley, the Calidad decal or the menu strips appear. So the object is a plain red-and-cream VW T1 side elevation, die-cut, with four VW roundels on it (⌀86.9 → 4.28 mm, ×4), SOLD. What it is NOT is a Tacombi object: strip the hand-painted script and the folk art and it is indistinguishable from the most exhausted decal in the field — the van-life VW bus — with the licensed party's mark on it and the unlicensed party's brand absent. `CONCEPT_BENCH_rev77.md`'s own free-8-1 names that exact trap; this concept does not.
   The trademark half is worse because the concept argues FOR legibility: it cites F333 ("traces cleanly and legibly in line") as a virtue. A de-minimis defence on a 4.28 mm mark evaporates the moment you claim the mark reads. VW AG enforces on the T1 silhouette and the roundel; the exposure here lands on a real operating restaurant, not on this repo. Its STATED RISK section is three items and none of them is "we are selling merchandise bearing a third party's registered mark."

2) IT WALKS INTO SPEC §10.10 WITHOUT A DEFENCE, AND ITS PEERS ALL BUILT ONE. §10.10 is a HARD BAR, "outranks convenience everywhere it applies", recorded at his explicit request, and its scope table names the script, the flank paisley, the Calidad decal and the menu strips. Sibling concepts in the same bench carry an explicit rule-34 paragraph ("the hard bar's object is the MODEL; a sticker is a different object … said out loud rather than done quietly"). This one carries none. It does not defend the omission; it does not notice it. And the ONE element it silently drops is the one he is most sensitive about — the table's own row reads "'Senor Tacombi' script — REJECTED BY DONALD". A three-ink sticker cannot render F157's owner-ruled BRIGHT SILVER script either; so the concept either deletes his name or draws it in the warm dark, contradicting a settled ruling.

3) THE SCALE VOIDS THE REASONING HIS "YES" WAS GIVEN UNDER, AND RE-OPENS THE EMBLEM AT THE SIZE HE CAN SEE IT. F330's own carried ceiling: he was asked whether the MODEL is done enough, not the EMBLEM; F191 was not withdrawn; and "a die-cut sticker at 60–80 mm cannot resolve the roundel, which is why the question could be answered yes with the emblem still wrong." This concept ships 200 mm — 2.5–3.3× the object described in the option text he chose from — and the driver is the phosphor's deposit, not the brief. At 200 mm the badge is 4.28 mm and the concept ASSERTS it will draw legibly. So the first physical thing built off his fresh authorisation is bigger than what he authorised and puts the mark he has rejected NINE times back where he will look straight at it. This project's own §0: a model right in ninety places and wrong in one is not 99 % done.

4) IT PRINTS OUR DOUBT ON HIS COUNTER, AND IT STRADDLES THE SPLIT HE JUST RULED. "So it will draw, and we do not know it is right. That sentence goes on the backing card." That is an internal provenance grade migrating onto customer-facing merchandise. Report the measurement with its ceiling is a rule for the RECORD and for HIM; it is not a rule for a five-year-old's sticker card. Related: it is filed CHILDREN'S LINE (F331) but ships a figures-bearing backing card, an epistemic disclaimer, a 200 mm laptop-decal format, and "Sold or given" — refusing to pick a side of the split he ruled a revision ago (free/handed = children, paid/kept = adults). Registers are not supposed to be honest about being two things; that is the drift F331 was ruled to stop.

FIFTH, SMALLER: the locked scene has TWO additions — "nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun AND the papel picado." The concept confesses dropping the sun and drops the papel picado without a word. That silence costs it the only element that would have made the object read Mexican rather than German, and it misses that F330 has just made the deferred papel-picado question askable for the first time since rev 44.

WHAT IT GETS RIGHT, AND I WILL NOT SAND IT OFF EITHER: the built count is 118, not the 115 in circulation — correct against `t1_detail.py`'s BULB_X0/X1/PITCH, where most of the bench is wrong. The unlit-pearl daylight festoon is a documented render failure converted into a design decision, which is the strongest single move in the concept. Its risk paragraph is honest about the green phosphor against the measured warm galley (1.000, 0.918, 0.790). And the first shippable step — daylight state on a home printer, scalpel, fridge, a scrap of glow vinyl in his other hand, this week, zero dollars — is exactly the "we need to finally present something" answer, and it is the reason this scores above 1.

WOULD HE BE PROUD TO HAND SOMEONE THIS? Not as filed. He would hand a family a plain red van with three chalky greenish patches, a dotted pearl line, no name on it, someone else's badge on the wheels four times, and a card explaining that we are not sure the badge is right.**

* ATTACKS: A1 TRADEMARK, UNNAMED: four VW roundels at 4.28 mm, screen-printed, on a die-cut T1 silhouette, "Sold or given". The concept's own legibility claim (F333) removes the de-minimis defence. Exposure lands on a real taqueria. Absent from its three stated risks. || A2 BRAND ABSENT: palette is RED/CREAM/dark line/phosphor. No Señor Tacombi script, no flank paisley, no Calidad decal, no menu strips — the brand's entire hand-painted identity is missing from the brand's merchandise, leaving a generic van-life decal wearing VW's mark. || A3 SPEC §10.10 UNADDRESSED: a hard bar, recorded at his explicit request, whose scope table names four elements this object drops; peer concepts in the same bench all carry an explicit rule-34 artwork defence and this one carries none. Compounded by F157 — three inks cannot render the owner-ruled BRIGHT SILVER script. || A4 PREMISE DRIFT ON A FRESH RULING: F330's yes was chosen against option text describing 60–80 mm, and the emblem was finessed precisely because that size cannot resolve the roundel. 200 mm is 2.5–3.3× that, driven by phosphor deposit, and the concept asserts the badge draws legibly. F191 stands and this walks into it. || A5 DOUBT ON THE COUNTER: "we do not know it is right" printed on a customer-facing backing card — an internal provenance grade leaking onto merchandise, about the one object he has rejected nine times. || A6 REGISTER STRADDLE: filed CHILDREN'S LINE (F331) but ships a figures card, a disclaimer, 200 mm, and "Sold or given" — refusing the free/handed vs paid/kept split he ruled last revision. || A7 HALF-CONFESSED SCENE DEPARTURE: the lock names the sun AND the papel picado; only the sun is confessed. The dropped element is the only Mexican-craft element in the object, and F330 has just made that deferred question live. || A8 THE DAYLIGHT OBJECT IS THE OBJECT: 100% of viewers see the shut state most of the time — plain red, three chalky pale panels, dull pearl dots, no name. The night state is a bet (its own risk 3) that a child prefers a shut bus to an open one.
* WOULD FIX IT: CONCRETELY, IN ORDER:

1. PUT HIS BRAND ON IT AND TAKE VW'S OFF. Add a fifth pass: the SEÑOR TACOMBI script in F157's owner-ruled bright silver (or a metallic/white spot), traced off the line pass on the flank, plus the flank paisley reduced to one flat from `folk_gen.py`'s measured palette. Replace all four hubcap badges with the plain ⌀86.9 light disc — the exact move `dream:la-linea` already uses ("a disc, not a glyph"). That kills A1, A2, A3 and A4's emblem exposure in one edit, and it is what makes the object HIS rather than Volkswagen's. Get a one-paragraph IP opinion before any run that is sold; giving it away at the counter is a materially different risk from selling it.

2. WRITE THE RULE-34 / §10.10 PARAGRAPH THE PEERS WROTE. One paragraph on the face of the concept: the hard bar's object is the MODEL; a 200 mm sticker is a different object under rule 34; here is which artwork elements survive at this scale, which are stylised to one flat, and which are absent by projection rather than by choice. Silence is what makes this a violation instead of a declared reduction.

3. HOLD THE SIZE HE WAS SHOWN, OR RE-ASK. Either build it at 60–80 mm — accepting the phosphor smear and dropping the per-lamp glow to a single continuous glow RULE along the drip rail plus three glowing apertures, which is the same magic and does not need 1.40 mm dots — or, if 200 mm is genuinely load-bearing, put it back to him as multiple choice with both printed and in his hand: "80 mm, lamps as one glowing line" vs "200 mm, 118 separate lamps, and the wheel badges are blank discs". Do not let a phosphor deposit silently redefine an object he has just authorised.

4. TAKE THE DOUBT OFF THE CARD. The backing card gets: the name, the three apertures at 515.5 × 403.0 r 55, "118 lamps, 28.52 mm apart", and "charge it in the light, then turn the light out". Nothing else. The ceiling on the badge belongs in `OPEN_FINDINGS.md` and in the handoff, where it already is. If the card must be adult-register, then issue TWO backing cards — the child's card above, and an adult card in the paid line — which is what F331 actually asks for.

5. PICK A LINE. Children's line = free, handed across the counter, no price, no figures. If you want the figures object, that is a separate adult SKU and it should say so in its own row.

6. NAME THE PAPEL PICADO. One sentence: "the lock names the sun and the papel picado; this drops both, and F330 has now made the deferred papel-picado question askable — ask it with this proof attached." That converts a silent omission into the question his own deferral was waiting for.

7. FIX THE GREEN HONESTLY. Two routes worth one afternoon each: (a) overprint the phosphor with a transparent warm-amber tint so the glow reads amber at the cost of brightness, and test it — the concept says "no ink fixes it", which is stated as a fact and has not been watched print; (b) accept green and say so on the card as a departure from the measured (1.000, 0.918, 0.790). Either is fine; asserting an untested impossibility is not.

8. KEEP THE FIRST STEP EXACTLY AS WRITTEN. Daylight state, home printer, scalpel, fridge, scrap of glow vinyl in the other hand. It is the best thing in the concept. Just make sure the sheet he holds has his own name painted on the flank, because that is the difference between an object he is proud to hand someone and a decal off a beach shop rack.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**DANGER — 2/4 — DANGER DECLARED IN THE PROSE, NEUTRALISED IN THE OBJECT. The one thing genuinely at stake here — you hand a child, at a restaurant, a picture of that restaurant SHUT — survives in the daylight state and is then cancelled two seconds later by a light switch. The concept has a brave STATED RISK block and a safe artefact. Of its three declared risks, exactly one (the green-cyan phosphor against the measured warm galley) is actually carried in the object and unfixable; the other two are labels. And the single most dangerous property this artefact has — strontium aluminate DECAYS, so the night bus goes dark again while the child watches — is not mentioned once, in a piece titled "Goodnight". The material was handing it an elegy and the concept took a party trick.**

* ATTACKS: WHAT IS GENUINELY DANGEROUS HERE (the material available):
(a) A merchandise object whose default, always-visible, daylight state is CLOSED. On a fridge, in a shop, in a child's hand, for 23 hours a day it says: we are not open, nobody is here, the shift ended. That is commercially uncomfortable in a way nothing else on the 78-title bench is.
(b) It contradicts the only owner-LOCKED scene in 76 revisions — "nothing but the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado" — by proposing night. A bus asleep has no sun.
(c) It bets against the only audience instruction he has ever given: "spark joy … for children."
(d) The phosphor is chalky, glows 520 nm green-cyan against a measured warm (1.000, 0.918, 0.790), and cannot be made to behave.
(e) The phosphor DIES. It charges from daylight and fades over tens of minutes. An object about a bus going to sleep, made of a light that goes out.

WHAT THE CONCEPT ACTUALLY CARRIES:
(d) ONLY. "The night bus will glow GREEN, not warm. That is a real departure from the measured light and no ink fixes it" is intact, unfixed, and printed anyway. That is the one place the object is braver than its caption. Credit it.

WHERE IT SANDED ITSELF SMOOTH — SPECIFICALLY:

1. THE REVEAL IS A RESCUE, AND IT DISARMS (a) AND (c) MECHANICALLY. "The bus you thought was closed is open." The shut state is not a subtraction, it is a setup with a payoff, and the payoff is on a wall switch the child controls. Risk 3 says a five-year-old may prefer the open, working, colourful bus — and then the design GIVES HER the open bus, on demand, instantly, for free. The prose declares a bet the object refuses to make. Nothing is lost, nothing stays lost, the sadness has an off switch. This is precisely the failure pattern I was sent to look for: the warning label kept, the content removed.

2. THE DECAY IS MISSING, AND IT IS THE WHOLE PIECE. Every description of the night state is static — "all 118 bulbs come on", "the bus lights up while you eat". Strontium aluminate does not hold; it is bright for a minute, ordinary in ten, gone in an hour, and it can only be refilled by tomorrow's daylight. The honest artefact is: the lamps come on, and then the child lies there and watches them go out, and in the morning she puts the bus in the window to charge. THAT is "Buenas noches, Combi". THAT is a goodnight object — separation, the end of a day, a light borrowed from a day that is over. The concept has the title, the physics, the audience and the register all pointing at it and describes a permanent glow instead. It is the difference between a nightlight and an elegy, and it chose nightlight without noticing there was a choice.

3. "COMPANION TO F18, NOT F18" IS A PERMISSION SLIP. Risk 1 announces a fight with the owner's locked scene and then files the paperwork that makes the fight cost nothing: it is not the sticker he ordered, it is a companion, he has to approve. If night is right, then the sun is wrong, and the concept should say so and take the ruling and risk losing. Declaring yourself a companion is how an idea survives without ever being tested. The dangerous version claims to BE F18.

4. NO HAND, NO PERSON, NO SHIFT. This is an object about closing time in which nobody closed anything. The lamps light themselves. F331's ruling explicitly leaves critic finding 4 — NO FOOD, NO PEOPLE, in a programme for a taqueria — OPEN, and this concept reproduces that blind spot on the one artefact where it bites hardest: a shut taqueria means people went home. Nothing in the daylight state is a trace of a person: no cloth over the plancha, no prop-stay dropped, no flap folded, no chair, no till drawer. A machine that puts itself to bed is uncanny, not tender.

5. THE SPANISH IS DECORATION. "Buenas noches" on a child's sticker for a Nolita clientele is flavour unless somebody decides who the child is. There is a reading that would give this real teeth and it is unclaimed: children AT the restaurant include the staff's children, and to them a shut combi at night is not a mood, it is dad's bus with the lights off at the end of a shift. The concept never asks whose goodnight this is, so it defaults to the safest possible answer.

6. THE BACKING CARD IS AUDIT COMPLIANCE DRESSED AS CANDOUR. "It will draw, and we do not know it is right" — printed on a card that goes to a five-year-old, about a badge 4.28 mm across, to discharge an obligation to F191. A child does not read a ceiling. This is the adult line (F331) leaking into a declared children's-line object so that the concept never has to DECIDE about the emblem. Either draw the four badges point-for-point off F333's clean trace and own them, or leave blank discs and say why.

7. COST AND PRINT RISK ARE PRE-DEFENDED RATHER THAN EXPOSED. "Expensive for a sticker, cheap for an object families keep" is a sales line, not a risk. The real exposure it does not state: nothing here shows a high-deposit phosphor screen can HOLD a 1.40 mm dot on white gloss vinyl in four-pass register. The pitch arithmetic is deployed as reassurance ("so, 200 mm") when it is only the geometric floor; the printability floor is unmeasured and could be well above it. That is a projection presented as a decision, which this project has a rule about.

8. MINOR, BUT IT NICKS THE ONE CLAIM THE PIECE STANDS ON. The concept prints 118 lamps. `t1_detail.py`'s own comment records the photographed string as pitch 28.6 ± 1.0 mm, "~115 bulbs"; 118 is the BUILT count (`n+1` off `BULB_PITCH`). Both are real numbers, but the artefact is sold as "the measured truth" that "the render has never managed", and it silently prints the model's count rather than the vehicle's. Say which one is on the vinyl and why.

BOTTOM LINE FOR MY LENS: there is something at stake here — more than in most of the bench — and it is not zero, which is why this is not a 1. But the artefact is engineered so that every uncomfortable thing about it is temporary, optional, or delegated to a caption. Score 2.
* WOULD FIX IT: PUT THESE BACK, CONCRETELY:

1. MAKE THE DECAY THE CONTENT, NOT A FOOTNOTE. Rewrite the night state as what it physically is: the 118 lamps come on, and they go out. The child watches the bus fall asleep; in the morning she puts it in the window to charge it for tomorrow. Then measure it rather than assert it: buy the actual glow stock, photograph a charged strip at 1, 10, 30 and 60 minutes at fixed exposure, and print THOSE four readings on the backing card beside the galley's measured (1.000, 0.918, 0.790). The card's sentence becomes: this light is borrowed from today and it will not last the night. That single change converts the piece from a nightlight to a goodnight, restores the mournfulness the switch currently cancels, and costs nothing but one afternoon and a scrap of vinyl.

2. DROP THE WORD "COMPANION". Put it to the owner as one artefact and two mutually exclusive scenes, multiple choice, one crop each: (A) bus in the sun with the papel picado, as F18 locks it; (B) bus shut, night, glow. Say plainly that B contradicts his lock and that you think B is right. Be prepared to lose. A scene departure he can approve without choosing is not a risk.

3. PUT ONE HUMAN TRACE IN THE DAYLIGHT STATE, measured on the model and sourced to a photograph: the tail board's prop-stay dropped, the counter flap folded down, a cloth over the plancha, the hatch shut on its seal. One element that a person did before walking away. Without it, the shut bus is a haunted machine, and the programme's open NO PEOPLE finding is printed onto its most-handled object.

4. DECIDE THE BADGE. Either draw the four 4.28 mm hubcap badges point-for-point off F333's clean line trace and stand behind them, or print blank discs. Take "we do not know it is right" off a child's backing card; it belongs in the ledger, where it already is.

5. SAY WHOSE GOODNIGHT IT IS. One sentence in the concept, and it changes the object: this is for the children at the restaurant, including the staff's, for whom the shut combi at night is the end of a shift, not a mood. If that is not the reading, name the one that is.

6. PROVE THE DOT BEFORE THE SCALE IS DECLARED DECIDED. Pull one screen at 1.40 mm dots in high-deposit phosphor on the actual white gloss vinyl and look at it. Until that strip exists, 200 mm is a geometric floor, not a printable one, and the FIRST STEP as written (home printer, scalpel, a scrap of glow vinyl in his other hand) does not test the only thing that can kill the object.

7. RECONCILE THE COUNT. State on the card whether the 118 lamps are the model's built string or the photograph's ~115, and which one is on the vinyl.
* **DANGER INTACT: False**

**DANGER — 2/4 — The RISK SECTION is intact; the DANGER is gone. These are different things and this concept is the cleanest specimen of the confusion on the bench. It files three risks honestly, refuses to sand any of them, and none of them is about the artefact's content — two are process risks (a spec contradiction, a preference bet) and one is a materials disclaimer. Meanwhile every hard edge in the subject matter is available, visible, and declined.

WHAT IS GENUINELY AT STAKE IN THIS SUBJECT, AND WHAT THE CONCEPT DID WITH IT.

1. A SHUT FOOD TRUCK IS NOT ASLEEP. IT IS CLOSED, AND CLOSED IS THE STATE NOBODY IN A TAQUERIA FAMILY WANTS. The concept uses "asleep" five times and "shut" as a synonym for it. Asleep is cosy, reversible and benign — it is precisely the word a nervous designer reaches for to defuse "closed." This project's own bench already carries BAJA DEFINITIVA, NUNCA VOLVIO A CAMINAR (the ex-voto), SOLD and CERO; the register for a stopped vehicle exists here and is unsentimental. The title BUENAS NOCHES, COMBI is Goodnight Moon. It is a competent, charming, completely safe frame, and it was chosen over the available uncomfortable one.

2. THE MISSING MECHANIC IS DECAY, AND IT IS THE WHOLE THING. Strontium aluminate does not hold. It dumps most of its brightness in the first minutes and tails off. So the honest artefact is: you turn out the light, the bus is open for a while, and then it closes — and the sun has to come back before it can open again. The concept describes the glow as a stable second state ("all 118 bulbs come on") and never mentions that they go out. That is both a physical inaccuracy on the record and the surrender of the only mechanic in the object that carries mortality. A child watching the bus be on is a nightlight. A child watching the bus go out is the artefact. The concept has bought the material and thrown away what it does. (CEILING: I have not measured an afterglow curve; this is the material's known behaviour, not a figure I watched print. It must be measured or bounded before it is published.)

3. THE CHILD'S-EYE FACT IS RIGHT THERE AND UNTOUCHED. A glow sticker goes on a bedroom ceiling. This places the family business in a child's dark room. In a taqueria family the truck is the reason a parent is not home at bedtime, and a child saying goodnight to the truck is saying it to where the parent is. That is genuinely poignant and slightly awful and it is one sentence on the backing card. The concept never approaches it. It talks about "spark joy" and stops.

4. THE DAYLIGHT SILHOUETTE IS SADDER THAN THE CONCEPT NOTICES. "Nothing projecting past the sheet metal" — no counter, no tail board, no propped hatch, no festoon strung out. That silhouette is not a taqueria. It is the panel van before the conversion, or after it. The concept states the fact and reads it as bedtime.

WHAT IT DID KEEP, AND I WILL NOT UNDERSELL IT.

The GREEN is real and un-filed-off. The measured galley light is (1.000, 0.918, 0.790); the phosphor is 520 nm green-cyan; a green-glowing Senor Tacombi is a Halloween-shop object and the concept says so plainly instead of hiding it. And "it will draw, and we do not know it is right" — the hubcap badge printed on the backing card against STATE.md's own SELF-CONSISTENCY ONLY — is an uncomfortable admission printed on the product, which is more than most things here manage. Both survive. But a stated risk that is neither exploited nor solved is a disclaimer. The green is left as an apology; it is not made to mean anything.

AND RISK (1) PRE-SURRENDERS. "It must be presented as a scene departure he has to approve — not slipped past" is procedurally correct and artistically a retreat: it concedes the lock before arguing with it. The argument was available and was not reached for. F18's locked scene is "the bus, die-cut tight, plus the sun and the papel picado." A phosphorescent sticker does not delete the sun — THE SUN IS THE MECHANISM. It is the only thing that charges the bus. The one artefact on this bench that could satisfy the sun clause by physics rather than by drawing it is the one that apologised for not having a sun.

REGISTER DRIFT, WHICH IS DANGER-ADJACENT. It cites F331's children's line, then specifies 200 mm, four screen passes, a kiss-cut die, a few hundred dollars of tooling, and "sold or given." That is a laptop-lid object for a design-literate adult, not a thing handed to a five-year-old at a counter. F331 was ruled ten days of project-time ago precisely so items would say which line they are in; this one says children and builds adult. The drift matters to my lens because the adult line is where a green nightlight bus reads as charming, and the children's line is where it would have had to carry weight.

SCORE 2. Not 1: the green is genuinely kept, the badge admission goes on the product, and the die-cut-path-IS-the-line-pass argument is the best technical case for F18 anyone has made in 33 revisions. Not 3: nothing is at stake in the artefact itself. It is a very well-engineered bedtime object about a business that has closed for the night, and it declines to be about that.**

* ATTACKS: Attacked from the danger lens only. (a) Verified the grounding first so I could attack content rather than fabrication: probe_rev71_bulbs.py exists; F249 carries the unlit-pearl-white finding with its ceiling; 118 lamps at 28.52 mm is the watched figure and "115" is the stale one, so the concept is right; F330/F331/F332/F333 are real rows; F18's scene lock is verbatim. The concept does not lie. (b) Then: identified four available sources of discomfort — closure as economic fact rather than sleep, phosphor DECAY as mortality, the glow sticker's placement in a child's dark bedroom in a family whose parent works nights, and the shut silhouette being a van rather than a taqueria — and found all four either absent or softened to "asleep." (c) Separated the concept's stated RISKS (a spec contradiction, a materials disclaimer, a preference bet) from artistic DANGER and showed only one of the three touches the artefact's content, and that one is left as an apology. (d) Found the pre-surrender in risk (1): it concedes F18's sun clause instead of noticing that the sun is the charging mechanism, which is the strongest answer available to the lock. (e) Found the F331 register drift: cites the children's line, specifies an adult object at 200 mm with four passes and a die.
* WOULD FIX IT: SIX CONCRETE PUT-BACKS. Each is a change to the artefact or the card, not a change of tone.

1. RESTORE THE DECAY AND MAKE IT THE MECHANIC. The bus does not come on; it goes out. Measure the afterglow with a phone camera at fixed exposure at 0, 1, 5, 15, 60 min on an off-the-shelf glow-vinyl scrap (this is the FIRST STEP already proposed, plus a timer — zero extra cost) and PRINT THAT CURVE ON THE BACKING CARD with its ceiling. The artefact's sentence becomes: turn out the light and the bus is open for about N minutes, and then it closes, and only the sun can open it again. That is a bus that shuts, a real physical fact, and the sad thing, all in one property already paid for.

2. KILL THE WORD "ASLEEP." It is the sanding. The state is SHUT / CERRADO. Retitle to what is actually said at the end of a service — YA CERRAMOS, or keep BUENAS NOCHES only if the card makes clear it is what the person locking up says, not what a child says to a toy.

3. ONE SENTENCE ON THE CARD, THE CHILD'S-EYE FACT: the lamps are on because someone is still there. Say who is not home when the bus is lit. That single line converts a novelty nightlight into something a family keeps, which is F18's own stated test, and it costs nothing.

4. MAKE THE GREEN MEAN SOMETHING INSTEAD OF APOLOGISING FOR IT. Two admissible routes, pick one and price it: (i) print the daylight lamps in the MEASURED warm (1.000, 0.918, 0.790) so the night state is visibly the WRONG colour, and put on the card "this is not the light this bus makes; at 200 mm it is the only light we could make" — the departure becomes the subject; or (ii) source a warm-emitting phosphor, state what it cost and how much dimmer it is. What is not admissible is leaving a measured 520 nm contradiction of the project's own photometry as a footnote.

5. ARGUE THE F18 LOCK INSTEAD OF CONCEDING IT. Put to him as multiple choice: the locked scene says "plus the sun" — in this artefact THE SUN IS THE MECHANISM, the only thing that charges the bus, and the sticker cannot work without it. That is a defensible reading of his own clause and it should be the question, not a pre-emptive surrender. (And per F330's ceiling: this is a companion to F18, not F18 — keep that, it is correct.)

6. DECIDE THE LINE UNDER F331 AND SAY SO. At 200 mm, four screen passes and a die, this is the ADULT line; stop citing the children's clause for it. If it is genuinely for children, build the children's version at whatever size the phosphor allows and take the honest reduction — the festoon as one continuous glowing rail rather than 118 discrete beads, stated on the card as a reduction and why. Either answer is fine; claiming one and building the other is how a concept ends up safe by accident.

MINIMUM SET IF ONLY ONE THING CAN CHANGE: item 1. The decay is already bought, already physical, already free to measure this week, and it is the difference between a nightlight and an object about closing.
* **DANGER INTACT: False**

### CALENDARIO DE LA CASA — AÑO XXI — audit mean 1.88/4

**FEASIBILITY — 2/4 — FEASIBLE ON THE MACHINE, NOT FEASIBLE AS SCHEDULED. The model side is real and I proved it this session — but the artefact cannot ship for the year printed on its own masthead, that year number is off by one, and its documentary version depends on releases this project's record says are unobtainable. Score 2: the A3 gate is a 4, everything after the gate is a 1.**

* ATTACKS: WHAT I VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED — and this concept survives the figure-checking better than anything I have audited here.

1. THE LAMP FIGURE IS EXACT. My first recompute gave 123 lamps at 28.69 mm and I was wrong — I had ignored `_aft`. Recomputed properly: BULB_X0 = T._aft(-1.8000) = -1.636806, span 3.336806 m, n = 117, so 118 lamps at 28.5197 mm. The concept's "118 at 28.52 mm" is correct to the digit and correctly derived. I watched it print.

2. THE 3/4 LINE UNDERLAY EXISTS TODAY. I ran `line_pass.py --view hero34f`: 2063 strokes, 18579 points, 0 outside frame, baked in 2.2 s, ~25 s wall including the build. I rasterised it and LOOKED at it. It is a clean, usable illustrator underlay — body, glazing, arches, counter, louvres, hubcaps, and the drip-rail festoon all legible. The concept's "hand the illustrator a correct three-quarter line drawing" is not a capability request; it is a solved problem costing under a minute.

3. THE LIGHT KEY NEEDS NO RENDER AND F92 DOES NOT BITE IT. The ratios are already prose+code in SPEC.md §10.23 and studio.py:601 (3.95:1 flank-to-72°, 12.5:1 palm trunk, 0.93:1 up-facing cream, foliage 0.104–0.113×), with the rig's own reproduction 4.00 / 1.91 / 0.787 sitting beside them. A printed numbers card requires running nothing. The concept over-worries the F92 gate on the light; it is right that the owner must open the terrace as SUBJECT.

4. "THE RENDER CANNOT DO THE LIT LAMPS" IS TRUE AND STRONGER THAN CLAIMED — BUT THE STATED MECHANISM IS WRONG. F144, in probe_rev61.py's own docstring: photograph 4.96 and 3.82 against render 1.40, 2.8–3.6× short, corroborated on two independent scenes, and BOTH levers ablated and BOTH failed — T1_BULB_STR=60 moved it 1.40 -> 0.88 (worse; the bead clips toward white and clipped pixels are neutral), T1_BULB_BASEV=0.30 did nothing. That is a measured, twice-ablated finding. It is NOT "at V 0.95 the view transform takes the chroma out of the emission," which is an unsourced mechanism for a real result.

5. THE A3 MOCK IS DIMENSIONALLY HONEST. 480×680 aspect 0.70588; A3 297×420 aspect 0.70714 — 0.18% apart, A3 = 0.619× linear. A true-proportion scale proof.

NOW THE ATTACK.

A. THE SCHEDULE IS ALREADY GONE. The concept states its own lead: "commission by June to hand out on 6 February." Today is 5 September 2026. There are 5.0 months to 6 Feb 2027 against a self-stated 8-month lead, and that is BEFORE the owner has been asked the question the first step exists to ask. AÑO XXI cannot ship on the concept's own timetable. This is not a risk; it is arithmetic, and the concept did not do it.

B. THE YEAR NUMBER IS WRONG, AND IT IS ON THE MASTHEAD OF THE SHEET YOU PRINT THIS WEEK. "It counts in Roman years from 6 February 2006" ⇒ AÑO I = Feb 2006–Jan 2007 ⇒ AÑO XXI = Feb 2026–Jan 2027 — a year already 7 months elapsed and unissuable. The concept's stated span (Feb 2027–Jan 2028) is AÑO XXII under its own rule. It works only if AÑO I means the first anniversary hand-out (2007–08), which is nowhere stated — and under THAT reading the back panel's "years I–XX, NO EMITIDO" assigns no number at all to the business's actual first year. So the masthead and the back panel cannot both be right under either reading. In a programme whose entire thesis is that figures are derived and printed with their derivation, the series is named for a number nobody computed.

C. LIKENESS IS NOT "COSTLY," IT IS PROBABLY CLOSED. The concept offers "get releases and keep the documentary truth, or invent the figures and lose it." Branch one is near-certainly unavailable: the reference set was ruled complete at rev 54, PHOTOS_WANTED items 1–5 are struck because the owner said new photographs are not possible, and this project has never once obtained new material. Tracking a cook, a customer and a child photographed years ago on a Playa del Carmen terrace and getting signed releases is materially harder than taking one new photograph — which has already been refused. So it collapses to branch two, and the concept itself says branch two loses the thing that makes it good. Cost that honestly: the version being sold is not the version available.

D. THREE OF THE FOUR NAMED RED FLAGS FIRE. It needs a commissioned external illustrator; it needs a factory minimum (the two measured spot colours are the whole thesis, spots force litho, litho forces ~2,000+ units, so there is NO cheap pilot of the artefact — printing 200 digitally deletes the reason the object exists); and it needs access to people nobody can reach. Only "a capability nobody has built" does not fire, because the line pass now exists.

E. IT IS A STEP CHANGE IN OPERATING MODEL, NOT A TASK. Seventy-seven revisions, one person plus an AI, zero vendors, zero dollars. This asks for a commissioned living illustrator, a 6-unit litho house with tin-strip finishing and block binding, a 5,000-unit run, and an ANNUAL RECURRING obligation whose own pitch is that it "only makes sense at number three" — i.e. ~$36k–54k and three years before the idea is proven, from a project that has never bought anything.

F. TWO SMALL, REAL MACHINE GAPS THE CONCEPT DOES NOT NAME. (i) `sheet.py` has line/poly/rect/fill/arc/circle/text and NO image primitive — the first step's "ref_rear34.jpg cropped and knocked back to 40%" cannot be drawn today, on either backend. That is 1–2 hours, but this is exactly the layer where the rev-77 ledger records two watched backend-drift defects (the rotated-text path dropped labels silently, and SVG and PIL disagreed by 180° on rotation sign). Write the kill first. (ii) `sheet.py` is a ONE-INK system — `mix(ink, stock, tint)`, a single ink tinted toward the stock. It cannot express six units or fifteen colours. The production artwork will never come out of this toolchain; it comes from the illustrator's files and a repro house. That is a fine division of labour but it should be stated, because it means the machine contributes perhaps 5% of the artefact's production effort.

G. NO CAMERA COMPOSES THE PICTURE DESCRIBED. I looked at hero34f: it is 3/4 front-LEFT, nose-forward, and it foreshortens the serving flank hard — the bays and counter recede behind the cab. The painting wants bay 2 dominant and frontal with an arm coming out. That view does not exist in studio.views(). It is one dict entry and a 25-second bake, so it is trivial — but "hand the illustrator a correct 3/4" currently means handing them the wrong 3/4.

WHAT I WILL CREDIT. The concept costs itself honestly and says so; it names F92 by number and refuses to act on it; it names the likeness problem instead of hiding it; it names the February risk; and it gates ALL spend behind a ~$5 A3 print and one owner ruling. That first step is genuinely this-week work and is the correct shape. The failure is not that the object is expensive. It is that the object as specified cannot be made for the year it is named after.
* WOULD FIX IT: 1. DO THE ARITHMETIC AND PRINT IT. Fix the numbering by derivation, not by ear: state the rule on the sheet — "AÑO I = 6 feb 2006 – 5 feb 2007" — and the masthead then reads AÑO XXII, Feb 2027–Jan 2028, with the back panel listing I–XXI as NO EMITIDO.
