Put the derivation in the colophon, as every other artefact in this programme does with its figures. Until that line exists the series is named for an unchecked number.

2. SPLIT IT INTO A PILOT YEAR AND A PAINTED YEAR — this is the single change that makes it shippable. Target the PAINTED calendar at 6 Feb 2028 (AÑO XXIII), which restores the full 8-month lead the concept itself says it needs. For 6 Feb 2027 hand out AÑO XXII as a ONE-INK, single-sheet calendar, printed digitally at 300–500 units, no illustration spend, drawn entirely by sheet.py from the hero34f line bake plus the 12-leaf grid. Cost roughly $400–900. That puts a real object in customers' hands on the real anniversary, establishes the series at number one instead of number three, fits the existing one-ink toolchain exactly, and — critically — tests the February start EMPIRICALLY, which is the concept's risk 3 and a market question no owner ruling can answer.

3. KILL THE LIKENESS PROBLEM IN THE BRIEF, NOT IN THE RETOUCH. The concept's "generalise the faces = sanding" is a false dilemma: not showing a face is not the same as blurring one. Brief the illustrator for figures seen from behind and in three-quarter rear — the cook's back and white cap, a hand and forearm coming out of bay 2 with the plate, the woman's chin-on-hand pose read from behind with her face occluded by the bay coaming. Every documentary particular that actually carries the memory survives: the pose, the magenta puffer, the orange vinyl, the gingham on the near table only, the palms. No identifiable face, no release needed, no truth sanded. If the owner later gets releases, the faces can be added; the reverse is not true.

4. TWO MACHINE ITEMS, ONE SESSION, BOTH WITH WATCHED KILLS. Add an `img()` primitive to sheet.py on BOTH backends (SVG `<image>` with a base64 data URI, PIL alpha-composite for the PNG proof) — and write the kill before the feature, because this is the exact layer where rotated text produced two backend-drift defects at rev 77. Add a `hero34f_serv` entry to studio.views() composed on the counter with bay 2 frontal, and bake it. Then the illustrator pack is: that bake exported as SVG, the A3 mock, and a one-page light card printing SPEC §10.23's 3.95 / 1.87 / 0.772 beside the rig's 4.00 / 1.91 / 0.787 with the note that the third was never fitted to.

5. FIX THE LAMP CAPTION — the claim is stronger than what is written. Cite F144: photograph 4.96 and 3.82 against render 1.40, corroborated on two scenes, and both levers ablated and both failed (T1_BULB_STR=60 → 0.88, worse, because the bead clips toward white; T1_BULB_BASEV → no movement). Drop "at V 0.95 the view transform takes the chroma out," which is an invented mechanism for a measured result. Keep 118 at 28.5197 mm and cite BULB_X0 = _aft(-1.8000) = -1.636806 so the next reader can recompute it — I nearly published 123 by skipping that call.

6. TURN THE BIGGEST NUMBER INTO A MEASUREMENT. One email, this week, zero dollars: quote two Mexican calendar litho houses for 480×680, six units, tin strips top and bottom, 12-leaf block, at 500 / 1,000 / 2,500 / 5,000. The entire go/no-go turns on whether a sub-1,000 run with two spot colours exists at a sane price, and $1.20–2.00 is an estimate. This project's whole claim is that it replaces estimates with measurements; do it here.

7. ASK ONE QUESTION, WITH THE OBJECT IN HAND. Not three off one sheet. Bring the A3 mock AND the printed one-ink pilot proposal, multiple choice: (a) painted AÑO XXII for Feb 2027, accepting the compressed lead and ~$12–18k; (b) one-ink pilot on 6 Feb 2027, painted AÑO XXIII on 6 Feb 2028; (c) not this. That is the F92 opening the concept correctly says it must have before anything is built.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**ORIGINALITY — 1/4 — Acquitted on the charge it was set up to fail — this is genuinely NOT the flat-measured-line aesthetic relabelled; it is the opposite register and it is honest about being so. It fails on the charge that actually matters to my lens: the artefact it proposes is the single most-occupied square in the entire subject area. "Polychrome painting of a VW Combi in the Galas de México register, given away as a business calendar" is not a fresh idea about this vehicle; it is the default answer, and it is one a rival taquería could commission next month for less money. The concept names its own prior art in its first line and never escapes it. One move inside it — NO EMITIDO, twenty unissued years printed on the back — is genuinely original and is buried as a footnote on the reverse of the thing.**

* ATTACKS: 1. THE REGISTER IS NOT AVAILABLE — IT IS THE MOST COPIED MEXICAN GRAPHIC IDIOM IN EXISTENCE. The concept cites "Galas de México" and "the Helguera/Galas register" as though naming a source were the same as having one. Galas printed millions of these; Jesús Helguera's "La leyenda de los volcanes" (1940) reached the public precisely as a calendar giveaway, alongside Eduardo Cataño, Jorge González Camarena and Armando Drechsler. The revival is equally documented — Angela Villalba's "Mexican Calendar Girls" (Chronicle, 2006) is a whole published book of exactly this artefact, and the Galas archive has been exhibited. Downstream, this register is the default visual language of elevated-taquería branding since roughly 2010, and of Mexican beverage marketing (Modelo, Victoria, Indio, Jarritos, La Costeña) continuously. The concept's own risk (2) calls this "one step from kitsch and one step from pastiching a specific dead artist's manner" and then answers it with a budget line. Commissioning a living illustrator solves the ethics of pastiche. It does not make the register fresh.

2. AND THE SUBJECT IS SATURATED INSIDE THAT REGISTER. The Combi is the single most-illustrated object in contemporary Mexican nostalgia graphics — VW de México built the Type 2 at Puebla until 2013 and the "Última Edición" farewell produced its own wave of commemorative posters and calendars. A hand-painted Combi, warm light, palms behind, is a standing Mercado Libre / Etsy / Society6 category. Register × subject compounds: this is not a concept that risks resembling prior art, it is the modal output of the prior art. The brief asked for "fresh ones, good ones, and lots of em." This is the one anybody arrives at first.

3. THE ANNUAL-COMMISSION-SERIES THESIS IS PIRELLI, SIXTY YEARS OLD. "A series that only makes sense at number three," issued free, not for sale, a new artist each year, the object collectible because of the sequence — that is the Pirelli Calendar's founding thesis (1964–), and Lavazza (1993–), Campari, Michelin and Ricard have all run it since. This is not disqualifying on its own, but the concept presents recurrence as its discovery ("both the burden and the entire point") when it is the best-known convention in the category.

4. THE MEASURED CONTENT IS DECORATIVE, AND THAT IS THE REAL VERSION OF THE CHARGE I WAS ASKED TO PRESS. The programme's spine is "vector line and flat colour, shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset." This concept samples nothing. Run the test: could a competent art director produce this artefact with zero access to the model, given only ref_playa_34.png, ref_rear34.jpg, ref_side.jpg and a colour picker? Yes. The fifteen hex codes are read off frames. The 3.95:1 / 12.5:1 / 0.93:1 light key is real (SPEC_rev41 §10.23, verified) but it is a photograph-derived rig solve — it came off ref_playa_34.png, not out of the geometry. The one figure that is genuinely model-derived, 118 lamps at 28.52 mm, is invisible in a painting: no viewer counts them and no illustrator paints exactly 118. The concept even argues that a painting is needed BECAUSE the render loses the lamp chroma at V 0.95 — which is an argument for a painting, and quietly an argument against the model. What the model actually contributes is one thing: a correct three-quarter underdrawing. That is a deliverable, and the sentence "every VW bus in every calendar painting ever made is a caricature with the nose too round" is the best line in the document — but everything else under MODEL: is post-hoc justification. In a programme whose owner asked "Why do we keep doing Revs that don't ship geometry", the concept that uses the geometry least is the one asking for $12–18k and a nine-month lead.

5. AND THE ONE THING THE MODEL SUPPLIES IS THE PROJECT'S WEAKEST ASSET. The differentiator is "correct where every other calendar painting is a caricature," and it rests on the nose. The owner has rejected the nose emblem NINE times; F197 is a live ruling that "we still have work to do on the shape of the nose"; F191 and F234 both stand. An originality claim staked on the geometry the owner has refused most often is a claim that cannot be cashed this year.

6. UNNAMED PRIOR-ART HAZARD IN THE MASTHEAD: "AÑO XXI". Roman-numeral era-counting in the form ANNO XXI is the Era Fascista printing convention (Italy 1922–43; Anno XXI = 1942–43), and it appeared on commercial printing and calendars of exactly that period. The concept's risk list is otherwise unusually candid and this is not on it. It is a resemblance, not an equivalence, and it is cheaply fixable — but a programme that audits its own briefs for transcription defects should not ship a masthead it has not prior-art-checked.

7. IT DUPLICATES A LANE THE BENCH ALREADY HOLDS THREE TIMES, INCLUDING THE #1 SHORTLIST ITEM. LA LOTERÍA is a vernacular Mexican printed giveaway in series. PAPEL DE GRASA is the warm food-contact object. AZULEJO 1963 is the Mexican vernacular craft register. Galas de México printed lotería boards and calendars out of the same shop — the shortlist's #1 and this concept come out of one drawer. CALENDARIO is the fourth entry in that lane, the least differentiated, and by an order of magnitude the most expensive. Its own claim to be "the only warm thing in the programme" is false against its own bench: PAPEL DE GRASA is amber, lit and food-contact, and BUENOS DÍAS is the bus waking up. Justifying itself by a gap that is not there is an originality overclaim.

8. IT IS ABOUT A COMBI, NOT ABOUT THIS COMBI. The described painting — bay 2 open, an arm with a plate, a woman at formica, orange vinyl, gingham — is a taquería scene. Nothing in it is falsifiably this vehicle. The three equal 515.5 × 403.0 apertures, the 28.0° photographed tail-board tilt against 38.0° built, the OFF SIDE that SPEC grades "E (never photographed)" — none appears. Compare the bench's strongest entries: TRES HUECOS and THE FILAMENT cannot be made about any other object. This can be made about any Combi, and therefore by anyone.

WHAT SURVIVES, AND IT IS SMALL BUT REAL: (a) NO EMITIDO — twenty years printed once, small, blank, on the reverse. I know of no commercial calendar that prints its own non-existence as a claim of lineage. Retroactive-series fiction exists in art practice; as a giveaway calendar it is fresh. (b) The February start is mildly fresh — offset years exist (academic, fiscal, liturgical, vintage-dated wine) but "our year begins on the day the roof came off" is not a convention I can place. (c) The refusals are exemplary and keep the danger intact: it refuses to sand the faces, refuses to answer kitsch with a disclaimer, names F92 correctly under rule 34 and says HE MUST OPEN IT. That is the right handling of that carrier and it should be preserved in whatever replaces this.
* WOULD FIX IT: 1. INVERT IT — MAKE "NO EMITIDO" THE CONCEPT INSTEAD OF THE BACKPRINT. Issue the whole series at once: twenty-one sheets in one wire-stitched block, AÑOS I–XX each carrying its date grid and nothing else, stamped NO EMITIDO, and only AÑO XXI carrying the painting. That object does not exist anywhere. It is this project's own history made physical — 76 revisions of measurement that shipped nothing — it converts "a series that only makes sense at number three" from a nine-year promise into something he can hold this quarter, and it costs ONE illustration rather than one per year. It also survives the register problem, because the reason to look at it is no longer the painting.

2. PICK ONE AND WRITE IT DOWN: make the model load-bearing, or drop the MODEL: section entirely. (a) Ship the underdrawing as the actual deliverable — a print-size, geometrically correct three-quarter line drawing off the model plus the light-ratio card, as the commission package. Then the model's contribution is a nameable artefact and the cost collapses to the illustration. (b) Or sample the model for real: composite a measured 118-lamp emission pass under the painting, so the one thing the concept says the render cannot do is the one thing the model supplies. As written the concept argues the render loses the lamps and therefore a human should paint them, which concedes the asset is optional. Either fix is honest; the current text is neither.

3. MAKE IT ABOUT THE OFF SIDE. SPEC grades the off-side elevation "E (never photographed)" — an entire flank of this vehicle that no frame in the reference set shows. Paint THAT. The calendar becomes the only image in the world of a side of this vehicle that has never been seen, reconstructed from measurement, and the model is not decoration but the sole possible source. No competitor can copy it, the originality claim is unfalsifiable in the right direction, and it is a better story than a front three-quarter. Caption it honestly — reconstructed, with its ceiling — which the programme's rules require anyway.

4. KILL THE ROMAN ERA-COUNT OR CLEAR IT. "AÑO 21", "XXI ANIVERSARIO", or a vernacular spelling. One hour of prior-art checking on the masthead before the A3 leaves the building.

5. GATE THE COMMISSION ON A ZERO-SPEND DIFFERENTIATION TEST, RUN BEFORE THE A3, NOT AFTER. Pull twenty images of current taquería and Mexican-beverage branding in the Galas register — including the client's own live material — and pin the mock beside them. If it does not read as distinct at 2 m, the register is not available and no illustration budget fixes that. This is one day and no money, and it is the test that decides whether the concept is a revival or a repetition.

6. WRITE THE TWO-HOUSE-STYLES RULE IN ONE SENTENCE, THIS REVISION, BEFORE ANY OF THE ABOVE. The concept correctly says nobody has written one. Proposed: the austere line programme is THE RECORD; the polychrome programme is THE INVITATION. Put it in the brief so the next context inherits it rather than re-deriving it.

7. KEEP VERBATIM, WHATEVER REPLACES THIS: the refusal to generalise the faces, the F92 handling under rule 34, and the instruction that he must open it before anything is built. Those are the strongest paragraphs in the document and they are not register-dependent.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**OWNER-AND-BRAND — 2.5/4 — The best brand instinct on the bench, wired to the one production spec most likely to get the owner's tenth rejection. A free wall calendar is the single most natural object a taqueria can hand a stranger — it needs no explanation, no design literacy, and it answers the standing "no food, no people" critique in one image. He would be proud to hand someone the finished painting. The problem is that he would be proud of the ILLUSTRATOR, not of the project: the model contributes a line drawing and a light card, a third party contributes everything the eye lands on, and the concept spends the programme's largest budget ($12–18k, recurring) on an outcome the machine does not control — for the owner whose complaint is "Why do we keep doing Revs that don't ship geometry." Three specific fit failures, none of them fatal, all cheap to fix. FIRST AND WORST — IT COMPOSITES TWO DIFFERENT VEHICLES IN TWO CITIES IN TWO SEASONS AND NEVER SAYS SO. The set is Playa: palms, gingham, vinyl chairs, the 3.95:1 light — all off `ref_rear34.jpg` and `playa_env.py`. The cast includes "the child in the magenta puffer", who is in `ref_nolita_front34.jpg` — New York, a winter coat, and a DIFFERENT BUS. `SPEC.md` §10.15 says it in bold: "This is the Playa bus, NOT the Nolita one." In the one artefact whose entire claim is documentary warmth, the documentary is a splice, and the figure list flags the women's faces as unmeasured while saying nothing about the cast being from another vehicle. A restaurateur who was there will see a Playa terrace with a kid dressed for a New York February in it. SECOND — SPEC 10.10 GETS NO DEFENCE AT ALL, AND THE CORE METHOD IS THE EXACT FAILURE MODE THE RULE WAS WRITTEN FOR. 10.10 is a hard bar at his explicit request: every painted element "REPRODUCED from photographs of the actual combi. Not approximated. Not invented. Not derived from palette statistics" — and its own text names the rev-8 disaster: the mural built from measured palette ratios, "plausible and generically 1960s", the bar being that "the owner recognises HIS OWN VEHICLE." This concept hands an illustrator five mural swatches (GROUND/RED/ORANGE/GOLD/PALE) and a light card and lets a person paint the mural, the paisley, the menu strips and the Señor Tacombi script — at 480×680 mm, where every one is large and legible, so no scale-derivative defence is available. Other bench items make the rule-34 object argument explicitly (the medal: "at 1:81.3 the script is 15.7 mm"). This one, the biggest and most legible, makes none. And the script is the element he REJECTED BY NAME. THIRD — THE ROUNDEL IS UNADDRESSED IN THE ONLY POLYCHROME, NOSE-CAPABLE PIECE IN THE PROGRAMME. Sibling concepts declare it ("a side elevation composes the VW roundel out. Declared: F191 is still shut"). This one says "a correct three-quarter line drawing" without saying WHICH three-quarter — and hero34f is a front three-quarter. Every vernacular bus calendar ever painted is composed nose-forward. So the default composition puts the mark he has rejected NINE times, in colour, a foot wide, on 5,000 walls, after $3,000–8,000 of unrevisable commissioned illustration. That is also a live Volkswagen trademark on commercial giveaway merchandise, in the Galas register — which is the register of the actual Volkswagen de México dealer calendars, so at ten feet the object reads as VW-affiliated. That is dilution running the other way and the risk list does not mention it. SMALLER: the one-line hook, "February is when the roof came off," is wrong on the record — SPEC grades 6 February 2006 as the OPENING (Wolos's own account, grade S); the roof came off before it. That sentence is the printed justification for the whole February start and it is a claim in prose, not a measurement. "NO EMITIDO" on the reverse prints a twenty-year confession of absence on the back of a warm giveaway, in a programme a critic already grades as "a joke, a proof, a confession or a diagram" — it is the austerity leaking into the one object that was supposed to escape it. And F331 now rules the audience split with free/handed-to-you in the CHILDREN'S line: this is free, handed to you, and a Galas-register lithograph of a woman waiting to be served. It is unassigned under a ruling that now exists. What it does right and should not lose: the F92 handling is the correct move in the whole bench (rule-34 object test made, then "HE MUST OPEN IT. Do not build it until he does"), the likeness risk is named unsanded, the cost is labelled ESTIMATE, and the first step spends nothing.**

* ATTACKS: 1. NOLITA CAST IN A PLAYA SET, UNDECLARED. Palms/gingham/vinyl/3.95:1 are Playa (`ref_rear34.jpg`, `playa_env.py`); the magenta-puffer child is `ref_nolita_front34.jpg` — different bus, different city, winter coat. `SPEC.md` §10.15: "This is the Playa bus, NOT the Nolita one." The documentary object is a splice and its figure list does not say so. 2. NO SPEC 10.10 DEFENCE, AND THE METHOD IS THE RULE'S OWN NAMED FAILURE MODE. Five swatches to an illustrator = "derived from palette statistics", verbatim what 10.10 forbids and what rev 8 shipped and he rejected. At 480×680 mm no scale-derivative defence exists. The Señor Tacombi script is an element he rejected by name and it would be hand-approximated again. 3. THE ROUNDEL HAS NO INSTRUCTION. "A correct three-quarter" is unspecified; hero34f is front three-quarter; calendar buses are painted nose-forward by convention. Default composition = the mark he has rejected nine times, in colour, a foot wide, after the commission is unrevisable. Sibling concepts declare this explicitly; this one does not. 4. VW TRADEMARK, TWO WAYS. A Volkswagen roundel reproduced in colour on commercial giveaway merchandise, in the exact register of the historic Volkswagen de México dealer calendars — so it also reads as VW-affiliated at a glance, which is Tacombi trading on VW's mark rather than the reverse. Named nowhere in the risk list. 5. THE HOOK IS FACTUALLY WRONG. "February is when the roof came off" — SPEC's grade-S source (Wolos) has 6 February 2006 as the OPENING, with the roof reeled back before it. That sentence is the printed rationale for the February start and for a perpetual Roman-numeral series. 6. FEBRUARY FORFEITS THE ONE RITUAL A CALENDAR HAS. Mexican businesses give calendars out in December, and that is when the gesture reads as generosity; the anniversary is a date the customer does not know. "Turns 6 February into an event the house already owns" is an asserted marketing outcome, unsupported — a claim in prose. 7. THE BUDGET BUYS AN OUTCOME THE MODEL DOES NOT CONTROL. Largest spend on the bench, recurring, and the visible quality is entirely the illustrator's. For the owner impatient about revisions that ship nothing, the failure mode is: good illustrator, model was decoration; weak illustrator, no measurement saves it. 8. "NO EMITIDO" PRINTS A CONFESSION OF ABSENCE ON THE BACK OF THE ONE WARM OBJECT — the austerity the concept claims to abandon, reappearing on the reverse. 9. F331 UNASSIGNED. Free/handed-to-you is now the children's line by ruling; this is a Galas-register lithograph for adults. Neither line, stated as neither.
* WOULD FIX IT: 1. RESOLVE THE CAST-AND-SET SPLICE BEFORE ANYTHING IS DRAWN. Pick ONE vehicle and ONE scene. Recommended: Playa only — palms, gingham, vinyl, the measured 3.95:1 light, and figures drawn from the Playa frames alone. Drop the Nolita child entirely (she is also the one subject who cannot consent and whose parent cannot be found). Add one line to the figure list: "SCENE AND CAST ARE BOTH PLAYA. `SPEC.md` §10.15 — the Nolita bus is a different vehicle and nothing from it appears here." 2. SPECIFY THE VIEW AND KILL THE ROUNDEL QUESTION IN ONE STROKE. Mandate the REAR three-quarter on the show side — which is also the only view the concept's own light key was measured for (`playa_env.py` recovers the camera at (-4.83, +2.22, 1.90), i.e. behind the bus on the +Y flank). The nose, and therefore the VW mark, is out of frame by construction. Put it in the illustrator's brief as a hard constraint, and print the declaration the sibling concepts print: "NOSE OUT OF FRAME. F191 IS STILL SHUT AND THIS DOES NOT TOUCH WHAT IT GATES." That also removes the VW-affiliation read and the trademark exposure at zero cost. 3. WRITE THE 10.10 ARTWORK PACK, AND MAKE IT THE COMMISSION'S FIRST DELIVERABLE. The illustrator gets the BAKED artwork — `lid_gen.py`'s mural board and `folk_gen.py`'s paisley at 2–4K, plus the source crops (`ref_side.jpg` (300,40)-(770,310) for the mural, (300,470)-(620,570) for the script) — as positional reference, not five swatches. Then apply 10.10's own stated method to the painting: measure the result back against the crop before it goes to plate. One acceptance line in the contract: "the mural, the paisley, the strips and the script are REPRODUCED, not interpreted; the check is that the owner recognises his own vehicle." That converts the concept's biggest liability into the one thing the project is uniquely equipped to supply. 4. FIX THE HOOK, THEN VERIFY THE DATE WITH HIM IN THE SAME SENTENCE. Replace "February is when the roof came off" with "6 February is when it opened" — that is what the grade-S source says. And because a Roman-numeral series is a perpetual, publicly checkable anniversary claim, put the date to him as a one-line confirmation with the A3 (he is the client; he knows). Do not print AÑO XXI off a dossier line the rev-77 bench itself flags as needing re-check. 5. ADD THE DECEMBER ANSWER RATHER THAN DEFENDING FEBRUARY. Keep the 6 February hand-out AND ship a small December companion — a single-leaf year card, same painting, no block — so the house is not empty-handed in the month when giving a calendar means something. That removes risk 3 instead of shrugging at it, and costs one extra plate change. 6. MOVE "NO EMITIDO" OFF THE OBJECT. If the series-of-three idea needs stating, state it forward — "AÑO XXII · FEBRERO 2028" as a footline — not backward as twenty blank years. Nothing warm should carry a null result on its reverse. 7. ASSIGN THE LINE UNDER F331 AND SAY IT OUT LOUD. This is the adult line despite being free; declare the exception and give the reason (it hangs on a wall an adult chose). Then propose the one-sentence house rule the concept asks for and nobody has written: POLYCHROME WHERE A STRANGER GETS IT FOR FREE AND MUST WANT IT; AUSTERE WHERE SOMEONE PAID AND WANTS THE PROOF. 8. DE-RISK THE COMMISSION IN STAGES. Do not go from A3 mock-up to finished painting. Insert a paid colour rough at ~25% of fee, shown to him, with an explicit kill point — he has rejected one drawing nine times and the whole budget must not sit behind a single reveal. 9. RECAST THE WOMAN AT THE TABLE AS A PARTY, NOT A LONE FIGURE WAITING. In the Galas register a solitary woman waiting to be served is one composition step from the thing that register is notorious for. Two people at the table, mid-conversation, plate arriving, solves it and is warmer besides.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**DANGER — 2/4 — PARTLY SANDED — and the part that got sanded is the part unique to this concept. What survives with teeth is generic to any recurring print series (the recurring money, the February start, NO EMITIDO). What has been replaced by a warning label is the thing that makes THIS artefact risky: it is a free giveaway wall calendar, in the Galas de México register, whose emotional centre is a real, unconsented young woman in a halter top. The register's historical product is the chica de calendario. The concept invokes the house by name and never once names what that house sold. Its risk paragraph calls the danger "kitsch." Kitsch is the polite word.**

* ATTACKS: WHAT I CHECKED. I opened the frames rather than reading about them. `ref_rear34.jpg` (1200×824) is the terrace scene this painting depicts. `playa_env.py`'s palette constants are real and the concept quotes them correctly — C_VINYL (195,110,68) = #C36E44, C_GINGHAM_G/W (143,173,127)/(175,199,164) = #8FAD7F/#AFC7A4, C_SIGN_INK (70,62,50) = #463E32 with the source's own comment "warm dark grey, NOT black". The palette provenance holds. Everything below is about what the picture contains.

1. THE UNNAMED DANGER, AND IT IS THE BIG ONE: THIS IS A CALENDAR GIRL AND NOBODY SAID SO. Galas de México ran on the chica de calendario — the pin-up given away free by garages, cantinas and tyre shops across twentieth-century Mexico. That is the genre's central commercial product, not a side effect. The concept picks that register by name, then puts at the centre of the picture the woman from `ref_rear34.jpg`: young, bare-shouldered in a floral halter, arm on the table, chin on her hand, looking straight down the lens. Painted at 480 × 680 mm, 5,000 units, handed out free by a restaurant, every year forever. The concept's account of this risk is "one step from kitsch and one step from pastiching a specific dead artist's manner." That is a craft risk. The actual risk is that a taqueria issues a pin-up calendar of a customer and finds out what that reads as in 2027. The illustrator brief as written — "Galas de México register" plus a light-ratio card — hands the genre's gravity a free run at her.

This is not a reason to kill it. Held deliberately it is the best thing in the programme: a calendar in the calendar-girl format whose woman is fully clothed, faintly bored, WAITING TO BE FED, with a napkin dispenser and a salt shaker in frame beside her. That is an inversion with real content. But an inversion has to be declared and specified or it does not happen, and nothing in this concept declares it.

2. THE RISK PARAGRAPH HAS NOT BEEN CHECKED AGAINST ITS OWN FRAME. It names "the cook in the white cap, the woman at the table, the child in the magenta puffer." The cook is in `ref_side.jpg`. The child in the magenta puffer is in `ref_nolita_front34.jpg` — New York, a different continent from this picture. Neither is in the scene this painting depicts. What IS in it, and unnamed: a SECOND woman, standing at the palm trunk, back to camera, grey skirt, carrying a bag. A likeness risk that lists two people who are not in the picture and misses one who is, is boilerplate borrowed from adjacent concepts. It is a label, not a load-bearing part.

3. THE MITIGATION IS A FORK HANDED TO SOMEONE ELSE, NOT A DESIGN. "Get releases and keep the documentary truth, or invent the figures and lose it. Both cost something; there is no free version." True, well put, and it changes nothing about the specified artefact. Compare the sibling concept in the same bench (`CONCEPT_BENCH_rev77.md:1518`), which ships an actual asymmetry into the object: the child appears only on the restaurants' own wall copies and never on anything sold; if the eater cannot be cleared he is drawn from behind AND THE CAPTION SAYS SO. That is danger surviving into the artefact. Here, nothing in the spec prevents "we'll generalise the faces" — the very move the round was condemned for — from being the path of least resistance at commission time, because the spec has no mechanism that would make its absence visible on the sheet.

4. THE FIRST STEP ROUTES AROUND THE ONE QUESTION THAT GATES THE CONCEPT. The concept says correctly "HE MUST OPEN IT. Do not build it until he does." Then the A3 asks him three things — the object's shape, the February start, the anniversary hand-out — and none of them is "may we work the terrace at all." Worse: the sheet puts `ref_rear34.jpg` in the image window at 40 % without asking whether that frame is on the table. Asking three safe questions with the unsafe one visible in the background is exactly the shape of sanding: the risk is present in the room and absent from the ask.

5. THE REGISTER PROBLEM IS NAMED AND ASSIGNED TO NOBODY. "Two house styles need a written rule about which is which, and nobody has written one." This is the concept that breaks the programme's only distinctive property — that every piece states what it does not know — and it hands the fix to an unnamed future person. In a project whose own rule 16 exists because unowned carriers get dropped, "somebody must write a rule" means the calendar becomes the exception that quietly retires the austerity.

WHAT IS GENUINELY INTACT, AND I WILL NOT LET IT BE TALKED DOWN:
- NO EMITIDO. Twenty years of the business's life printed small on the back and declared NOT ISSUED. That is bleak, it is the sharpest and saddest line on the sheet, and it will be the first thing softened to something like DESDE 2006 at print. It must not be.
- IT STARTS IN FEBRUARY. A calendar that is unusable as a calendar for most of its first year, replacing the civic new year with the house's own. Small institutional arrogance, and good.
- THE MONEY, STATED. $12,000–18,000 a year, recurring forever, for a giveaway, put in front of an owner who has just said "we need to finally present something." Brave and unhedged. Though the concept states the annual figure and lets the reader not do the multiplication: "only makes sense at number three" is a ~$40,000 commitment before the idea works at all, and that number should be on the page.
- THE ADMISSION THAT THE MODEL CANNOT DO THE ONE THING THE EYE GOES TO — 118 lit lamps that the view transform strips the chroma out of at V 0.95, which a painting gets for free. An argument that the programme's central asset is inadequate here, made against interest, in the programme's own document. Keep it.
* WOULD FIX IT: 1. NAME THE GENRE, ON THE OBJECT AND IN THE BRIEF. Add to the risk section, in as many words: "Galas de México's calendar trade was the chica de calendario. This is a free giveaway calendar with a young woman at its centre and it lands inside that history whether it intends to or not." Then commit to the inversion in the illustrator brief, as an instruction with a failure state: ESTA NO ES UNA CHICA DE CALENDARIO. ESTÁ ESPERANDO SU COMIDA. Specify it — she is turned toward bay 2 and the arriving plate, not toward the viewer (better composition and it breaks the lens contact the photograph has); fully clothed as photographed; the napkin dispenser, salt and the near table's gingham skirt in frame with her so the picture is about a meal arriving, not about her. If the inversion is not taken, do not use this register.

2. RE-CUT THE RISK PARAGRAPH AGAINST `ref_rear34.jpg` ITSELF. The people in this scene are TWO WOMEN: one seated at the near table, face-on and identifiable; one standing at the palm trunk, back to camera, grey skirt and bag. There is no cook in a white cap (that is `ref_side.jpg`) and no child in a magenta puffer (that is `ref_nolita_front34.jpg`). Say so.

3. MAKE THE LIKENESS DECISION PART OF THE PRINTED ARTEFACT — pick one now, do not leave a fork. Either (a) the seated woman is traced, asked, paid, and CREDITED BY NAME in the foot line beside the address, which converts the legal exposure into the warmest thing on the sheet and is the only version that earns the documentary claim; or (b) she is not traceable, she is painted three-quarters away, and the foot line prints the absence: LA CLIENTA DE LA MESA · FEBRERO 2010 · NO IDENTIFICADA. Both ship a visible consequence. The banned third option — quietly generalising the faces — must be named as banned in the spec, not only in the risk note, because nothing currently stops it.

4. PUT THE GATING QUESTION ON THE A3. Add a fourth ruling, multiple choice with the crop attached: "The picture is set at your PLAYA terrace, which F92 deprioritised. (A) Open it for this object only. (B) Move the scene to the Nolita frontage. (C) No terrace — the bus alone." He cannot rule on the object's shape without knowing whether the scene is permitted, and the sheet currently shows him the frame without asking about it.

5. WRITE THE TWO-REGISTER RULE IN THIS CONCEPT, since this is the concept that breaks it. One sentence on the sheet: the austere register states what is NOT known; the polychrome register states what the place is LIKE; no piece is in both, and everything the polychrome register asserts is either traceable to a measurement or captioned as invented — which is why the two women's faces are captioned UNMEASURED and the strip ink is captioned CONSTRUIDO.

6. PUT THE THREE-YEAR NUMBER ON THE PAGE. "$12,000–18,000, and it only works at number three" means roughly $40,000 committed before the idea pays. State the total, not just the annual.

7. DO NOT TOUCH NO EMITIDO. Twenty years declared un-issued is the load-bearing melancholy of the whole object and it is the first thing that will be softened.
* **DANGER INTACT: False**

### EL TROQUEL — audit mean 1.88/4

**FEASIBILITY — 2/4 — BUILDABLE BUT MIS-COSTED BY ROUGHLY AN ORDER OF MAGNITUDE, AND THE OBJECT AS SPECIFIED WOULD NOT READ. Nothing here needs a bus you cannot access, a photographer, or a capability nobody has invented — that is the good news and it separates EL TROQUEL from most of the pool. The relief master is genuinely reachable on this machine: scipy is on the box (bootstrap installs it), the height field is a 2D array, and the hard algorithm (gradient-domain relief compression) is published. But the concept's load-bearing feasibility sentence — "Building it is one line plus a 32-bit EXR output — an afternoon" — is false, and the arithmetic that shows why is arithmetic the concept never ran. I ran it. Everything else in the audit follows from that one number.**

* ATTACKS: ATTACK 1 — THE RELIEF BUDGET. THIS IS THE DECISIVE ONE AND IT IS ARITHMETIC, NOT OPINION.
The side ortho's depth axis is Y. STATE.md's measured lateral extent is full-Y [-1.0637, 1.1500], so the depth span the medal must hold is 2.2137 m = 2213.7 mm. A struck Ø55 mm medal carries ~0.8-1.5 mm of relief. Linear compression at a 1.2 mm budget is 1:1845. WATCHED PRINT:

    serving aperture (BAY_DEPTH 420 mm)   0.2277 mm
    tail board projection (~400 mm)       0.2168 mm
    wheel arch lip to tyre gap (39.7 mm)  0.0215 mm  =  21.5 micron
    lid_rail RAIL_PROUD (21.3 mm)         0.0115 mm  =  11.5 micron
    panel shut line (~8 mm)               0.0043 mm  =   4.3 micron
    V-swage (~6 mm)                       0.0033 mm  =   3.3 micron

A fine ball-nose tool radius is 250-500 micron. A legible step in struck bronze is tens of microns. So under linear compression the medal has EXACTLY TWO features — three shallow holes and a tilted board — and the entire rest of the vehicle is 3 to 22 microns, which is below the cutter, below the metal, and below the eye. "Render the side ortho, invert, cut" produces a bus-shaped blob. This is not a polish problem; it is the object failing to be the object.

ATTACK 2 — THE FLANK'S IDENTITY IS PAINT, AND PAINT HAS ZERO HEIGHT. UNFIXABLE INSIDE A Z PASS.
I checked tex/: senor.png, lidmural.png, calidad.png, lidsign.png, swirl.png are TEXTURES on the mesh. The Señor Tacombi script, the folk-art mural, the menu strip, the calidad burst — every graphic that makes this vehicle THIS vehicle — has a depth gradient of exactly zero. The concept files this under risk (4) as a LEGIBILITY problem at 1:81.3, curable by scale. It is not. It is a topology problem: at Ø300 mm in plaster the script is still 0.0000 mm tall. The showpiece does not rescue it. No relief-compression algorithm can amplify a gradient that does not exist.

ATTACK 3 — "AN AFTERNOON" IS WRONG BY 10-30x, AND THE MISSING WORK IS NAMED.
The Z pass itself is real and cheap — studio.py sets no use_pass_z, adding it plus an EXR output is genuinely half a day. Everything after that is the job:
  * background masking (Z = clip_end on every non-bus pixel),
  * NONLINEAR depth compression — the standard answer is gradient-domain (Weyrich et al. 2007, "Digital Bas-Relief from 3D Scenes"), a Poisson solve over a ~2000x1400 grid. Implementable with scipy, but it is 1-2 days to write and several more to tune, not a line,
  * DRAFT ANGLE (see attack 4),
  * edge fillet, boundary rim, tool-radius simulation.
Call it 3-6 working days of code plus 2-4 weeks ELAPSED, because every iteration has to be looked at as a physical part and physical parts have a turnaround. The concept's own project rules forbid publishing a figure you have not watched print; "an afternoon" is such a figure.

ATTACK 4 — NO DRAFT ANGLE. NOBODY HAS MENTIONED IT AND IT BREAKS BOTH OBJECTS.
A Z buffer is single-valued, which the concept correctly notes means no undercuts. But single-valued is not the same as demouldable: a raw Z pass has 90-degree vertical walls at every silhouette and every aperture edge. Chocolate needs ~5-10 degrees of draft or the tablet locks in the polycarbonate cavity and the relief edges snap off on release. A striking die with vertical walls gets incomplete metal fill and accelerated die wear. This is fixable by a slope-limiting pass on the height field, but it is not in the tree, it is not in the concept, and it is the single most common reason a first CNC'd relief master is scrapped.

ATTACK 5 — THE CHOCOLATE PROCESS CHAIN AS NAMED IS NOT HOW CHOCOLATE MOULDS ARE MADE.
"A Ø55 mm bas-relief cut on a 4-axis CNC in food-grade polycarbonate." Two errors. (a) A flat circular relief is a 3-AXIS job; 4-axis is a rotary setup and its presence signals the fabrication was not costed by anyone who has cut one. (b) Professional polycarbonate moulds are THERMOFORMED over a CNC'd aluminium or brass master, not machined directly in PC. Direct-machined PC exists for tiny runs but leaves tool marks, and chocolate gloss and release both depend on a mirror-polished cavity — machining marks cause exactly the sticking that attack 4 also causes. So the chain is: CNC aluminium positive, polish, thermoform PC. One more step, one more supplier, and the "US$400-900" needs to absorb the master.

ATTACK 6 — CAVITY COUNT DOES NOT FIT THE SHEET.
"A CNC'd polycarbonate mould of 21 or 24 cavities." A standard mould sheet is 275 x 175 mm. Ø55 mm at a realistic 58-62 mm pitch gives 8 to 12 cavities per sheet — watched print: 4x3=12 at 58 mm pitch, 4x2=8 at 60 mm. So 24 cavities is TWO OR THREE MOULDS, and the trade estimate triples to roughly US$1.2-2.7k before the aluminium master. Not fatal, but it is the difference between an impulse decision and a purchase order.

ATTACK 7 — THE MASS FIGURE IS WRONG AND IT IS PUBLISHED AS A SPEC, NOT AS AN ESTIMATE.
"Ø55 mm, ~4 mm thick, ~65 g." Watched print: a Ø55 x 4 mm disc is 9.50 cm3; at bronze 8.8 g/cm3 that is 83.6 g, and 65 g implies 3.11 mm. The concept correctly hedges its COST figures as trade estimates but states the mass flat. In this project that is the exact defect class the rules exist to catch.

ATTACK 8 — THE FIRST STEP DEPENDS ON HARDWARE NOBODY HAS ESTABLISHED EXISTS.
"3D-print the Ø55 mm relief at 0.05 mm layers overnight — a grey plastic medal in his hand inside twenty-four hours" and "cut the SAME height field at Ø300 mm in MDF or plaster on a desktop router... a US$40 blank plus an afternoon." Both assume a printer and a CNC router in the room. Nothing in the repo establishes either, and the same assumption runs through other concepts in the bench, so it has never been tested. If he does not own them: the print is a bureau job at US$15-40 with 3-10 days including shipping, and the Ø300 relief is a job shop at US$150-400 with a week, or a US$2-4k machine plus CAM software (VCarve/Carveco, US$360-700) and a learning curve. The 24-hour pitch is the concept's whole persuasive move and it rests on an unverified premise. Compare: a lotería card reaches his hand from any inkjet in an hour.

ATTACK 9 — THE MEDAL HALF IS A CAPITAL COMMITMENT MISFILED AS A CONCEPT, AGAINST AN OWNER WHO SAID "WE NEED TO FINALLY PRESENT SOMETHING."
US$2.5-5k tooling plus an MOQ of 200-500 at US$18-35 landed is US$6.1k-22.5k of committed spend and dead inventory in an object with zero demonstrated demand, on a 4-8 month lead. The concept's own bench document already indicts this: its top critique names "CNC brass and struck bronze" among "the six most expensive processes in merchandise" and says almost nothing sits in the US$4-25 impulse band. EL TROQUEL is cited there by name. The concept knows this — it says "year-two object" and "do not touch metal in week one" — which is real discipline and I credit it. But a concept whose headline artefact is 8 months and five figures out is answering a different question than the one the owner asked this revision.

WHAT SURVIVES, STATED FAIRLY:
* The camera claim is CORRECT and I verified it. Watched print: side ortho 5.90 gives a 5.900 x 4.056 m frame with its top edge at z=3.548 against bbox top 3.132 — FITS. Front and rear at ortho 3.55 give 2.4406 m of height, top edge z=2.740 — CLIPS. "One face, and the reason is stated" is an honest machine constraint honestly declared, and it is the only concept move here that improved my opinion of the author.
* 1:81.3 checks (4065/50.0).
* The infrastructure argument is sound and now CHEAPER than the concept knew: line_pass.py shipped at rev 77 and design_out/ already holds a rendered lotería card and an SVG. The Z pass is a sibling of work that now exists rather than a cold start.
* The reduction-cut route is coherent: the Ø300 mm plaster showpiece IS the pantograph pattern a reduction hub is cut from. That is a real, traditional, correctly-sequenced chain, and it means the expensive showpiece is not a throwaway.
* Declaring the VW roundel composed out at 3.4 mm is the right call, made rather than dodged.
* The mesh-export gap is correctly declared — and for a 3-axis relief cut it does not matter, because a 16-bit greyscale heightmap goes straight into Vectric/Carveco/Fusion. The concept half-says this; it is more in its favour than it claims.
* WOULD FIX IT: 1. RUN THE RELIEF BUDGET BEFORE ANYTHING ELSE, AND MAKE IT A PROBE. Write probe_rev78_relief.py that renders the side Z pass, masks the background, and prints the histogram of depth-per-feature against a declared tool radius and a declared minimum legible step. If the three apertures and the tail board are the only things above 50 micron under linear compression, the probe says so in its own summary line and the concept is re-scoped on a measurement instead of a hope. Half a day, no fabrication, and it is the single cheapest thing that can kill or save this.

2. REPLACE LINEAR COMPRESSION WITH GRADIENT-DOMAIN RELIEF COMPRESSION. Weyrich et al. 2007. Take the gradient of the height field, attenuate large gradients logarithmically, integrate back with a Poisson solve. scipy is already installed by bootstrap.sh, so this is scipy.sparse.linalg on a ~2000x1400 grid — 1-2 days to write, several to tune. This is what makes the wheel arches, the swage and the shut lines survive at all. Budget it as a named work item, not as "an afternoon".

3. GET THE PAINT BACK BY COMPOSITING THE LINE PASS, NOT BY SCALING UP. This is the fix that turns the object from a blob into the bus, and the tooling now exists. line_pass.py emits camera-normalised polylines from the side view. Rasterise them into a SECOND, SHALLOW height layer — a V-groove engraving of the Señor Tacombi script, the mural outline and the menu strip at a declared 0.10-0.15 mm — and add it to the compressed form layer. Form from Z, line from Line Art: that is literally the owner's own locked style ruling ("vector line and flat colour, shading and occlusion sampled from the 3D asset") expressed in metal. It also gives the concept a defensible answer to "what does the model uniquely contribute" that does not collapse when the paint turns out to be flat.

4. ADD A DRAFT PASS AND STATE THE ANGLE ON THE ARTEFACT. A slope-limiting filter over the height field enforcing a minimum wall angle (start at 7 degrees; sweep it). Print the chosen angle in the summary line as the pose choice it is. Without this the first chocolate tablet does not leave the mould intact and the first die does not fill.

5. RESEQUENCE THE FABRICATION SO WEEK ONE NEEDS NO MACHINE HE MAY NOT OWN. Before quoting any lead time, ASK ONE QUESTION: does he have access to a 3D printer and a CNC router, yes or no. If no, the honest first step is a bureau print (US$15-40, 3-10 days) and the pitch is "a fortnight", not "twenty-four hours". Do not build the pitch on hardware that has never been confirmed.

6. FIX THE CHOCOLATE CHAIN AND THE COUNT. CNC an aluminium positive master, polish it, thermoform polycarbonate over it. Re-quote for 8-12 cavities per sheet and say how many sheets. Get ONE real quote before any of these numbers is repeated — which the concept already says and should now actually do.

7. CORRECT THE MASS TO ~84 g at Ø55 x 4 mm in bronze, or drop the thickness to 3.1 mm and say so.

8. SPLIT THE DELIVERABLE AND KEEP THE MEDAL AS THE STATED DESTINATION, NOT AS THE SHIP. Rev 78 ships the relief master, the Ø300 plaster or MDF roundel, and a printed Ø55 mm proof. That is presentable, costs low hundreds, and is the reduction pattern anyway. The chocolate is a 2-3 month path once a mould supplier is engaged. The medal stays in the document as the object the die exists for — because deleting it deletes the concept's nerve — but with its real number attached: US$6.1k-22.5k committed, 4-8 months, MOQ 200-500. Put THAT in front of him as the multiple choice, with the plaster roundel in his hands. He can say no to a medal; he cannot say no to a number he was never shown.

9. STAMP THE HONEST CONSTANT, WHICH THE CONCEPT ALREADY GOT RIGHT. PROFUNDIDAD EXAGERADA · CONSTANTE DECLARADA and ALTURA: SIN MEDIR are the best things in this proposal. Keep them verbatim. But add the compression ratio you actually shipped, because after fix 2 the relief is no longer a single scale factor and a medal that confesses its method should confess that too.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**ORIGINALITY — 1.5/4 — ORIGINALITY: WEAK. The obverse is a 1997 published technique making a Franklin-Mint object; the reverse is the programme's own signature move for the third time, including one verbatim collision with an existing bench title. What is genuinely new here is small, real, and buried: one master, two lifespans. Everything built around it is precedented in three separate industries. It is NOT simply the flat-measured-line aesthetic relabelled — the relief is authentically three-dimensional and the model dependency is real — but strip the relief off and the content that remains is THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE wrapped around a disc.**

* ATTACKS: 1. "A DEPTH PASS IS A BAS-RELIEF, THERE IS NO TRANSLATION STEP" IS A 28-YEAR-OLD PAPER, AND THE PAPER SAYS THE OPPOSITE. Cignoni/Montani/Scopigno, "Computer-Assisted Generation of Bas- and High-Reliefs" (J. Graphics Tools, 1997) founded this; Weyrich et al., "Digital Bas-Relief from 3D Scenes" (SIGGRAPH 2007) is the canonical modern treatment, with a standing subfield after it (Kerber, Sun, Bian & Hu, Ji). The literature exists PRECISELY BECAUSE naive linear compression of a Z buffer produces a bad relief — crushing ~2 m into ~1.2 mm destroys the gradients that read as form, which is why every paper in the field replaces range compression with gradient-domain compression. So the concept's stated intellectual core is (a) not a discovery and (b) the specific claim the field was founded to refute. Commercially it is a menu item: ArtCAM/Delcam, Vectric Aspire, JewelCAD, Deskproto and ZBrush's bas-relief export all ship height-field-from-3D to small routing shops daily, and mints (Royal Mint, Casa de Moneda) have run scan-to-digital-relief-to-CNC-master as standard workflow since the late 1990s. "Render the side ortho, invert, cut" is what a sign shop does on a Tuesday.

2. THE OBJECT IS FRANKLIN MINT, AND THAT IS THE REAL SOURCE OF THE TONE RISK. A struck bronze medal of a vehicle in bas-relief with dry specification data on the reverse is not an unusual object — it is a saturated 1970s-80s mass-market category (Franklin Mint's automotive medal series, Danbury Mint), sitting under a living fine-art discipline (FIDEM, biennial since 1937, vehicles and machines routinely). VW specifically is one of the most medallised objects on earth: officially licensed T1 collector coins from the New Zealand Mint / Niue exist in bus-shaped and coloured form, plus German anniversary tokens, belt buckles, keyrings. Add the corporate-anniversary medallion and the US challenge-coin tradition and the form arrives pre-loaded with its genre. The stated risk — "a taqueria issuing one is a joke that only works told absolutely straight" — misdiagnoses this as a register problem the client might not share. It is a prior-art problem: the reason it can read as insufferable is that the nearest neighbours in the world are kitsch and corporate self-congratulation, and craft cannot outrun a genre.

3. THE CHOCOLATE IS A CHOCOLATE COIN, AND THE SUPPLY CHAIN IS A COMMODITY LISTING. A Ø55 mm relief disc of tempered chocolate is gelt — arguably the most precedented confection in existence. "CNC-cut polycarbonate mould from your supplied 3D file" is an off-the-shelf trade service (Tomric, Chocolate World, Pavoni advertise exactly this), and "branded chocolate handed over with the check" is default restaurant practice from hotel turndown service down. Heightmap-to-CNC-to-chocolate is also a decade-old maker recipe. Nothing in Object B is unavailable to anyone with a logo and $600, which undercuts the claim that this is the strongest model dependency in the set: the mould shop does not care that the height field came from a measured mesh rather than a Blender freebie.

4. THE REVERSE COLLIDES WITH THE PROGRAMME VERBATIM. `DESIGN_PROGRAM_rev76.md` line 168 already carries the bench concept `ENGINE: NONE. TRANSMISSION: SOLD.` with a stated design instruction to invert the hierarchy so it is the largest element. The medal's reverse reads `MOTOR: NINGUNO · TRANSMISIÓN: VENDIDA` — same fact, same pairing, same joke, translated. That is not a companion, it is the same concept on a different substrate. Compounding: "a real measured dimension on every face" is LA LOTERÍA's core move; `ALTURA: SIN MEDIR` with the tolerance confessed is THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE's entire thesis; "everything the record could not read" is NO LEGIBLE. The concept's own pitch says the reverse is "where this stops being decoration" — and the reverse is the third telling of one joke this programme already tells twice on the shortlist.

5. FLAT-MEASURED-LINE CHARGE: ACQUITTED ON FORM, CONVICTED ON CONTENT. Grant the defence fully — a relief is not a line drawing, the object is genuinely solid, and no other concept in the 78 uses the mesh as a height field. That is real. But the register is declared "beautiful-not-rigorous," and the artefact as specified is roughly 60% rigour-signalling: five dimension figures, a declared-constant stamp (`PROFUNDIDAD EXAGERADA · CONSTANTE DECLARADA`), an edge of measurements, a footnoted scale ratio 1:81.3. Strip the bas-relief and what remains is measured text in a factory register set around a rule. The concept has changed the substrate from paper to metal and kept the voice unchanged.

6. IT WALKED PAST THE ONE TRADITION THAT WOULD HAVE MADE IT ORIGINAL AND SAVED THE TONE. A carved die that makes an edible relief is a 500-year-old folk form with deep non-European lineage: Dutch/German speculaasplank and springerle boards, Japanese rakugan moulds, Chinese mooncake moulds. In MEXICO specifically it is alfeñique and sugar-skull moulds, pan-de-muerto stamps, and the stamped-tin milagro votive — and the Casa de Moneda de México (1535) is the oldest mint in the Americas. Every one of those is on-register for a taqueria and none of them reads as pompous. The concept instead reaches for Belgian polycarbonate and the European art-medal, i.e. the two lineages guaranteed to make a food business look like it is commemorating itself. The programme already knows this seam — NUNCA VOLVIÓ A CAMINAR (the ex-voto) is on the bench — and this concept did not touch it.

7. A MEDAL CANNOT CARRY A CEILING, AND THIS ONE STRIKES A GRADE-U CLAIM INTO PERMANENT BRONZE. The biography on the reverse (1963, bought green in Mexico City, $3,000, three windows missing, opened 6·II·2006, engine scrapped, transmission sold) is real and in the record — SPEC_rev41 §0.1, graded **S**, from Tacombi's own account and Dario Wolos. But SPEC_rev41 §7 is a wrong-bus warning, and its last line is: "Whether it is physically the same vehicle is **U**." So the concept strikes one bus's biography onto a relief of a vehicle whose identity with it the record grades UNKNOWN — in the one material that cannot print a footnote, on the artefact whose proudest move is printing footnotes. The irony is total and it is the concept's own standard failing on its own object.

8. WHAT SURVIVES, STATED FAIRLY, BECAUSE IT IS THE ONLY THING WORTH REBUILDING AROUND: one tool, two lifespans — the permanent and the eaten off a single master. I cannot name a restaurant, brand or artist doing that pairing, and it is a genuinely uncommon structural idea rather than a decorative one. The edge inscription that admits an unmeasured dimension is a good small move (edge lettering historically carries mottos and mint marks, never an admission of ignorance). The plaster-first / palm-second production order is smart but is a routing-shop staple. That is the whole fresh inventory: one structure and one sentence.
* WOULD FIX IT: 1. SHIP THE DIE, NOT WHAT THE DIE MAKES. The concept is named EL TROQUEL — "the die" — and then delivers the two most precedented objects a die can produce. The negative is the object nobody has: the bus sunk into hardened steel or carved into end-grain, hung on the wall, with the two positives beneath it. A tool is not self-congratulation; a medal of yourself is. This single inversion removes the Franklin-Mint adjacency AND the pomposity risk AND makes the title mean something.

2. RE-LINEAGE FROM THE MINT TO THE DULCERÍA. Cut the mould in the alfeñique / sugar-skull / pan-de-muerto register — carved wood or tin-glazed earthenware, not Belgian polycarbonate — and make the perishable object sugar paste or bread rather than tempered couverture. "A Mexican food business carves a mould" requires no defence and has a 500-year ancestry; "a taqueria strikes a bronze medal" requires a paragraph of defence and has Franklin Mint as its nearest neighbour. Name NUNCA VOLVIÓ A CAMINAR (the ex-voto) as the sibling in the same folk-devotional seam rather than pretending the seam is empty.

3. CONVERT THE FALSE PREMISE INTO THE PROJECT'S FIRST REAL FINDING. Do not ship "there is no translation step." Enable `use_pass_z` on the side ortho, render 32-bit EXR, and build BOTH height fields: naive linear range compression, and gradient-domain compression per Weyrich et al. 2007. Cut both at Ø300 in MDF and LOOK AT THEM (rule 1), then publish the pair. The literature predicts the naive cut loses the swage, the shut lines and the aperture edges. Watching that fail is rule 3 satisfied, it is a genuine measurement, and it turns the concept's weakest claim into its strongest evidence — including a printable figure for how much depth compression the vehicle's form survives.

4. MAKE THE TWO OBJECTS NON-IDENTICAL, WHICH IS THE ONLY UNPRECEDENTED TERRITORY HERE. Right now the chocolate is the medal in brown, which means the pairing's novelty is structural only and invisible in the hand. Design for the MELT: the three serving apertures are the deepest cut, so they are the last form to survive or the first to fill. Specify which, then film it. "The bus dissolves roof-first and the three holes are the last thing to go" is an image no prior art in any of the three industries covers, and it is the perishable half finally doing something the permanent half cannot.

5. EVACUATE THE REVERSE AND GIVE IT BACK. `MOTOR: NINGUNO` returns to ENGINE: NONE; `ALTURA: SIN MEDIR` returns to THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE; the dimension list returns to LA LOTERÍA. Put on the reverse the one thing only a relief can say and no flat artefact can: the depth scale itself — the horizontal 1:81.3 beside a DIFFERENT vertical ratio, i.e. the exaggeration, stated as a second number. That is content the substrate earns rather than content imported from paper. Or leave the reverse blank and let the edge carry everything; a blank reverse on an object this loaded is a stronger move than five more figures.

6. STRIKE ONLY WHAT THE RECORD WILL BEAR, OR STRIKE IT INTERROGATIVELY. SPEC_rev41 §7 grades the vehicle identity **U**. Either resolve it before any metal is cut, or remove the biography, or set it in the record's own voice — the medal that says `¿LA MISMA COMBI? · SIN RESOLVER` is a better and far more original object than the one that asserts a provenance it cannot prove.

7. NUMBER THE EDITION 115, AGAINST THE FESTOON LAMPS, NOT AGAINST A STRIKING HOUSE'S MOQ. A run size that is itself a measurement off the vehicle is the kind of detail that separates this from every corporate anniversary medallion ever pressed — and it is free.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**OWNER-AND-BRAND — 2/4 — OWNER-AND-BRAND-FIT AUDIT of EL TROQUEL. The concept survives the checks that usually kill this class of idea, and then fails on the one it did not run: WHOSE FAILURE IS THE PUNCHLINE.

WHAT I VERIFIED AND IT HOLDS — say this first, because it is the strongest thing here. Every line of the reverse inscription is GROUNDED at SPEC's highest grade. `SPEC.md` §0.1: *"Founder Dario Wolos: the bus was **green** when bought, cost **$3,000**, was **'missing three of its windows'** … Opened 6 February 2006"* and Tacombi's own account *"The engine was scrapped, transmission sold"* — all graded **S**, all from the client's own published words. COMPRADA VERDE · $3,000 · LE FALTABAN TRES VENTANAS · ABIERTA 6·II·2006 · MOTOR: NINGUNO · TRANSMISIÓN: VENDIDA are not invented lore. `ALTURA: SIN MEDIR` matches `STATE.md` verbatim. I expected fabricated provenance struck into bronze and did not find it. Credit that; most concepts in this bench would have failed it.
The SPEC 10.10 defence also holds. 10.10's object is the MODEL's painted elements ("must be REPRODUCED from photographs of the actual combi"); a derivative merch object is a different object, and rule 34 is exactly the rule that says so. The concept makes that argument explicitly rather than assuming it, which is more than the shortlist does.
And the side-camera claim is right: `studio.py` `"side": dict(loc=(0.0, 26.0, 1.52) …)` looks down **+Y**, and `STATE.md` reads *"open serving apertures on +Y: 3"* / *"show flank 0.0 mm"* — so the obverse is the SHOW flank, the photographed one, not the grade-E side. Its one-face limitation is honestly reported.

NOW THE ATTACKS.

**1. THE REVERSE IS A CONFESSION ABOUT US, HANDED TO HIS GUEST.** This is the killer and the concept does not name it. `ALTURA: SIN MEDIR` is not a fact about the bus — it is a fact about the survey. It means *the people he hired never measured the roof.* Same for `PROFUNDIDAD EXAGERADA · CONSTANTE DECLARADA`. The medal's most quotable line is an admission of our incomplete work, edge-lettered into a permanent object his customer takes home. NO LEGIBLE and THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE carry the same self-referential DNA and get away with it because they are STUDIO artefacts in a design register; this one is handed across a counter with a check. A restaurateur reads that edge and asks why he is paying to publish his contractor's gap. The concept's stated risk (1) is about a medal being *pompous*; the real risk is that it is *ours, not his*.

**2. THE TRADEMARK PARAGRAPH ANSWERS AN OBJECT THAT IS NOT IN THE FRAME.** The figure is arithmetically right — `t1_core.py` locks `ROUNDEL_D = 0.280`, and 280/81.3 = 3.44 mm — but the nose roundel faces FORWARD and does not appear in a side elevation at all. It was excluded by the camera, not "composed out on purpose"; the design gets no credit for a decision the projection already made. What IS in the relief, four times, is the hubcap badge: `CAP_EMBLEM_D = 0.3170 * CAP_D` = 86.9 mm → **1.07 mm of VW roundel per wheel**, unmentioned. And the real exposure was never the badge: it is the T1 **trade dress** — the whole obverse — on a **struck collectible medal**, which is precisely the format VW licenses and polices. This project's own record is already trademark-aware (F168 keeps `vw_canonical_2019.svg` deliberately un-named `ref_*` as *"a DIFFERENT OBJECT"*). Worse, a die is a TOOL: a poster can be revised after a letter, US$2.5–5k of hardened steel cannot. Risk analysis on a permanent tool must be stronger than on a print, and here it is weaker.

**3. THE MEDAL DELETES EVERY TACOMBI MARK AND KEEPS ONLY THE VOLKSWAGEN ONES.** The concept concedes the artwork survives "as FORM, not paint" — the mural, the paisley, the `Señor Tacombi` script at 15.7 mm, the menu yellow (172,144,17), the burst, all gone. What remains legible at Ø55 mm is a T1 profile and four VW hubcaps. To a guest holding it, that is a Volkswagen bus with a counter. It simultaneously **maximises the trademark exposure and minimises his own mark** — the exact inversion of a brand object. The only Tacombi-specific signal on the entire artefact is the reverse text, which is attack 1.

**4. IT STRIKES A KNOWN-WRONG NUMBER INTO METAL, AND NOT THE ONE IT DISCLOSED.** Good on `BAY_DEPTH = 0.42`. But the propped tail board is the most sculptural silhouette element on a side relief, and it is **built at 38.0° against a photographed 28.0°, unresolved for seven revisions**. The rear hatch is `REAR_OPEN_DEG = 64.0`, logged in source as *"angle NOT MEASURED -- no frame shows it"*. THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE's answer to that disagreement is *draw both*. A relief cannot draw both; it picks one and hardens it. His standard, verbatim, is *"any single measurement off is unacceptable — per-measurement, not on average."* A medal is the least forgiving possible carrier for that standard, and raking light — how a medal is actually read — is the lighting that most exposes form error, not least. The claim elsewhere in the programme that line/relief "hides most of" the photoreal defects is true of PAINT and false of FORM.

**5. AGAINST *"we need to finally present something."*** MOQ 200–500, tooling US$2.5–5k, "a year-two object", two supply chains — after 76 revisions and *"Why do we keep doing Revs that don't ship geometry."* The concept's own mitigation is real and is its best feature: a Ø300 mm plaster/MDF roundel for a US$40 blank and a Ø55 mm print overnight. But BOTH depend on infrastructure that does not exist — I confirmed `studio.py` sets no `use_pass_z` and there is no mesh export path — and "an afternoon" is a figure nobody watched print, in a project whose cardinal rule is rule 5. It also assumes a printer and a desktop router this repo has no evidence of; every output in 76 revisions has been pixels. That is a new supply chain, not an afternoon.

**6. IT DOES NOT DECLARE ITS LINE, AND THAT IS NOW MANDATORY.** F331 is `RULED-rev77`: *"Split it deliberately — kids get one line, adults another"* — free/handed-to-you for children, paid/kept for adults — and *"Every future shortlist must say which line each item is in."* EL TROQUEL straddles: the chocolate is a handed-to-you children's object, the medal a paid adult one, and the concept proposes putting **the same three lines on the chocolate wrapper**, which drags the adult confession into the children's line. That is the one place the split is actively violated.

**7. "ONE die, two objects" IS FALSE AS WRITTEN**, by its own body: tool steel for striking AND food-grade polycarbonate for moulding is TWO tools. The shared asset is the height field, not the die. Minor, but the title asserts the unity the text withdraws, in a project where a claim in prose is not a measurement.

WOULD HE BE PROUD TO HAND SOMEONE THIS? The chocolate — unreservedly yes: food, warm, cheap, shareable, on-brand, works for a child and an adult, and it uses the solid-model fact nothing else in the 78 uses. The plaster roundel on his wall — yes, and it is the best 24-hour pitch object in the shortlist. The bronze medal with THAT reverse — no. He would read `ALTURA: SIN MEDIR` and hear an excuse.**

* ATTACKS: (1) The reverse's payload is a confession about the PROJECT, not a fact about the bus — `ALTURA: SIN MEDIR` tells his paying guest that his contractor never measured the roof; the concept's stated tone risk is about pomposity and misses this entirely. (2) The trademark paragraph clears the nose roundel (correct at 3.44 mm from `ROUNDEL_D = 0.280`) but the nose roundel is not in a side elevation at all — it was excluded by the camera, not composed out; the four hubcap badges that ARE in relief (`CAP_EMBLEM_D = 0.3170 * CAP_D` = 86.9 mm → 1.07 mm each) go unmentioned, and the actual exposure is T1 trade dress on a struck collectible, the one format VW licenses — on a PERMANENT tool that a cease-and-desist cannot revise. (3) At Ø55 mm the relief deletes every Tacombi mark (mural, paisley, 15.7 mm script, menu yellow) and keeps only the Volkswagen-shaped ones: it maximises his trademark risk and minimises his own brand simultaneously. (4) It strikes the tail board — the most sculptural element on the face — at the built 38.0° against the photographed 28.0°, unresolved seven revisions, plus `REAR_OPEN_DEG = 64.0` logged as NOT MEASURED; against *"any single measurement off is unacceptable"*, and raking light exposes form error rather than hiding it. (5) MOQ 200–500, US$2.5–5k tooling, "year-two", two supply chains, against *"we need to finally present something"*; and the 24-hour mitigation rests on infrastructure I confirmed absent (`studio.py` sets no `use_pass_z`, no export path) plus a printer/router this repo has never had. (6) F331 (`RULED-rev77`) requires every item to declare its line; this one straddles and then puts the adult confession on the children's chocolate wrapper. (7) "ONE die, two objects" is contradicted by its own body — tool steel plus polycarbonate is two tools; the shared asset is the height field. — WHAT SURVIVED MY ATTACK, stated because it is unusual: every inscription fact checks out at SPEC grade S (§0.1: green, $3,000, three windows missing, 6 Feb 2006, engine scrapped, transmission sold); the SPEC 10.10 rule-34 defence is sound; and the obverse really is the photographed show flank (`studio.py` side cam at y = +26.0, `STATE.md` "apertures on +Y: 3").
* WOULD FIX IT: SPLIT THE PAIR AND SHIP THE FOOD. They share one asset — the height field — and nothing else; stop pretending they are one object.

1. **THE CHOCOLATE SHIPS ALONE, AND ITS WRAPPER IS ABOUT THE BUS.** Ø55 mm tablet, polycarbonate mould, given with the check. Wrapper carries the S-grade STORY, not the survey's gaps: `COMPRADA VERDE · MÉXICO, D.F. · 2006 · $3,000 · LE FALTABAN TRES VENTANAS`. Every one of those is the client's own published sentence and flatters him. Delete `ALTURA: SIN MEDIR` and `PROFUNDIDAD EXAGERADA` from anything a customer touches. Declare it: **children's line, F331**.

2. **MOVE THE CONFESSION WHERE IT BELONGS.** `ALTURA: SIN MEDIR` is a good line in the wrong mouth. Give it to THE UNCERTAINTY PLATE, which exists to print exactly that and is read by adults in a design register, and let the medal — if it ever gets cut — carry only facts about the vehicle.

3. **RE-CUT THE MEDAL'S RISK, DON'T RE-WORD IT.** Get a written opinion on T1 trade dress on a struck collectible BEFORE any steel, and put the four hubcap badges in the question, not the nose roundel. If the answer is no, the height field still makes the chocolate, the plaster and the plate — nothing is lost. Say explicitly in the pitch that a die cannot be revised after a letter, which a poster can.

4. **DO NOT STRIKE A CONTESTED ANGLE.** Either resolve the tail board's 38.0/28.0 first (it is a live open item, not a design constraint), or pose the board SHUT in the relief and remove the disagreement from the object entirely. A closed tail board is also a stronger silhouette at Ø55 mm.

5. **REBUILD THE FIRST STEP SO IT COSTS NOTHING TO BE WRONG.** Week one is: add `use_pass_z` to the existing side ortho, render 32-bit EXR, and post a greyscale HEIGHT MAP and a screen-space relief preview — pixels, no fabrication, no printer, no router, half a day and verifiable. Only after he has seen and liked that image does anyone quote a mould. Delete "an afternoon", "overnight at 0.05 mm" and the US$40 blank until a real quote and a real machine exist — they are figures nobody watched print, and the ONE thing this owner has been trained to distrust is exactly that.

6. **RENAME IT.** `EL TROQUEL` names a die that turns out to be two tools. `EL RELIEVE` (or LA MATRIZ) names the thing that is actually singular — the height field — and stops the title asserting a unity the body withdraws.

7. **PITCH IT IN THIS ORDER:** the plaster roundel on his wall, then the chocolate in his hand, then — as an option, priced, with the trademark opinion attached — the medal. Presented that way it is two on-brand objects plus an idea; presented as written it is one expensive object he can only accept or reject.
* **DANGER INTACT: True**

**DANGER — 2/4 — The raw material here is genuinely dangerous and the write-up has spent most of its energy making that danger respectable. What is at stake in EL TROQUEL is not "tone" — it is that this is a FUNERARY object. Read the reverse cold: COMBI · 1963 / bought green / three windows already gone / opened 6·II·2006 / MOTOR: NINGUNO · TRANSMISIÓN: VENDIDA. That is an epitaph. Born, disfigured, opened, engine scrapped, transmission sold. The vehicle in this project is a corpse — SPEC §0.1 in the founder's own voice: engine scrapped, transmission sold, towed very slowly, parked, cut open. It will never move again. A struck medal is what institutions issue for the dead. And the companion object is the same effigy, EATEN, handed over with the check. In Mexico that is not a clever pairing, it is a calavera de azúcar: you write the name of the dead on the sugar skull and the living eat it. This concept is one inch from the most loaded object in the entire 78 and it does not know it. Instead of naming the corpse and the communion, the write-up justifies the chocolate with an aphorism — "a measurement that cannot be recovered from what we hold is well served by an object that melts" — which is a pun standing where the grief should be, and frames the epitaph as "a medal's native content is a date and a fact, and this project has ninety of them." An obituary re-labelled as data. That is the sanding, and it is done by the same instinct that runs through every risk note: each live wire is picked up, insulated with a Spanish-caps stamp or a rule-34 defence, and put back. What survives intact and is genuinely excellent: ALTURA: SIN MEDIR incuse on the edge, in the position real medals reserve for the mint mark and the fineness of the metal. Printing "height: unmeasured" in the place where a mint certifies its own authority is a real transgression against the form, permanent, in bronze, unretractable. Keep that at any cost. But one live wire out of five is not a dangerous concept, it is a well-behaved one with a good line on it.**

* ATTACKS: 1. THE REVERSE IS THE SAFE CHOICE AND IT IS THE WHOLE OBJECT. You have a two-sided artefact about a vehicle with one known side and one side that NO PHOTOGRAPH IN THE REFERENCE SET SHOWS — SPEC grades the off flank E, never photographed. A medal has an obverse and a reverse. The one thing this form is built to do is hold a known face and an unknown face, and the concept fills the unknown face with TEXT. DALE, sitting two entries above it in the same document, already found this move and used it better ("a piñata spins, so the side nobody has ever seen faces the room half the time"). EL TROQUEL had the same opportunity in a harder, more permanent material and took the inscription instead. That is not a limitation of the machine; that is a choice to be legible rather than uncomfortable.

2. "PROFUNDIDAD EXAGERADA · CONSTANTE DECLARADA" IS THE EXACT FAILURE MODE I AM HERE TO CATCH. The risk named is real — BAY_DEPTH = 0.42 is flagged NOT MEASURED in t1_shell.py and it governs the object's most sculptural feature. The response is to stamp a label on it. A label does not carry the discomfort; it converts a defect into a credential, and in Spanish caps on a bronze roundel it reads as MORE institutional gravitas, not less. The concept ends up congratulating itself for the invented number. Nothing is at stake once it is captioned.

3. THE DEPTH RISK IS ALSO PROBABLY MIS-STATED, AND THE TRUE ANSWER IS BETTER AND UGLIER. A Z pass from the side ortho does not stop at BAY_DEPTH — it looks straight through the three serving apertures into the galley, the counter, the backdrop. In a relief compressing ~2 m into ~1.2 mm the three holes will render as near-black pits with tiny cooking equipment in them. You can see the kitchen through the medal. That is the strongest content the object has and the write-up never noticed it, because it was busy defending a constant.

4. TWO EXCULPATIONS THAT ARE TELLS. (a) "No trademark exposure: the VW roundel is 3.4 mm at this scale and illegible, and it is composed out on purpose." From a pure side orthographic view the roundel is on the NOSE and is not in frame at all. The geometry excluded it; nobody composed anything. Manufacturing a decision out of a machine fact is precisely what risk (3) accuses itself of and then repeats one paragraph later. (b) The F92 note and the rule-34 defence are internal audit paperwork appearing inside a concept pitch. Their presence is the document's own anxiety, and their volume is inversely proportional to how much the concept is willing to risk.

5. THE CULTURAL QUESTION IS THE ONE UNFLAGGED RISK IN A DOCUMENT THAT FLAGS EVERYTHING. This concept lawyers SPEC 10.10, F92, rule 34 and trademark exposure meticulously, and never once asks whether a New York hospitality brand handing out an edible effigy of a dead vehicle with its death notice on the wrapper is beautiful or is Day-of-the-Dead cosplay sold to gringos. That is the actual brand-risk question and it is the only one absent. Compare SIGUE LA TIRA in the same document, which does put the appropriation question honestly and says "if the budget only stretches to the imitation, do not make this."

6. THE TONE RISK IS NAMED BUT NOT CARRIED. "Insufferable self-regard, and there is no middle" is an honest sentence in a risk section. Meanwhile every design decision in the artefact — bronze, struck, incuse, edge-lettered, Spanish caps, 65 g — is optimised for maximum institutional dignity. The concept banked the disclaimer and then built the safe object anyway. Naming a risk in prose is not carrying it; it is the concept-pitch version of "a claim in prose is not a measurement."

7. THE FAILURE IMAGE IS NOT DRAWN. MOQ 200–500 at US$18–35 landed. The risk is not the tooling spend, it is a carton of 380 unsold bronze medals of your own van in a storeroom in year three. That image is the honest cost and it is nowhere in the cost section.
* WOULD FIX IT: Six changes, all subtractive or harder-edged, none of them craft polish.

1. GIVE THE REVERSE TO THE SIDE NOBODY HAS SEEN. Strike the off flank on the reverse — grade E, never photographed — cut from the expert inference (twin cargo doors, three windows), and mark it, incuse, LADO IZQUIERDO · NUNCA FOTOGRAFIADO. Or, harder and better: leave the reverse a blank raised field, unstruck, tooling-marked, with that one line and nothing else. A medal with a blank face is genuinely uncomfortable to hold and impossible to explain away. Move the epitaph to a spiral on the rim or onto the chocolate wrapper only.

2. DELETE "PROFUNDIDAD EXAGERADA · CONSTANTE DECLARADA" AND MAKE THE EXAGGERATION VISIBLE INSTEAD. Push the Z compression until the three apertures read as caves and the vehicle reads as obviously sculpted, not surveyed. An object that could never be mistaken for a survey needs no caption saying it is not one. Trade the label for the consequence.

3. LET THE Z PASS THROUGH THE HOLES AND DO NOT CLEAN IT UP. Three deep pits with the galley legible at 0.3 mm — counter, warmer, racks. No fill, no flattening, no minimum-depth clamp. The kitchen inside the medal is the object's real content and it costs nothing but the decision not to tidy it.

4. SAY THE WORD FUNERARY, AND SAY CALAVERA DE AZÚCAR, AND PUT BOTH TO HIM AS THE QUESTION. Not as a risk note — as the concept's actual premise: this is a memorial to a vehicle that was gutted to make a business, and the chocolate is what the funeral hands out. If he says yes, then the chocolate ships on a date that means something — 6 February, the opening, or 2 November — wrapped in the death notice, and it is openly an effigy that is eaten. If he says no, kill the chocolate and keep the medal; do not keep a half-version that is merely a nice thing with a bus on it.
